# Unreleased

- The free-LLM dry run tries the next free model when one is rate-limited, and says which model and why (owner's OK, 2026-11-13: "yes fix the dry run") (**SEO: none; no page, markup, sitemap or robots change; no Prisma change; no deploy or migration**). **Found:** the second dry run (after #119) stopped at its first request with "OpenRouter free-model rate or daily limit reached (429)" and printed neither the model nor OpenRouter's message, so a limit on one free model could not be told from the account's daily cap. **What:** `chatOpenRouter` now puts the model id and the first 300 characters of OpenRouter's answer in the 429 error; `rankFreeOpenRouterModels` (replaces `pickFreeOpenRouterModel`) returns all free text models, preferred ones first; the dry run lists up to 4 of them at the top of its report, moves to the next on a 429 and stops only when every one has refused. The free-only guard is unchanged (`:free` id and price 0 on the list, re-checked before every call; 402 still stops the run). **Not verified:** whether a 429 here is per model or per account; the next run's printed message will show. Tests: `ai/freeLlm.test.ts`.

- The free-LLM dry run picks its OpenRouter model from today's free list (owner's request, 2026-11-13, after the first dry run failed) (**SEO: none; no page, markup, sitemap or robots change; no Prisma change; no deploy or migration**). **Found:** the first dry run (0 passed, 3 failed) got `404 This model is unavailable for free` for the guessed default `meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct:free`; the free-only guard did its job and never used the paid slug. OpenRouter's public list (`GET /api/v1/models`, read 2026-11-13) had 13 models with a `:free` id and a price of 0, not including it. **What:** `pickFreeOpenRouterModel` (`lib/ai/freeLlm.ts`) takes models whose id ends in `:free`, whose prompt and completion prices are exactly 0 and whose output is text, skips code and preview models, and prefers `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free`, then `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it:free`, then `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free`, else the first other one by id; the dry run script uses it unless the `OPENROUTER_MODEL` variable is set, and `chatOpenRouter` still re-checks the `:free` suffix. **Not verified:** that the chosen model answers well or within the free limit, that is what the next dry run shows; the order of preference is my guess, not a benchmark. Tests: `ai/freeLlm.test.ts`.

- A dry run of free-LLM job enrichment, with a check that the rewrite invents nothing (owner's OK, 2026-11-13: "yea for dry run") (**SEO: none; no page, markup, sitemap or robots change; no Prisma change; nothing is scheduled, deployed or written to the database**). **What:** `scripts/enrich-jobs-dry-run.ts` (`npm run ai:enrich:dry-run`) reads recent jobs inside a `READ ONLY` transaction, picks the day's sample with `selectJobsToEnrich`, builds the existing `buildAiEnrichPrompt` prompts, and, when a free key is set, asks OpenRouter (a `:free` model) or Cloudflare Workers AI for the rewrite, then prints it with the checks' verdict; without keys it is prompt-only (selection and token sizes); it has no write call. `lib/ai/enrichValidate.ts` flags a rewrite whose numbers (years, pay, counts), tech names or benefits are not in the posting, or that leaves out responsibilities or requirements the posting lists; numbers match as whole numbers, so "10+ years" is not found inside "10,000 customers" (a first version let it pass; its test caught that). `.github/workflows/ai-enrich-dryrun.yml` runs it by hand (sample of 1 to 20) with the production-database fingerprint guard, and reads `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` from repository secrets and the optional `OPENROUTER_MODEL` / `CLOUDFLARE_MODEL` from variables. The default OpenRouter model (`meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct:free`) is a guess at a model that is free today: free models rotate, so set `OPENROUTER_MODEL` if the run reports it missing. **Not verified:** the database query was type-checked but not run against the live database, and the providers' response shapes are from their docs, not a live call. A workflow can only be started from GitHub once it is on `main`. Tests: `ai/enrichValidate.test.ts`, `ai/enrichDryRun.test.ts` (new).

- Groundwork for free-only LLM enrichment: a client that cannot spend money and a rule for which jobs get a rewrite (owner's request, 2026-11-13: use only free tiers, never credits, and rewrite only some jobs, not all) (**SEO: none; no page, markup, sitemap or robots change; no Prisma change; nothing is called or scheduled, so this needs no deploy, no migration and no key**). **Found** reading the providers' docs on 2026-11-13: OpenRouter's free models allow 50 requests a day (1,000 only after buying credits), Cloudflare Workers AI gives 10,000 neurons a day free, Hugging Face gives free accounts no inference credits, and Meta Muse's "1 billion tokens" is an app bonus with no API; so the free tiers cover roughly 50 to 150 jobs a day, not the 27,614 live jobs. **What:** `lib/ai/freeLlm.ts` calls OpenRouter and Cloudflare Workers AI and refuses anything that could cost money: only OpenRouter model ids ending in `:free` (checked before any network call), a 402 "payment required" answer stops the run, a 429 or "daily free allocation" answer ends that provider's day, plus a `DailyCap` request counter and a JSON-object reader for chatty answers; `lib/ai/enrichSelection.ts` picks the day's jobs: live, not yet enriched, posted in the last 3 days, description of at least 800 characters, ranked by pay as a multiple of the currency's six-figure floor, priority employers and freshness, at most 100 a day (`DEFAULT_DAILY_ENRICH_LIMIT`), ties broken by newest then id so a rerun picks the same jobs. The existing `buildAiEnrichPrompt` (short bullets, requirements, benefits, tech stack) is the planned prompt. **Not verified:** written from the providers' public docs without a live key, so the exact response shapes (OpenRouter `choices[0].message.content`, Cloudflare `result.response`, the "daily free allocation" wording) are the first thing to check on a real call. **Not done:** the runner script and workflow, storing results, and any key; keys go in GitHub Actions secrets, never in chat or the repo. Tests: `ai/freeLlm.test.ts`, `ai/enrichSelection.test.ts` (new).

- Groundwork for using freehire's structured fields: company-size mapping and a conservative "worldwide" rule, not wired into ingest (owner's request, 2026-11-13) (**SEO: none; no page, markup, sitemap or robots change; no Prisma change; nothing in the live ingest or sync changes, so this needs no deploy and no migration**). **Found** by reading freehire's API on 2026-11-13: its jobs carry `countries`, `regions` (including `global`), `cities`, `company_size`, `company_type`, a summary and a requirements list, while `mapFreehireJob` keeps only the free-text location and a remote flag; and the AI company enricher writes size ranges (`1001-5000`, `5000+`) that the site's size filter, which matches `Company.sizeBucket` exactly against `1-10`, `11-50`, `51-200`, `201-1000` and `1000+`, can never match. **What:** `lib/companies/sizeBucket.ts` maps any size range (freehire's `201-500`, `501-1000`, `1000+`, the enricher's, "200 employees") to the site's bucket by the top of the range, and `scripts/backfill-company-metadata.ts` now uses it (it wrote the enricher's ranges as they were); `lib/scrapers/freehireStructured.ts` reads `work_mode`, `countries`, `regions` and `location` into a scope (worldwide, countries, regions, unknown), believing `global` only for a remote job with no country and text that names no place, because in a sample of 100 newest `regions=global` USD jobs 18 were not remote and 33 also carried a country. **Not done:** the module is not used by `mapFreehireJob` or ingest yet; storing the result needs new nullable columns and a migration, to be applied with `db-migrate.yml` before any code that reads them is deployed (a column the schema lists but the database lacks breaks every query that reads the table). Tests: `companies/sizeBucket.test.ts`, `scrapers/freehireStructured.test.ts` (new).


- Database audit: four more read-only queries to size freehire coverage and where remote jobs lose their country (owner's request, 2026-11-13, after asking whether "50 to 68 worldwide jobs" was right) (**SEO: none; no page, markup, sitemap or robots change; no Prisma change**). **Found** by reading freehire's API (counts at the pay floors, last 30 days: USD 30,018, EUR 3,849, CAD 1,025, CHF 857, GBP 822, AUD 282, about 36,900 in all; 247 USD jobs it marks `regions=global`) against our 6,781 live freehire jobs, and by the scraper's mapping, which keeps only the free-text `location` and a remote flag and drops freehire's `countries`, `regions`, `cities` and company size. **What:** `scripts/audit-db-coverage.ts` now also reports remote jobs by source with and without a stored country (and how many say just "Remote"), remote jobs whose description says worldwide or anywhere, AI-enrichment and description-length coverage by source, and live freehire jobs by currency. SQL run against an in-memory Postgres built from `schema.prisma` (15 of 15 queries; a write in the read-only transaction was refused).

- Remote jobs whose text lists several countries list all of them; "North America" and "Americas" list their countries; region lists leave out countries an employer cannot hire in (owner's OK, 2026-11-13, "multi-country parsing", on the same branch as the worldwide change) (**SEO: the `applicantLocationRequirements` in `JobPosting` JSON-LD changes for remote jobs with no stored country whose text names two or more countries, or says "North America" or "Americas", and loses Iran from the Middle East and EMEA lists (Cuba and North Korea are in no region list); `jobLocationType`, `jobLocation`, `validThrough`, canonicals, robots, sitemaps and every other job are unchanged; no Prisma change**). **Found** by running the builder on the most common location texts of country-less remote jobs in the 2026-11-13 audit: "Remote, Canada; Remote, United States" (44 jobs), "US, CA, Remote" (42), "United States & Canada" (18) and "Remote (United States | Canada)" (15) gave the United States only; "Remote (US/Canada)" (10) gave Canada only, dropping the US; "North America" (29) and "Americas" (34) gave the US only. About 190 jobs in the top 25 texts alone. **What:** `countriesFromText` (`lib/location/jobLocation.ts`) reads every country a text names, splitting on commas, semicolons, `|`, `/`, `&`, "or" and "and"; a two-letter code (US, CA, GB, UK, AU, NZ, IE, DE, FR, NL) counts only when every part of the text is a country, so the CA in "San Francisco, CA, US" is still California; the builder uses it when it finds two or more, after a stored country (which still wins) and before the single-place reader. "North America" is the US and Canada (`lib/location/regionCountries.ts`; Mexico left out, one line to add) and "Americas" the 35 UN states of the Americas; both are kept out of `REMOTE_REGIONS` so no chip or filter appears. `WORLDWIDE_EXCLUDED_COUNTRIES` is now `EXCLUDED_ELIGIBLE_COUNTRIES` and also filters the Europe, EMEA, APAC, Middle East, North America and Americas lists (Iran was in the Middle East list); a country the posting names itself is never removed. **Not changed:** a bare "Remote" (422 jobs) keeps the company/currency guess; a text such as "North America" or "US, CA, Remote" still also gets a US `jobLocation` (the old rule treats any text not starting with "remote" as a workplace), which sits oddly beside two eligible countries and is left for a separate decision; the text reader knows only the 32 countries with names in `COUNTRY_NAMES`. Tests: `location/countriesFromText.test.ts`, `seo/jobJsonLdMultiCountry.test.ts` (new), `location/worldwideCountries.test.ts`.

- Worldwide remote jobs list every eligible country instead of the company's country; three more audit queries (owner's OK, 2026-11-13, after the first live audit) (**SEO: the `applicantLocationRequirements` in `JobPosting` JSON-LD changes for remote jobs whose location text says worldwide or anywhere (68 by loose wording, about 50 by exact text, of 27,673 live jobs in the 2026-11-13 audit) and have no stored country: from `United States` (or another guess) to 190 `Country` entries, about 8 KB each; every other job, `jobLocationType`, `jobLocation`, `validThrough`, canonicals, robots and sitemaps unchanged; no Prisma change**). **Found:** the audit of 2026-11-13 and a run of the builder: "Remote - Worldwide" with no stored country got the company's or the currency's country, telling Google a worldwide job is open only there ("false location data" in Google's policy), and Google says a 100% remote job must name at least one eligible country, so leaving the property out would not do. Google's page does not mention worldwide jobs; listing the countries is the truthful reading, not a documented one, so watch the job-posting reports in Search Console after the deploy. **What:** `lib/location/worldwideCountries.ts` holds the 193 UN member states with ISO codes (the list on un.org read 2026-11-13) and `WORLDWIDE_EXCLUDED_COUNTRIES` (Cuba, Iran, North Korea: the owner's starting set, to be confirmed with counsel or OFAC's country-list FAQ; Syria and the occupied regions of Ukraine are NOT excluded until decided; one line to change); `lib/seo/jobJsonLd.ts` uses it when `isWorldwideLocationText` (the site's own check: worldwide words and nothing that limits them) says so. A stored country still wins, as on the job page; text such as "Worldwide (US preferred)", "Anywhere in the US" and "Worldwide, EST hours" is not worldwide. **Not changed:** a bare "Remote" (422 jobs) keeps the company/currency guess; "US, CA, Remote"-style multi-country text and "North America"/"Americas" are still to be sized. **Audit (`scripts/audit-db-coverage.ts`):** company completeness now also counts size bucket, tags, LinkedIn, country, ATS provider and funding summary; new queries count country words in the text of country-less remote jobs (US and Canada together, UK, continents) and list remote jobs that have a stored country but say worldwide. The new SQL was run against an in-memory Postgres built from `schema.prisma` (11 of 11 queries, and a write in the read-only transaction was refused). Tests: `location/worldwideCountries.test.ts`, `seo/jobJsonLdWorldwide.test.ts` (new), `auditDbCoverage.test.ts`.

- Remote jobs that name a region list that region's countries; a read-only database audit you can run from GitHub (owner's OK, 2026-11-13) (**SEO: the `applicantLocationRequirements` in `JobPosting` JSON-LD changes for remote jobs whose text names Europe, EMEA, APAC or the Middle East, and for a stored `europe`/`emea`/`apac` region; it widens to the real region instead of the company's or the euro's country (Germany) or a sample of 8; other jobs, `jobLocationType`, `jobLocation`, `validThrough`, canonicals, robots and sitemaps unchanged; no Prisma change**). **Found** by running the builder on sample remote jobs: "Remote - Europe" with euro pay gave Germany only (the guess from the company country or the currency), and a stored `europe` or `emea` region gave 8 countries, so Google was told those jobs are open to far fewer people than they are. **What:** `lib/seo/jobJsonLd.ts` takes the countries of Europe, EMEA, APAC, the Middle East and UK & Ireland from `REMOTE_REGIONS` (the same table the `/remote` pages use; Kosovo's non-ISO `XK` dropped) and reads a region from `locationRaw` when it names exactly one and nothing narrows or widens it ("Eastern Europe", "US or Europe", "Europe & Asia" and "Europe and EMEA" keep the old behaviour); a stored country still wins. **Not changed, decision needed:** jobs that say "worldwide" or "anywhere" still get the company's country, so a worldwide job is marked US-only. Google's job-posting page says a 100% remote job must name at least one eligible country, so omitting the property is not an answer, and it says nothing about worldwide jobs; listing countries needs a chosen list. Also unchanged: remote jobs with no region text ("Remote") keep the company/currency guess. **Database audit:** `.github/workflows/db-audit.yml` (manual run) runs `scripts/audit-db-coverage.ts`: nine SELECT-only queries in one `READ ONLY` transaction behind the production-database fingerprint guard, written to the run summary (live and new jobs, company profile completeness, currencies, how remote jobs name their location, real expiry dates by source). The SQL was run against an in-memory Postgres built from `schema.prisma` with sample rows, and a write inside the read-only transaction was refused; the Prisma wrapper itself could not be run without a database. A workflow can only be started from GitHub once it is on `main`. Tests: `jobJsonLdRemoteRegion.test.ts`, `auditDbCoverage.test.ts` (new).

- Sponsored placements, set by hand, and a real `/post-a-job` page (owner's request, 2026-11-13: "manual placement", sold through `/post-a-job`) (**SEO: `/post-a-job` stops being a redirect to `/jobs` and becomes a `noindex, follow` page with an absolute canonical and no hreflang, linked from the footer and in no sitemap; the sponsored link is `rel="sponsored noopener"`; the card has no JobPosting markup and does not touch the jobs list, `ItemList` JSON-LD, canonicals or pagination; no Prisma change, `SiteConfig` already exists**). **Found:** `/post-a-job` redirected to `/jobs`, so there was nothing to sell from, and the admin Site Config page (`/ubaid93/config`) already edits plain text values by key. **What:** ten new keys there, `sponsor.1.` and `sponsor.2.` each with `name`, `text` (140 characters), `url` (https only), `logo` (https, optional) and `until` (last day shown, YYYY-MM-DD, UTC). `lib/sponsors/slots.ts` shows a slot only while name, https link and last day are all set and the day has not passed, so a forgotten placement ends by itself; the end date is checked on every request, not frozen in the 5-minute cache; links get `utm_source=6figjobs&utm_medium=sponsored&utm_campaign=slot-N` unless the sponsor set their own. `SponsorStrip` renders a "Sponsored" label and the cards (logo on a white tile) above the job list on page 1 of `/jobs` and above "Latest jobs" on the home page, and nothing when no slot is active. `/post-a-job` says what a placement is and is not, and points to `support@6figjobs.com`; **it states no price** (none is in the repo). `__tests__/guards/internalLinks.test.ts` used `/post-a-job` as its example of a redirect-only page; it now checks `app/jobs/usa/page.tsx` by file. Not changed: `/terms` has no advertising wording; worth a look before the first paid slot. Tests: `sponsors/slots.test.ts`, `components/sponsorStrip.test.ts` (new).

- `/companies` tiles show new roles this week and median pay (owner's request, 2026-11-13, batch 2 of the layoffsandhires.com ideas) (**SEO: none to markup; the tiles' visible text grows by one short line; no change to canonicals, robots, the `ItemList`/`CollectionPage` JSON-LD, pagination or URLs; no Prisma change**). **Found:** a tile gave only a name and a job count, though every company's open jobs already carry the posting date and salary range. **What:** `lib/jobs/companyHiringStats.ts` reads the public jobs of the 48 companies on the page (the same `getPublicJobWhere()` filter the counts use, cached 5 minutes) and gives, per company, the jobs posted in the last 7 days (posting date, else the day first seen: the rule behind "new this week" elsewhere) and the median of each job's pay midpoint, in the one currency most of its salaried jobs use (currencies are never mixed; no median under 3 salaried jobs; amounts beyond `getAnnualSalaryCapForCurrency` are dropped as parsing slips). `CompanySearch` shows "4 new this week · median $210k" under the count, only for companies that have a fact to show; `/remote/companies` is unchanged. A failed read leaves the tiles as before. **Chosen over a separate table:** a table would list the same companies twice and replace the tile design. Tests: `companyHiringStats.test.ts` (new), `companySearch.test.ts` (new).

- New `/method` page ("How it works"), a "Job list last refreshed" time on the homepage, and job counts on the homepage filters (owner's request, 2026-11-13, after comparing layoffsandhires.com) (**SEO: one new indexable page, `/method`, with an absolute canonical, `WebPage` and `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD and no hreflang; it is not added to any sitemap (phase 1 submits hub pages only, and `/about` is not listed either); no change to other canonicals, robots, noindex rules or URLs**). **Found:** `/about` explains the rules in two short paragraphs and nothing said how salaries are checked, what is left out, or when the list was last refreshed. **What:** `app/method/page.tsx` lists where jobs come from, the yearly floor for every currency in `HIGH_SALARY_THRESHOLDS` (read from that file, so the page cannot drift), the euro top-of-range rule (`TOP_OF_RANGE_CURRENCIES`), what is excluded (no salary, junior/intern/graduate titles, part-time/contract/temporary, duplicates) and how listings age out (7 days unseen, 45 days maximum); linked from the footer, `/about` and the homepage stats note. The stats strip shows `Job list last refreshed Nov 13, 2026, 08:10 UTC` from the newest `Job.lastSeenAt` among live jobs (`loadLastRefreshedAt`, cached 5 minutes); an absolute time on purpose, since the page is cached and "5 minutes ago" would be wrong when read. The hour uses `hourCycle: 'h23'`: with `hour12: false` the CI runner's ICU printed midnight as `24:00` (caught by `siteStatsStrip.test.ts`). The homepage filter shows live counts beside each country, "Anywhere in the world (remote)" and Remote/Hybrid/On-site, from the existing `loadSiteCounts()` (no new queries); an option with no count is shown without one. **Not changed, to check:** the ingest gate (`salaryPublicationGate.ts`) needs the bottom of the range to reach the floor outside the euro, while the listing filter (`buildHighSalaryEligibilityWhere`) accepts either end, so `/method` states only the floors and the euro rule. Tests: `heroFilters.test.ts` (new) and `siteStatsStrip.test.ts`.

- `/api/health` answers 503 when the database cannot serve jobs (**SEO: none; `/api/` is already disallowed in robots.txt**). **Found** on 2026-11-13: after the move to Openship, a deploy at about 00:43 UTC pointed `DATABASE_URL`/`POSTGRES_PRISMA_URL` at a database the app could not use. For about three hours every page still answered 200: listings showed 0 jobs and job pages rendered the "temporarily unavailable" fallback with `noindex`, so neither the host nor any check noticed. Rolling back the code did not help; pointing both variables back at the Railway database did. **What:** `app/api/health/route.ts` runs `SELECT 1 FROM "Job" WHERE "isExpired" = false LIMIT 1`: 200 `{ ok, dbMs }` when a live job exists, 503 when the query fails (body carries only the Prisma error code, never the message, which can name the database host) or the database has no live jobs (an empty or wrong database). `no-store`. Meant for the host's health check and uptime monitors. **Checked:** 3 route tests (live jobs, empty database, failing query without leaking the host); lint and typecheck pass.

- freehire runs reliably about every 6 hours; the daily schema smoke test samples job pages again (**SEO: none; no page, sitemap, robots or JSON-LD change**). **Found** on 2026-11-12: GitHub started the 05:17 UTC freehire run at 13:43 and never started the 11:17 and 17:17 light runs, so new jobs still arrived once a day; the SEO Schema Smoke Test failed on Nov 11 and 12 with "No URLs found in sitemap" because `sitemap-jobs.xml` is an empty `<urlset>` in indexing phase 1 since #105 (job pages themselves were fine). **What:** `.github/workflows/freehire-sync.yml` is scheduled every hour (`17 * * * *`); a new `plan` job runs `scripts/freehire-plan.js` (plain Node, no npm ci) on scheduled runs, reads earlier runs' `sync (<mode>)` jobs through the GitHub API and picks `full` when no full run started in 20 hours, `light` when no sync started in 5.5 hours, otherwise `skip` (the run ends in seconds). Failed runs count, so a failing run is not retried hourly against freehire's quota; if the API cannot be read it falls back to the old fixed times (05 full; 11, 17, 23 light). Manual runs are named `sync (manual)` and run every step as before. `scripts/seo-schema-smoke.ts` falls back to the job links on `/jobs` and `/remote` when the jobs sitemap is empty, skips noindex pages in that mode (non-English jobs are noindex by design) and fails if none was validated. **Checked:** 7 new planner tests; the planner on the real run history picks light until 20 hours after the 13:43 full run, then full; the smoke test against production passed 20/20 job pages.

- Crawlers skip the server-drawn preview images; the post-merge SEO check waits for the deploy (owner's OK, 2026-11-12) (**SEO: robots.txt changes for named non-Google crawlers only; pages, sitemaps and the default group untouched**). **Found** in Railway's HTTP log for the 5 minutes after the #110 deploy: 319 requests, 7 from browsers; PerplexityBot 166 (160 of them `/job/<slug>/opengraph-image`), the repo's own `seo-validator` 44, Ahrefs and Semrush 62, OpenAI's search bot 23. Job pages took 26.6 s at the median and 117 requests were given up (499). Two causes: the page cache lives on Railway's disk and is empty after every deploy, and each preview image is drawn by the server (seconds of CPU on its one thread) the first time it is asked for. **What:** (1) `app/robots.txt/route.ts`: `Disallow: /*/opengraph-image` for the SEO-tool group (Ahrefs, Semrush, MJ12, Dot, Petal), the AI answer group (PerplexityBot and, newly listed, OAI-SearchBot, both Crawl-delay 2) and GPTBot, anthropic-ai, ClaudeBot. The default `*` group (Googlebot, Bingbot and link-preview bots such as Facebook, X, LinkedIn, Slack) is unchanged, so shared links keep their image cards. (2) `.github/workflows/seo-gates.yml`, SEO Production Proof: waits 6 minutes before its first attempt (the deploy takes ~4 and then warms the busiest pages) and checks 2 URLs at a time instead of 4; timeout 30 to 40 minutes. **Checked:** robots route tests (new: the image rule is in each named group and not in the default group), typecheck, full test suite, workflow YAML parses.

- freehire: three extra light syncs a day, and a closed-job check that fits the API quota (owner's request, 2026-11-12) (**SEO: none; job pages appear sooner and closed jobs are removed again**). **Found** in the 2026-11-11 run: the freehire step itself worked (689 jobs created, 437 updated, no rate limits), but the closed-job check looked up all 5,994 active freehire jobs one request each; after ~250 lookups every one failed at once (4,985 errors, nothing expired, 15-minute budget used). The API key's daily quota was 10,000 requests when last seen (2026-11-05) and two full runs within 24 hours (13:26 and a manual 16:46 run on 2026-11-10) plus this one used it up; the check logged no error text, so the log could not say so. **What:** (1) `.github/workflows/freehire-sync.yml`: light runs at 11:17, 17:17 and 23:17 UTC besides the full 05:17 run. A light run syncs freehire over the last 0.75 days (overlapping the previous run even when GitHub starts one hours late, as it did by 7.5 h on 2026-11-11), then the self-heal, the 7-day expiry and the slices; the closed-job check, direct employers, 4-day-week, worldwide boards and the Google Indexing drain (its 180-a-day cap is per run) stay on the full run. (2) `lib/scrapers/freehireClosedCheck.ts`: at most 2,000 lookups per run (`MAX_LOOKUPS_PER_RUN`), least recently refreshed jobs first, so every job is still checked every few days; stops at the first lookup refused for the quota and expires nothing ("freehire's daily quota is used up"); logs the first three distinct lookup errors with the HTTP status and the rate-limit headers. (3) **Safety fix:** before expiring a job freehire reports closed, the check asks freehire's search whether it is still listed; a failed search used to read as "not listed", so a quota or network failure at that point could expire open jobs. It is now "unknown" and the job is kept (`isStillListed`). **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite (1,591; new tests: cap and order, quota stop, failed second opinion keeps the job); the workflow YAML parses with both schedules. **Not checked:** a real run; the next full run (05:17 UTC) and light run (11:17 UTC) will show it.

- Six UI and motion additions the owner picked (2026-11-12) (**SEO: none; nothing hidden in server HTML, H1/JSON-LD unchanged, the job page salary block and apply button still never move**). (1) **Bars and numbers:** the hero card's salary-band bars and the listing pages' typical-salary bars grow in on load, one after another; the salary-tier share bars grow when the cards scroll in; tier, location and role counts count up as they scroll in (`AnimatedNumber reveal`). (2) **Section indicator:** a small pill under the header on the homepage (from 768px) names the section being read ("Latest jobs", "Salary", "FAQ") from the sections' own pill labels, with a scroll-progress line; aria-hidden, it only repeats visible labels. (3) **Parallax:** the hero card drifts up 40px over the first 700px of scroll and the logo card moves 24px either way as it crosses the screen, both CSS scroll-driven animations (no JS), still where unsupported and under reduced motion. (4) **Page transitions:** on client-side navigations the content fades and lifts in (260 ms, `components/ui/RouteTransition.tsx`); never on the first load. React's `<ViewTransition>` needs React 19.3 (19.2.0 installed), so this is a layout effect plus a CSS animation that works in every browser. Revealed elements now end at `translate: none` instead of `0 0`, so they are never the containing block of fixed children. (5) **Saved search:** after a search on /jobs, a chip under the search bar: "Get alerts for “seo” jobs" (jumps to the alert box) while email alerts are on, otherwise "Follow “seo” jobs" as an RSS feed of that search (`/feeds/jobs.rss?q=`, escaped, cached per query, noindex). (6) **Search skeletons:** the suggestion list shows four shimmering placeholder rows while a new query's suggestions load. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite (1,588), production build, `seo:validate:strict` and `seo:sample`; in a browser: indicator hidden over the hero then "Latest jobs" and "FAQ", hero bars animate, tier bars end at full width, hero card translate 0 to -40px, transition class added on navigation and no translate left after it, 4 skeleton rows during a 1.5 s delayed request then 8 suggestions, chip and per-search feed (40 items).

- Speed: no more prefetch storm; exact salary comparisons; live search finds job titles; hero card fits at 90% zoom (owner's requests, 2026-11-12) (**SEO: no change to H1 text, canonicals, robots, JSON-LD, sitemaps or URLs; no loading.tsx, so 404s and redirects stay real HTTP responses**). **Found** in Railway's HTTP log (2026-11-12 01:12-01:16 UTC, right after a deploy): /job/* pages took 4.8 s at the median, /jobs 16.9 s, many requests ended 499 (visitor gave up), while cached pages answered in 5-20 ms. Requests queue behind work the server does not need to do: (1) every page view prefetched every link in view: 33 full renders on the homepage, 29 on /jobs, 24 on /remote, most sent twice; listing pages read their search params, so Next could not reuse those prefetches and the click fetched the page again; (2) social preview images (`opengraph-image`, 12-48 s under load) were drawn on every request; (3) crawlers (Perplexity, OpenAI, Semrush, Ahrefs) and the repo's own production SEO check. **What:** (1) `components/ui/Link.tsx`, used by every public page instead of `next/link`: no prefetch until the visitor shows intent (pointer over, focus, touch), then a full prefetch the click reuses. Header links show a sweeping lime bar while their page loads (`useLinkStatus`). (2) The three `opengraph-image` routes are cached for a day (`revalidate = 86400`). (3) **Salary accuracy:** `loadPeerSalaries` read only 3,000 rows in no order, so job pages said "Pays more than 3% of 3,000 listings" and gave a typical $238k against the homepage's median of all 20,765 USD listings ($195k). It now reads every listing (same filter as the homepage), and `rankSalary` uses the same percentile as the homepage snapshot, so the two always agree; listing rows' "Pays more than X%" uses the same data. The job page pay card draws the job's own range (lime) against where most comparable listings pay (25th-75th percentile) and the median, on a 5th-95th percentile scale, with a legend. (4) **Live search:** suggestions add live job titles containing every typed word with their counts (e.g. "Principal Data Engineer (10)", linking to that exact search), after the role pages, so roles without a page are found; the suggested jobs put the best title matches first (the results page keeps New first, via a `newFirst` option). (5) **Hero card:** two sample jobs instead of three (all three stay in the HTML), one-line pay floors and less padding: about 700px tall, inside a 1600x900 view (a 1440px screen at 90% zoom). **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite (1,588; new test: job-page and homepage medians match), production build, `seo:validate:strict` and `seo:sample` locally; prefetches on load 0 on `/`, `/jobs`, `/remote` (were 33/29/24); hovering then clicking Remote: one prefetch, no request on click, 100 ms; H1, canonical and JSON-LD unchanged on `/`, `/jobs`, `/remote` and a job page.

- Homepage and listing follow-ups to the workflows.io refresh (owner's requests, 2026-11-12) (**SEO: no change to H1 text, canonicals, robots, JSON-LD, sitemaps or URLs; search results order changes**). (1) **Typing search:** the empty homepage, /jobs and /remote search boxes type example searches into their placeholder ("Search: Staff software engineer", then the next), like freehire.me's box. It starts after load, stops while the box is focused or has text, and is off under reduced motion; the server HTML keeps the plain placeholder. (2) **Hero:** copy, search and filters left-aligned, the salary snapshot as a framed card on the right from 1180px: typical salary and the middle range as tiles, the salary-band bars with their sample jobs, pay floors and jobs by type side by side. (3) **Live search on /jobs and /remote:** a centred search bar under each page's header (`ListingSearch`, the same `SearchBar` as the homepage: role and job suggestions as you type, "/" focuses it). On /remote it searches remote jobs (`remoteMode=remote`). The header search is hidden on those two pages. (4) **Logo wall:** "Top Companies Hiring Now" is two rows sliding in opposite directions (CSS, each row repeated so it never shows a gap at 1920px, repeats `inert`; pauses on hover or focus; still under reduced motion). (5) **Numbers band:** companies hiring, new this week and countries at the size of the main figure (were 1.25rem). (6) **Search order:** "Most relevant" puts jobs carrying the New tag (posted in the last 3 days, `NEW_TAG_WINDOW_MS`) first, then the rest, each by score; a search like "seo" had new jobs mid-list. (7) **Job rows:** the posting time shows next to the New tag ("New · 4h ago"). **Checked:** typecheck, lint on changed files, full test suite (1,587; new `rankByRelevance` tests), production build, `seo:validate:strict` and `seo:sample` locally; H1, JSON-LD count and canonical on `/`, `/jobs`, `/remote` unchanged; in a browser: placeholder cycles, suggestions on /remote while typing "engin", the two logo rows move in opposite directions and the hovered row pauses, no console errors.

- Design refresh after workflows.io (owner's request, 2026-11-11): cooler off-white page, Outfit headlines mixing light and semibold words, pill labels, lime buttons and arrow chips with black text, white hairline panels instead of lime-tinted blocks, and scroll-triggered reveals (**SEO: no change to H1 text, canonicals, hreflang, robots, JSON-LD, sitemaps or URLs; reveals never hide server HTML**). **Reference checked:** workflows.io uses Framer Motion: blocks fade in and lift 6-10px over 200-320ms with 40-110ms staggers, easing `cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)`, and headings come in word by word. **What:** (1) Tokens (`src/styles/design-tokens.css`, `app/globals.css`): page `#f4f6f5`, cooler greys, fill lime `#a3e635` (near-black text on it ~13:1; never used as text), `--font-display` is Outfit (already self-hosted for the wordmark, no new font file). Shared classes: `.eyebrow-pill`, `.arrow-link` / `.arrow-chip`, `.panel-hairline`, `.section-heading`. (2) Pass 1, homepage hero: centred, the H1 in light + semibold weights (same text), pill of markets, white search bar with a lime button, and the salary snapshot as one hairline panel of three cells. Desktop header: flat at the top, plain text links with a lime underline on the current page, a white capsule once scrolled. (3) Scroll reveal: `components/ui/ScrollReveal.tsx` (one IntersectionObserver, mounted in the layout) marks only elements still below the viewport as pending and reveals them as they enter. Content on screen at load, crawlers' tall viewports, no-JS and reduced motion get the plain page. `data-reveal-stagger` reveals children in sequence (heading words via `RevealWords`, homepage job rows and card grids); `JobRowList` reveals each row on its own on every listing page, including rows added by infinite scroll. Replaces the CSS `.reveal-on-scroll` (scroll-scrubbed, it left below-the-fold sections blank in full-page captures). (4) Pass 2, homepage: section headings via `SectionHeading` (pill label + light/semibold words), job rows with larger logo tiles, black mono salary, a "New" chip and a lime "View job", the numbers band and live feed as white hairline panels, flat card hover (no lime glow). (5) Pass 3, rest of the site: the listing header (`/jobs`, `/remote`, role, location, tier pages) without the tinted block and with a light H1 whose `Accent` words are semibold; job page hero and description as hairline panels; company page header; salary pages (`PageChrome`); `/jobs/4-day-week`; companies pages; `JobCard`; footer feed box and buttons. **Checked:** typecheck, lint on changed files, full test suite (1,584 pass; the NewRolesStrip test now reads the heading's text, which is unchanged, instead of raw HTML), production build, and both SEO gates (`seo:validate:strict`, `seo:sample`) on a local build seeded like CI. Screenshots at 1440px and iPhone 13: no horizontal overflow; reveal states go pending to in on scroll and stay off under reduced motion; H1 text on `/`, `/jobs`, `/remote` and a company page is the same as before.

- Salary parsing: small numbers are ignored when the text has an annual figure (**SEO: no markup change; Mastercard and other employers whose benefits text sits next to the pay range can get jobs paying $100k+ listed, which adds job pages**). **Found** (2026-11-11) in the daily sync log: 225 Mastercard jobs (Workday) were rejected with `salaryMin=10`. Mastercard's postings have no pay field, so ingest reads the description: `extractCompensationSnippet` starts in the benefits paragraph ("deferred compensation ... 10 annual paid U.S. observed holidays"), and `extractNumbers` counted any number from 10 to 999 as money once a currency appeared anywhere in the text. A posting with "Pay Ranges ... $61,000 - $102,000 USD" parsed as 10 to 102,000. **What:** `extractNumbers` (`lib/normalizers/salary.ts`) drops numbers under 1,000 when the text also has one of 1,000 or more; hourly rates on their own ("$150 per hour") are unchanged. **Checked:** the live Mastercard posting now parses as 61,000 to 102,000; 1 new test; full suite (1,585 tests), typecheck and lint pass. The effect shows in the next daily sync.

- Direct-employer sync: ends inside its time limit, reads the least recently synced employers first, and one failed Workday job page no longer fails the whole board (**SEO: no change**). **Found** (2026-11-11): the freehire daily sync's "Sync direct employers" step no longer crashed (the undici fix held) but "has timed out after 90 minutes", part-way through the Workday boards, so the employers after them were not refreshed that day. The Workday lane reads one board at a time and every job's own page (salaries are only there): Mastercard took 359 s for 896 jobs (35 kept), and Intel spent 135 s and then failed whole because one job page answered 404 (taken down between the list and the page). **Changed:** (1) `scripts/sync-direct-employers.ts` starts no employer after a 70-minute budget (`DIRECT_EMPLOYERS_BUDGET_MIN`), lets the one in progress finish, and logs the ones not reached; (2) employers are read least recently synced first (`orderBySyncAge` in `lib/companies/directEmployerSchedule.ts`, never-synced first), and a successful sync stamps the company's `lastScrapedAt` (until now never set by this path; the only other reader marks board copies of ATS jobs with a flag nothing reads), so whoever was not reached goes first the next day, well inside the 7-day expiry; (3) `scrapeWorkday` skips a job whose page fails and keeps the board, logging how many it skipped. A cap on Workday job pages per board was considered and not done: a job not read is not marked seen, and the 7-day expiry would close jobs that are still open. **Checked:** 2 new tests (a 404 job page skipped, the other jobs kept, failing on the old code; the sync order); typecheck, lint, full test suite. Not run against the live boards here; the next daily sync will show the step finishing.

- Phase 1 submits hub pages only; `/remote` has page links; job pages' similar jobs match titles only (**SEO: in phase 1 (the default) the sitemap index no longer lists `/sitemap-jobs/1` and `robots.txt` no longer declares `/sitemap-jobs.xml`; both job sitemaps answer an empty `<urlset>` (200) until `INDEXING_PHASE=2`. Job pages keep `index, follow`, their canonicals and JobPosting, and stay linked from `/jobs` pages. `/remote?page=N` pages are new: indexable, self-canonical (as `/jobs?page=N`), titled "… (Page N)", 404 past the last page. No other title, meta, canonical, robots or JSON-LD change**). **Asked** (2026-11-11): fix the slow job pages and `/remote` pagination, and make sure only hub pages are submitted for indexing first, not all 5,000 job pages. **Found:** (1) Phase 1 (`lib/seo/indexingPhase.ts`, `lib/seo/sitemapPolicy.ts`) already held back cities, skills, levels and the rest, but kept the jobs sitemap "for Google Jobs": 5,000 job URLs beside 103 hub URLs, while Google had indexed only the homepage and left `/jobs`, `/remote`, `/companies` and the role and salary hubs "Crawled - currently not indexed". (2) Of 40 job pages timed on their first render after the 14:52 deploy, the 29 whose similar jobs come from a title-word search averaged 3.34 s (15 over 3 s); the 6 with a known role averaged 1.54 s and the 5 with no similar-jobs query 1.67 s. The search matched each title word in title, company, location, tech stack and skills (case-insensitive patterns, twice for the count and the candidates), and its cache key is the job's own title words, so it was rarely shared. Description processing was ruled out (about 1 ms per job on four real descriptions). (3) `/remote` listed the 12 newest jobs and linked to `/jobs?remoteMode=remote`, a `noindex` view, for the rest. **What:** (1) `sitemap-jobs` leaves `PHASE_1_ENABLED_SITEMAP_FAMILIES` and `'jobs'` leaves the phase-1 set in `shouldAdvertiseSitemapFamily`; `app/sitemap-jobs.xml` and `app/sitemap-jobs/[page]` return `buildPhase1SilencedSitemapResponse` in phase 1 (Google already has `/sitemap-jobs/1` from earlier reads). Phase 1 now lists: `sitemap-hubs.xml`, the released pSEO batch, salary bands, salary guides, the phase-1 countries and published companies. CLAUDE.md's sitemap rule says so. (2) `queryJobs` takes `keywordFields`; the job page's similar jobs pass `['title']`. (3) `/remote` lists 24 jobs a page with numbered page links (`Pagination`, as `/jobs`); the page's list is cached per region and page; the hub snapshot no longer loads a job list. **Checked:** a production build on a local database with `INDEXING_PHASE=1`: `robots.txt` declares `sitemap.xml` only; the index lists hubs, batch, salary and country sitemaps and no job sitemap; `/sitemap-jobs.xml` and `/sitemap-jobs/1` answer an empty urlset with `X-Indexing-Phase-Silenced: 1`; `/remote` (77 remote jobs) lists 24 and links pages 2–4, page 4 has the last 5, page 5 is a 404, each page self-canonical and indexable. New tests: phase-1 job sitemaps empty (shard and index), robots declares the jobs sitemap from phase 2, sitemap index without job sitemaps in phase 1, keyword matched in the title only with `keywordFields`. Full suite (1,582), typecheck and lint pass. **Not measured yet:** the job-page time on production after this change (the local database is too small to show it); re-time the same title-search pages after the deploy.

- Job pages: when a company has another open job with the same title, the page title names the city (or "Remote") (**SEO: `<title>`, `og:title` and `twitter:title` change on those job pages only; no description, canonical, robots, JSON-LD or sitemap change**). **Asked** (2026-11-11): analyse the whole site with the checks from github.com/Illyism/seo-skills (`seo-autopilot`'s daily health check and `seo-title-optimization`) and fix any major issue. **Checked** (crawl of all 5,103 sitemap URLs, 3 requests at a time, plus 2,599 internal link targets and Search Console through SEOConsole): every sitemap URL answers 200 without a redirect; each has one absolute self-canonical, no `noindex`, one `<title>` and description, one H1, valid JSON-LD, `lang`, viewport and `og:image`; every JobPosting has Google's required fields; no internal link on a hub page is broken; `/jobs?page=N` pagination is plain links and indexable, so every job page can be reached; old URLs Google still knows return 404/410, redirect, or are `noindex`; the sitemap index was read on 2026-11-08 with no errors; IndexNow is verified and submitting. **Found:** (1) 605 of the 5,000 job pages shared a title with another (213 titles), mostly one employer posting the same role per city (Esri's "Account Manager - Native Nations" in Redlands, Vienna and Denver), since the title is "Role at Company — pay" with no place. (2) Not fixable in code: Google indexes almost nothing besides the homepage. Of the hubs inspected on 2026-11-11, `/jobs`, `/remote`, `/companies`, `/blog`, `/jobs/software-engineer`, `/jobs/100k-plus` and `/salary/software-engineer` are "Crawled - currently not indexed" (fetched fine, robots allowed, canonical accepted), `/high-paying-jobs` and `/jobs/no-degree` are unknown to Google, and Search Console shows 15 impressions in 28 days. That points to how much Google values the site (few links to it, many job-feed pages), not to markup; it needs links from other sites, requested indexing for the key hubs and time. (3) Smaller: 8.6% of job pages took over 3 s on a first request; 24 hub pages carry 400–480 KB of HTML; `/remote` lists 12 jobs and loads more by button, with no page links (its jobs are also on `/jobs`). `validThrough` is absent on purpose when a source gives no closing date (`lib/seo/jobJsonLd.ts`), as Google advises. **What:** `fitTitle` (`lib/seo/jobMeta.ts`) takes an optional place and tries "Role at Company (City) — pay", then "Role (City) — pay", before the usual forms; `jobTitlePlace` gives "Remote" or the city; the job page counts the company's other open jobs with the same title (one indexed count by `companyId`) and passes the result to `buildJobMetadata`. Postings that share title, company and city (19 groups) keep one title. **Checked:** a production build on a local database: two same-titled Wise postings read "Staff Backend Engineer at Wise (Denver) — C$315k–C$394k/yr" and "… (Austin) — $242k–$303k/yr", a job with a unique title is unchanged, canonicals unchanged. 3 new tests (titles per city, 60-character limit with a place, `jobTitlePlace`); full suite (1,577), typecheck and lint pass.

- Warm-up: a hub sitemap that comes back empty is tried again, so the first pass after a deploy also warms the skill, city and senior-role pages (**SEO: no change; no page, sitemap or robots output changes**). **Found** (2026-11-11, first deploy of the hub warm-up): the pass after start warmed 45 hub pages instead of about 66; every later pass warmed all 78 (12 + 66), from 05:27 onwards. The 21 missing pages are the ones listed in `/sitemap-batch/1.xml` (10 skills, 6 cities, `/jobs/level/senior`, 4 senior roles). That sitemap builds its page list from the database the first time it is asked after a restart, and if that build fails or runs long it answers with an empty `<urlset>` (200) rather than an error (`collectPseoBatchEntries`, `app/sitemap-batch/[batch]/route.ts`; the code's own comment: "only the very first build after a restart can make a request wait or fail"). Which of the two happened was not logged. So those pages waited 45 minutes after each deploy for the next pass. **What:** `hubPaths` (`lib/runtime/warmPages.ts`) reads a sitemap with no URLs again after 15 s and then after 30 s (`EMPTY_SITEMAP_RETRY_MS`); the build keeps running after the first request, so a later try gets the list. A sitemap still empty after three tries adds no pages, and the others are kept. **Checked:** 2 new tests (an empty batch sitemap read three times and its pages warmed; one that stays empty is skipped after the two waits); both fail on the previous code. Full suite (1,574), typecheck and lint pass.

- SEO: titles, H1s and descriptions use the words people search for (six-figure jobs, high-paying jobs, jobs that pay $100k a year), and hub pages no longer go cold for Googlebot (**SEO: `<title>`, meta description, `og:title` and H1 change on `/`, `/jobs`, the four salary bands, `/high-paying-jobs`, `/remote`, `/remote/<role>`, `/jobs/no-degree`, country, city, skill, company and role salary-guide pages; the footer gains a sitewide link to `/high-paying-jobs`; no canonical, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemap change**). **Asked** (2026-11-11): make every page SEO-optimised, on-page and technical, with relevant keywords for roles, skills and jobs and for the site's own name (six figure jobs, 6 figure jobs, high paying jobs). **Found** by crawling the 76 pages in the hub sitemaps plus company, job and search pages (title, description, H1, canonical, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD): canonicals, robots, JSON-LD and hreflang were all correct, but (1) the biggest related searches were missing from the pages that should rank for them. OpenSEO's US figures (monthly searches, 2026-11-11; estimates that change): "high paying jobs" and "highest paying jobs" about 201k each, "high paying jobs without a degree" 22.2k, "jobs that pay 100k a year" 8.1k, "high paying remote jobs" 8.1k, "six figure jobs" and "6 figure jobs" 5.4k each. `/jobs` said "Six-Figure Tech Jobs" though it lists sales, finance and legal roles too; salary bands said "$100k–$199k Jobs with Published Salaries"; `/remote` "Remote $100k+ Jobs"; country H1s said "$100k+ Jobs in Australia" above a page whose floor is A$140k; skill titles started with "$100k+"; salary guides read "$100k+ Backend Engineer salary guide" (the search is "backend engineer salary"; the machine-learning one was 63 characters, over the 60 limit); company pages were titled "OpenAI $100k+ Jobs - 659 Open Positions" with the H1 "OpenAI"; "Devops Engineer" on remote and salary pages. `/high-paying-jobs` had no sitewide link. (2) Hub pages few people visit took 10–19 s on a first request (`/jobs/100k-plus` 19 s, `/jobs/300k-plus` 13.7 s, `/jobs/city/austin` 13.5 s, `/jobs/skills/aws` 10.6 s), then under 1 s: a cache entry is served for its TTL plus one hour and then expires, and those pages are not visited that often. `/jobs/4-day-week` took 3.5 s on every visit (its job list was not cached). **What:** new `lib/seo/metaText.ts` helpers `buildCountryTitle`, `buildSkillTitle`, `buildSalaryGuideTitle`, and new wording in `buildJobsHubTitle`/`Description`, `buildSalaryTierTitle`/`Description`, `buildCityTitle`, `buildCompanyPageTitle`. Examples: `/` "Six Figure Jobs | High-Paying $100k+ Jobs, Salary Shown" (also the share title); `/jobs` "19,764 Six-Figure Jobs — High-Paying $100k+ Roles", H1 "All six-figure jobs ($100k+)"; `/jobs/100k-plus` "5,753 Jobs That Pay $100k–$199k a Year | Six Figure Jobs", H1 "Jobs that pay $100k–$199k a year"; `/high-paying-jobs` "High-Paying Jobs: 65 Highest-Paying Roles | Six Figure Jobs" when it fits; `/remote` "4,710 High-Paying Remote Jobs ($100k+) | Six Figure Jobs", H1 "High-paying remote jobs ($100k+)"; `/remote/<role>` "506 Remote Software Engineer Jobs Paying $100k+"; `/jobs/no-degree` "High-Paying Jobs Without a Degree ($100k+) | Six Figure Jobs"; countries "1,637 High-Paying Jobs in Canada (C$120k+) | Six Figure Jobs", H1 "High-paying jobs in Canada", and a description that reads as a sentence instead of "Find high paying Canada roles, six figure jobs…"; cities "High-Paying Jobs in Austin ($100k+)"; skills "674 Python Jobs Paying $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs"; salary guides "Software Engineer Salary: $229k Median | Six Figure Jobs" (the guide's own USD median; "… Salary Guide ($100k+)" when there are too few listings for one); companies "OpenAI Jobs: 659 Openings Paying $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs", H1 "OpenAI jobs". `slugToLabel` writes DevOps, MLOps and DevSecOps, and salary guides use it. The footer's Jobs column gains "Highest-Paying Jobs" and "No-Degree Tech Jobs" becomes "Jobs Without a Degree". Warm-up (`lib/runtime/warmPages.ts`, `instrumentation.ts`): after the 12 busiest pages, every page in the hub sitemaps (read from `/sitemap.xml`'s children, minus the job and company sitemaps) is requested once, 2 s apart, and the whole set again every 45 minutes, inside the cache's TTL-plus-one-hour window, so each request serves the cached page and refreshes it in the background; passes never overlap, the log lines are `[warm:start]`, `[warm:hubs]` and `[warm:rewarm]` with any page over 3 s named, and `WARM_ON_START=0` still turns it all off. `/jobs/4-day-week` caches its job list for 10 minutes like its counts. **Not known:** how these changes move rankings or clicks (Search Console returned no query data through SEOConsole today, so there is no baseline here; compare impressions per page in 4–8 weeks); whether Google treats "six figure" and "6 figure" as the same search (the homepage title says "Six Figure", its description "6-figure"). **Not changed:** blog posts, `/salary`, job pages (already "Title at Company — $pay"), role pages (already "N Role Jobs Paying $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs"), the homepage H1, page weight (the homepage HTML is about 476 KB and salary guides about 500 KB, mostly page data for the job lists; worth a separate look). **Checked:** a production build against a local database: titles, H1s, descriptions, canonicals and robots as above on 17 page types, all titles within 60 characters; `[warm:hubs] 37/37 pages in 75s` (the local sitemaps list 37 hubs; production lists 76). Tests: updated metaText tests plus new ones for country, skill and salary-guide titles (length limit across every label and count), and 5 warm-up tests (sitemap parsing, job/company sitemaps skipped, never throws, pause between pages, re-warm interval inside the cache window). Full suite (1,572), typecheck and lint pass.

- In-memory cache: least recently used, 2,000 entries, and the search vocabulary goes through it (**SEO: no change; pages render the same, faster after crawls**). **Found** (2026-11-11, after the PR #100 deploy): `/jobs` answered in 0.04–0.6 s most of the time but took 14.6–17 s now and then (01:54:11, 01:54:35, 02:01:09 in Railway's request log). Between 01:56:36 and 02:01:07 the server answered 501 requests for 478 distinct pages, 448 of them job pages from this repo's post-merge production check (`seo-validator/1.0`); each job page adds its own cache entries ("similar jobs", salary rank), and `memoAsync` held 500 entries and evicted by last write, so a few minutes of crawling pushed out the entries `/jobs` reads however often it was visited, and the next visitor waited for the full rebuild (`/jobs` alone is 26 s uncached on production data, per the deploy's warm-up log). Separately, `getSearchVocabulary` kept its own 30-minute cache with no shared load and no stale-while-revalidate: at each expiry every request (the `/jobs` page, each keyword search) read the same 20,000 rows at once and waited. **What:** `lib/cache/memo.ts`: a read moves its entry to the back of the eviction order (least recently used), and `MAX_ENTRIES` is 2,000 (from 500); `lib/search/vocabulary.ts` loads through `memoAsync('search-vocabulary', 30 min)`, so concurrent callers share one load and an expired vocabulary is served while a new one loads. **Not measured:** the extra memory of 1,500 more entries on production data (most are small lists and counts; expected well under 200 MB). **Checked:** new tests: an entry read every 100 writes survives 2,050 new keys while one read once is evicted; three concurrent vocabulary calls make one database read; an expired vocabulary is returned without waiting. They fail on the old code (3 of 11) and pass now; full suite (1,563), typecheck and lint pass.

- Deploys: busiest pages warmed one at a time on start, SEO-tool crawlers slowed, the post-merge production check runs 4 requests at a time (**SEO: `robots.txt` gains a group for AhrefsBot, SemrushBot, MJ12bot, DotBot and PetalBot (`Crawl-delay: 5`) and one for PerplexityBot (`Crawl-delay: 2`), each repeating the `*` group's disallows; the `*` group, Googlebot, Bingbot and the sitemap lines are unchanged**). **Why** (2026-11-11 outage, about 00:10–00:51 UTC): right after the PR #98 deploy, with every page cache empty, crawlers (PerplexityBot from 8 IPs, AhrefsBot, MJ12bot, Applebot, PetalBot) and this repo's own "SEO Production Proof" job (`seo-validator/1.0`, 12 parallel requests over about 1,150 sitemap URLs) asked for heavy pages at once; the server ran out of its ~760 MB heap (`NODE_OPTIONS` on Railway, since raised to 4 GB) and crash-looped. Cloudflare could not have stopped it: the Free plan allows one rate-limiting rule (in use: 150 page requests / 10 s per IP, verified bots exempt) with 10-second windows per IP, and Perplexity's burst was about 5 requests per IP. **What:** `instrumentation.ts` runs `warmPages` (`lib/runtime/warmPages.ts`) 3 s after a production server starts: `/`, `/jobs`, `/remote`, `/companies`, `/salary`, `/high-paying-jobs`, `/jobs/100k-plus`, `/jobs/200k-plus`, `/jobs/location/united-states`, `/jobs/software-engineer`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/sitemap-jobs.xml`, one request at a time (the first retried while the server starts listening), and logs `[warm] N/12 pages warmed`; `WARM_ON_START=0` turns it off. `robots.txt` asks SEO-tool crawlers (no visitors) for 5 s between requests and PerplexityBot (AI answers, sends visitors) for 2 s; AhrefsBot, SemrushBot and MJ12bot are widely documented as honouring `Crawl-delay`; whether PerplexityBot, DotBot and PetalBot do was not confirmed, so it may not slow them; Googlebot ignores it and is not listed. `.github/workflows/seo-gates.yml` runs the production proof with `SEO_CONCURRENCY=4` (same 1,000-per-sitemap sample). **Checked:** a production build started as on Railway logged `12/12 pages warmed in 2s` on a local database, and the first visits to warmed pages took 25–56 ms; 3 new warm-up tests (one request at a time, first retried, never throws) and a `robots.txt` test (groups, delays, disallows, no Googlebot/Bingbot); full suite (1,560), typecheck and lint pass.

- No job source named in any page: the browser gets each job's public id (the code in its URL) instead of the internal id, and no source fields (**SEO: no change; job URLs are built from the internal id exactly as before, server side, and no title, meta, canonical, robots, JSON-LD or sitemap output changes**). **Asked** (2026-11-11): no mention of freehire on any page. **Found:** no page showed the name as text, but every page listing a freehire job carried it in its source: the RSC payload's job props (`"id":"board:freehire:…"`, `"source":"board:freehire"`), React keys, and the job page's apply, save and report buttons (`jobId`); live, `/jobs/location/switzerland` had 72 occurrences and a freehire job page 15. The "load more" APIs (`/api/jobs`, `/api/company/<slug>/jobs`) and the homepage feed (`/api/jobs/latest`) sent the same. **What:** `lib/jobs/publicJob.ts`: `publicJobId` (the URL's short code, which is `Job.shortId`), `toPublicJob` (public id, precomputed `href`, without `source`, `sourceRef`, `externalId`, `dedupeKey`, `sourcePriority`), and `jobByClientId` (finds a job by public id or, from pages rendered before this change, internal id). Used where jobs reach client components: listing rows (`JobRows`, `JobList`, `LatestOpportunities`), `/jobs`' infinite list and `/api/jobs`, `/api/company/<slug>/jobs`, the job page's similar-job cards and its apply/save/report buttons, and homepage feed items; `JobRow` and `JobCard` use the precomputed `href`. `/api/apply-click` and `/api/report-job` look the job up with `jobByClientId` and store the click against the internal id. Admin pages (`/ubaid93`) are unchanged. Jobs saved in a visitor's browser before this change show as not saved (they were keyed by internal id). **Checked:** on a production build with 12 jobs stored as `board:freehire:…` (source `board:freehire`): `/`, `/jobs` (pages 1 and 2, `?country=CH`), `/jobs/location/switzerland`, a role page, `/remote`, `/search`, `/companies`, a company page, `/api/jobs`, `/api/jobs/latest`, `/api/company/<slug>/jobs`, `/feeds/jobs.rss`, `/sitemap-jobs/1` and a freehire job's own page contain "freehire" 0 times, while their public ids appear where those jobs are listed; an apply click sent with a public id was stored against the internal id, and a report was filed. Live, the blog index and its 6 posts, `/about`, `/mcp` and `/llms.txt` had no mention. 4 new tests; 2 existing tests updated to expect public ids; full suite (1,556), typecheck and lint pass.

- Listing pages: jobs on the first screen, counts on Seniority and Company size, sort beside the results, one search box on the homepage and `/search` (**SEO: on `/jobs` the category tiles (links to `/jobs/category/*`) move below the results, still in the HTML; on `/jobs`, role, salary-tier and country pages the header loses the "Browse N jobs" jump link and the work-type links (to `?remoteMode=…` views, which are noindex); the header search form is not rendered on `/` and `/search`, which have their own; no title, meta, canonical, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemap change**). **Found** (2026-11-10 review): the first job started at 786px on `/jobs` at 1440×900 and at 1,355px on a 390px phone (below the header, its "Browse N jobs" button and work-type links, two or three rows of top-role chips, the typical-salary card with its bars, and the category tiles); Seniority and Company size had no counts, so an option could leave nothing; the sort control sat in the page header, away from the list; the homepage and `/search` showed two search boxes. **What:** (12) `loadLevelSizeCounts` (`lib/jobs/levelSizeFacets.ts`) counts jobs per seniority (one `RoleInference` group-by) and per company size (five counts) with the page's other filters, cached like the other facets; the pills show the count and an option with none is disabled unless it is on. (13) The four pages with the results bar drop the header's jump button and work-type links (the bar's count and the Work type filter say the same); on `/jobs` the category tiles follow the results; on phones the header's chip rows scroll sideways in one row and the salary card hides its bars. `lib/listing/workTypeStats.ts` and its test are removed (no caller left). (14) The sort control (and the phone Filters button) sit at the right of "Showing N jobs". (15) `SiteHeader` leaves its search out on `/` and `/search`. **Checked:** on a production build against a local database: first job at 518px on `/jobs` and 524px on a role page at 1440×900, 690px and 595px on a 390px phone; Seniority and Company size pills show counts; Seniority pills read Mid-level 13, Senior 24, Staff 12, Principal 11, Lead 12 on remote jobs, the same as a direct database count, and "1–10" employees (none) is disabled; screenshots at 1440 and 390px. Full suite (1,552; the 3 `workTypeStats` tests went with the file), typecheck and lint pass.

- Listing filters redesigned: one pill style everywhere, a short sidebar, "Showing N jobs" with removable chips, a loading state, and "Show N jobs" on phones (**SEO: no URL, title, meta, canonical, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemap change; filters write the same query parameters, and filtered views stay `noindex, follow`. The homepage freshness line changes wording**). **Found** (2026-11-10 review of the live site): three filter designs (homepage pills and selects, a checklist sidebar on `/jobs`, five selects on `/search`); the `/jobs` sidebar was nine open sections, about 2,480px of content in an 800px scroll box, so it looked cut off after "Work Type"; one drag of the salary slider sent 19 requests, and its hint ("most accurate when a country is selected") stood for "without a country only US-dollar salaries are compared"; nothing above the results said how many jobs matched or which filters were on; a click changed the list with no sign of loading; the phone sheet was about 2,560px with no way to see the result or close it but the ×; the homepage said "5,094 new this week · Newest listing posted Sep 3" because its newest job was the one scraped last. **What:** `JobsFiltersPanel` (`app/jobs/_components/JobsFilters.tsx`) uses pills for salary ($150k+ to $300k+, in the selected country's currency: `lib/jobs/salaryFilter.ts`, now also what `/jobs` compares in), work type, seniority and company size; countries, roles, remote regions and technologies show six options and "Show all N" (with a search box for long lists); Remote region, Company size, Technology and Visa start closed unless in use; "Clear all" shows only when something is on. `ActiveFilters` puts "Showing N jobs" and a chip per active filter (× removes it) above the results on `/jobs`, role, salary-tier and country pages (`lib/jobs/activeFilters.ts`; pinned filters such as a country page's country get none). `useListingNavigation` runs each change in a transition and dims the results while it loads. The phone sheet scrolls between its title and a footer with "Clear all" and "Show N jobs", which closes it; the toolbar button shows how many filters are on. `/search` and the homepage share `FilterPills` (`components/filters/`): `/search` work arrangement, minimum salary and seniority are pills, location and remote region stay selects. The homepage line reads "newest posted 3h ago" and, with the new "Most Recent" order, is the newest posting. **Checked:** on a local database: chips, counts and "Clear all" on `/jobs`; a country click dims the list and applies; the phone sheet's footer read "Show 72 jobs", then "Show 18 jobs" after $200k+, and closed the sheet; `/search` and the homepage screenshotted at 1440 and 390px. 7 new tests; full suite (1,555), typecheck and lint pass.

- Listing filters: Role, Seniority and Company size work again (**SEO: no change to indexable URLs; the filtered views these parameters produce were already `noindex, follow`, as any query parameter but `page` makes a listing page noindex (`shouldNoindexListingPage`)**). **Found** (2026-11-11, live site): `proxy.ts` removes any query parameter outside an allowlist with a 301, and `role`, `seniority` and `companySize` were not on it, so `/jobs?seniority=senior`, `/jobs?role=software-engineer` and `/jobs?companySize=1000%2B` all redirected to `/jobs`; on role and salary-tier pages (`/jobs/<slug>`) a second rule removed `seniority` and `tech` with a 308 (`/jobs/software-engineer?seniority=senior` → `/jobs/software-engineer`). Every click on those filters in the panel reloaded the unfiltered list. **What:** the three parameters join the allowlist, and the `/jobs/<slug>` rule keeps removing only the legacy `skill`. **Checked:** a new test sends each parameter the panel writes through `proxy` (10 cases): 7 failed against the old file, all pass now; unknown parameters and `skill` are still removed.

- Job lists: "Most Recent" means newest posting first, one date style, no placeholder locations, and the Feedback button off the salaries (**SEO: JobPosting `jobLocation` no longer carries a placeholder such as "Defer to Job Opening Location" as `addressLocality` (the country stays); the order of jobs on listing pages sorted by date changes; no title, meta, canonical, robots, hreflang or sitemap change**). **Found** (2026-11-10 review of the live site): (1) `/jobs` sorted "Most Recent" by `createdAt` (when we first saw a job) while each row shows the posting date, so page 1 read 9/3/2026, 8/5/2026, "4 days ago" … 12/9/2023; (2) on desktop the lime Feedback pill, fixed in the bottom-right corner, covered the salary of the job row under it ("€180k - €21…" on `/jobs`, "$152k - $20…" on `/search`); (3) Quicknode's "Senior Software Engineer - Go" showed "Defer to Job Opening Location" as its location; (4) rows mixed "4 days ago" with US-style dates ("9/3/2026") for anything older than 30 days, and the label used `updatedAt`, which the daily sync refreshes. **What:** (1) `queryJobs` with `sortBy: 'date'` reads the newest jobs with a posting date (by `postedAt`) and without one (by `createdAt`, the rule "new this week" already uses), ids and dates only, and merges them (`mergeByPostingDate`, `lib/jobs/queryJobs.ts`); Prisma cannot order by `COALESCE(postedAt, createdAt)` and no schema change or index is added; (2) from 1024px wide the Feedback button is a slim tab on the right edge, half way down, in the page's colours (`components/feedback/FeedbackWidget.module.css`); phones keep the small icon that hides while scrolling; (3) `isPlaceholderLocation` (`lib/location/jobLocation.ts`) treats "Defer to Job Opening/Posting Location", "See job description", "TBD", "N/A", "Not specified" and similar as empty in job rows and cards, on the job page and in JobPosting, which then fall back to the stored city or country; (4) `formatRelativeTime` gives one compact style ("just now", "3h ago", "4d ago", "2mo ago", "1y ago"), and rows and cards label the posting date (`postedAt`, else `createdAt`), as the "New" tag and the order do. **Checked:** on a local database (184 jobs, posting dates from today to two years ago, some first seen recently): page 1 of `/jobs` is all "New", page 4 runs 17d to 23d ago, the last page ends with the 1y and 2y old postings that used to lead; the placeholder job shows "USA"; the tab sits in the gutter at 1440 and 1280px and phones are unchanged. 3 + 3 + 4 new tests; full suite (1,538), typecheck and lint pass.

- Job descriptions: job-search lists pasted in by job boards are removed; `/companies` title and description say which companies they count (**SEO: `/companies` `<title>` and meta description change; JobPosting `description` and the visible description get shorter on affected job pages; no canonical, robots or hreflang change**). **Found** (2026-11-10): `/job/data-engineer-j-esms6l` (a WhatJobs posting) showed three lists before the job ("The cities near North Cowichan, BC that boast the highest number of engineer jobs are:", "The 10 cities near … that have the most job openings are:", "The 10 most popular job searches in … are:", each followed by up to ten city names or search terms) in both the page and its JobPosting JSON-LD. Separately, `/companies` was titled "402 Companies Hiring $100k+ Roles" and described as "402 companies hiring for 16,684 $100k+ tech jobs" while the home page says "2,557+ companies": both are right (402 is the directory of companies with 5+ open jobs, 2,557 every company with an open job), but the title read as the site total. **What:** `stripLocalSearchLists` in `lib/jobs/descriptionCleaning.ts`, run by `cleanJobDescriptionHtml` (job page, JSON-LD and ingest), removes each lead-in and the whole-line items after it (at most ten, each at most 30 characters with no sentence punctuation), with `<br>`- or `<p>`-separated lines, and keeps the rest of the description; stored rows are cleaned when rendered, so no backfill is needed. `/companies` is now titled "402 Companies With 5+ Open $100k+ Jobs | Six Figure Jobs" and described as "402 companies with 5+ open $100k+ jobs (16,684 jobs between them), out of 2,557 companies hiring. …" (`buildCompaniesIndexTitle`, `buildCompaniesIndexDescription` in `lib/seo/metaText.ts`). **Limits:** a short heading (30 characters or less, no punctuation) right after a list of fewer than ten items would also be removed. **Not changed:** the 47 + 25 near-identical "Account Specialist(s)" pages are one employer's separate postings per city, each titled with its location, so they stay separate pages. **Checked:** 5 new description tests and updated `/companies` meta tests; the live WhatJobs description went from 4,963 to 4,167 characters with no list left; full suite (1,524 tests), typecheck and lint pass.

- Job alerts send through Resend's HTTPS API (Railway blocks SMTP below Pro), stop cleanly at the provider's daily limit, and the homepage "6figjobs today" band is light (**SEO: no change; homepage CSS only, the same text and numbers**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): find a free service that can send the alert emails, and make the "6figjobs today" band not dark. **Found:** Railway's outbound networking docs say "SMTP is only available on the Pro plan and above" and recommend HTTPS email APIs, Resend first. The alerts sent only over SMTP (`EMAIL_SERVER`), so confirmation emails from the web app would have failed below Pro. Free tiers checked (each from the provider's page or third-party summaries; they change): Resend 3,000 a month and 100 a day; Brevo 300 a day; Mailjet 6,000 a month and 200 a day; Cloudflare's Email Service sends only on the paid Workers plan. **Changed:** (1) `RESEND_API_KEY` sends through `POST https://api.resend.com/emails`, with the same List-Unsubscribe headers. SMTP (`EMAIL_SERVER`) remains as an alternative; the key wins when both are set, and `alertsEnabled()` accepts either. A 429 is retried once after 1.5 s; a second 429 stops the run (`SendLimitError`). Errors never include the provider's response body, which can echo the address. (2) The digest run loads the due alerts longest-waiting first (never sent, then oldest last email), pauses 600 ms between emails, and on the provider's limit stops and reports `left_for_next_run`. Those alerts stay due and go first the next day, instead of the same ones missing out every day. (3) The workflow passes `RESEND_API_KEY`. `docs/job-alerts-launch-checklist.md` has the Resend steps: a sending subdomain, SPF/DKIM in Cloudflare with DNS only, DMARC, and a sending-only API key. It also gives the free plan's rough capacity (about 100 daily or 600 weekly subscribers). (4) `SiteStatsStrip` uses the light theme tokens: a green-tinted light panel like the listing headers, dark text, and the darker brand green for the big number, since lime on light fails contrast. **Checked:** 5 new tests (Resend request body and headers, 429 retry then limit, retry success, refusals without the body, the switch with either sender). Full suite (1,520), typecheck and lint pass. On the local database, 3 due alerts with a provider that refuses after the first email: the run sent 1 (the never-sent alert) and left 2; the next run sent those 2, oldest first. The band was screenshotted at 1440 and 390 px (light, readable, no horizontal scroll). **Not checked:** a real send through Resend (it needs the owner's account and domain).

- Company logos: nine companies' wrong or missing websites corrected after every sync, and the seed script no longer guesses websites (**SEO: no markup change; real logos instead of a letter or another company's logo on /companies, /remote/companies and those companies' pages; JobPosting `hiringOrganization.logo` follows the corrected website**). **Found** (2026-11-10): every tile on `/companies` (9 pages, 402 tiles) and `/remote/companies` (137) was checked against logo.dev with `fallback=404`. Of 409 distinct lookups, 7 had no logo: Grafana Labs (`grafana-labs.com`) and Ashby (`ashby.com`), whose websites `scripts/seed.ts` had guessed as `https://<slug>.com`, and TurnerTownsend, clearstreet, Empleora and ZoomInfo Technologies LLC, which have no website and whose names logo.dev cannot match. A visual check of the 82 tiles whose domain is `<slug>.com` found three more guesses on someone else's domain: `kong.com` (not Kong Inc.), `spring-health.com` (not Spring Health), `carrot.com` (not Carrot Fertility). A stale Pure Storage tile (`pure-storage.com`) on `/remote/companies` had already been fixed by today's sync. **What:** `lib/companies/websiteCorrections.ts` lists the nine slugs with their real domain, each checked against the site's homepage title; step 7 of `scripts/remediate-data-quality.ts` (run after every sync) sets the website and drops a stored logo.dev logo for another domain, using the same rule as the direct-employer sync (`websiteUpdatesFor`, moved out of `companyUpdatesFor`). `scripts/seed.ts` leaves website and logo empty instead of guessing. **Checked:** 2 new tests; all 216 company tests, typecheck and lint pass; the remediation run on a local database corrected two wrong rows and changed nothing on a second run.

- Workday employers synced one at a time, two job pages at a time (**SEO: no change by itself; lets NVIDIA, Capital One, Cisco, Intel, Salesforce and the other Workday employers get their jobs**). **Found** (2026-11-10): after the crash fix, the manual sync ran all 371 employers (32,371 jobs read, 4,451 created) but every one of the 19 Workday employers failed with HTTP 429, some within 4 seconds. The sync ran three employers at once and the Workday scraper four job-page requests per board, so up to three Workday boards and about twelve requests hit Workday together from one GitHub runner; Workday rate-limits per caller across its tenants. The same boards read without a 429 at this pace from another machine on 2026-11-09. **What:** `scripts/sync-direct-employers.ts` runs Workday boards in a lane of their own, one after another with a 15-second pause, while the other providers keep three workers alongside; `DETAIL_CONCURRENCY` in `lib/scrapers/ats/workday.ts` is 2 (was 4). **Checked:** a dry run on Zoom, Etsy, OpenAI and Linear read all four, the two Workday boards one after the other. Whether this pace is low enough for Workday from GitHub's runners shows in the next daily run.

- MCP server: a salary benchmark, a list of the roles/countries/regions that have jobs, and easier setup (**SEO: `/mcp` gains a "Quickest: let your agent set it up" section and two more tool descriptions, and its meta description now mentions salaries; `/llms.txt` gains an "AI assistants (MCP)" section. No canonical, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemap change.
