Database: no change.**) **Asked** (2026-11-10): build the small items from the freehire `/agents` comparison (browser extension and mobile app skipped). **Added:** (1) `salary_benchmark(role, country?, remote?, salary?)` returns the middle range (25th-75th percentile) and median of live listings with published pay for one role, in the country's currency (US dollars by default), plus, given a salary, the share of those listings it pays more than (the site's own `rankSalary`). The role is matched by name, slug or synonym among roles that have jobs. Fewer than 15 listings gives a note instead of figures (the same floor as the site's pay comparison). Every answer states the method, including that only six-figure jobs are listed, so the figures describe the six-figure market, not every job with that title. (2) `list_filters` returns roles and countries with live job counts (cached 10 minutes) and the remote regions. `search_jobs` gains `role` (a slug from it) and `remote_region`. (3) `/mcp` has a copyable setup prompt for Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Gemini CLI (the agent adds the server and runs one test search), and `/llms.txt` lists the server and its four tools. **Checked:** 2 new unit tests (quartiles, role matching). End to end with the official MCP SDK client against the dev server, using 21 local Data Engineer listings: median $190k, middle range $165k-$215k, and a $200k offer "pays more than 60%", all matching a hand calculation (12.5 of 21). A role with 1 listing gave the note; an unknown role, a missing role and a bad region gave clear errors; `search_jobs` by role slug paged correctly. The copy button put the prompt on the clipboard at 390 px with no horizontal scroll. Full suite (1,512), typecheck and lint pass. **Not checked:** how many roles have 15 or more listings in production (no production database here).

- Email job alerts: the address is checked properly, likely typos are caught before sending, each signup records the page it came from, and an admin page shows the subscribers (**SEO: no change**; the signup form gains a "Did you mean …?" line when shown. **Database:** one nullable column, `JobAlert.source`, added to the alerts migration `20261110180000_add_job_alerts`. That migration is not applied anywhere yet, so it was edited in place; applied fresh, it matches the schema.) **Asked** (2026-11-10): make sure job alerts collect the email correctly. **Found** reviewing the signup path (it already stored, confirmed and mailed correctly in local tests): (1) the address check accepted undeliverable domains (`a@-b.com`, `a@b..com`, a local part with `..` or starting with a dot) and refused valid internationalised endings (`.xn--p1ai`); (2) zero-width characters copied with an address were kept, making it undeliverable; (3) nothing caught typos, so `name@gmial.com` or `@acme.con` would be stored, confirmed never, and lost; (4) nothing recorded where signups came from; (5) the owner had no way to see subscribers. **Changed:** (1, 2) `lib/alerts/email.ts`: `normalizeEmail` strips invisible characters, lower-cases, and checks the local part (at most 64 characters, no leading, trailing or double dots) and each domain label (letters, digits and inner hyphens; an alphabetic or `xn--` ending). (3) `suggestEmailFix` offers "Did you mean jane@gmail.com?" for an address one or two slips from 15 common mailbox domains, and for mistyped `.com`/`.net`/`.org` endings (`.con`, `.cmo`). It never corrects silently. A first submit with a likely typo stops and shows the suggestion; a second submit sends the address as typed. To avoid false fixes, short domains (`me.com`, `aol.com`) are never fuzzy-matched, and regional endings of providers that have them (`outlook.de`, `yahoo.fr`) are left alone. Only single-domain providers (Gmail, iCloud, Proton) get a fixed ending. (4) the form sends the page path, stored as `JobAlert.source` (path only, no query). (5) **Admin: `/ubaid93/alerts`** (in the admin sidebar) shows unique confirmed subscribers, alerts by status, signups in 7 and 30 days, the confirmation rate, the top 15 signup pages, and the latest 100 signups (address, alert, frequency, status, page, dates). `/api/ubaid93/alerts/export` downloads active subscribers as CSV; it is admin only, and values starting with `= + - @` are neutralised against spreadsheet formula injection. **Found and fixed while testing:** clicking "Get alerts" right after typing a typo submitted it without the suggestion. The click blurs the field first, which showed the suggestion, and the form counted that as already seen; now only a submit counts. **Checked:** 8 new tests (24 in the alerts suite): 17 bad and 5 good addresses, invisible characters, 9 typo fixes and 10 addresses left alone (regional, short and real company domains), source paths, CSV escaping. Full suite (1,510), typecheck and lint pass. End to end in a real browser on the dev server with a local mail server: `Jane.Doe@gmial.com` showed the suggestion; taking it stored `jane.doe@gmail.com` with source `/jobs/software-engineer` and that page's filters. `ops@acme.con` was stopped once, then sent as typed on the second submit. The admin page showed the right counts, sources and rows; the CSV export gave 401 without the admin login and the confirmed subscriber with it.

- Direct employers' checked domain replaces a website on another domain (**SEO: no markup change; real logos for those companies instead of a letter**). **Found** (2026-11-10) checking logos after the daily sync: Scale AI and Relativity Space still drew letters. Their stored websites were guessed from their slugs (`scale-ai.com`, `relativity-space.com`); the sync only filled a missing website, so after it dropped their wrong stored logos the logo was looked up from the wrong website instead. **What:** `companyUpdatesFor` sets the website to the employer's checked domain whenever the stored one is on a different domain (a page on the same domain is kept). 1 new test.

- Scrapers import `cheerio/slim`, so the daily employer sync no longer crashes Node's fetch (**SEO: no change by itself; lets the direct-employer sync finish, so the employers added in #73 and #75 get their jobs**). **Found** (2026-11-10): the daily sync's "Sync direct employers" step died after 98 of 371 employers with `AssertionError: assert(!this.paused)` in `node_modules/undici/lib/dispatcher/client-h1.js`, right after Capital One's Workday board answered 429. Importing cheerio's main entry loads undici 7.16 (a cheerio dependency), which installs its own `Agent` as the process-wide fetch dispatcher (`Symbol.for('undici.globalDispatcher.1')`); Node's built-in fetch then ran on that other undici version, and a connection closing during the 429 retries tripped its internal assertion, an uncaught exception that ended the process. **What:** the 18 scrapers and 5 scripts that import `cheerio` import `cheerio/slim` (same `load`, `Cheerio`, `CheerioAPI`; no undici, parses with htmlparser2 instead of parse5). **Checked:** importing `cheerio` sets the global dispatcher and `cheerio/slim` does not (Node, locally); a new test loads the scrapers and the employer-sync chain and finds no dispatcher installed (it fails on the old imports); live pages from Built In, RemoteLeaf, 4dayweek and Ashby parse to the same links, headings, scripts and JSON-LD with both parsers (page text identical except 0.2% on Built In); all 1,485 tests pass.

- Email job alerts: save the search you are looking at and get its new jobs weekly or daily. **Off until switched on**; see `docs/job-alerts-launch-checklist.md` (**SEO:** listing pages (`/jobs`, role, salary, country, `/remote`) and the footer gain a signup form while alerts are on, and nothing changes while they are off. The new `/alerts/<token>` pages are `noindex, nofollow` with `no-referrer`, linked only from emails and absent from sitemaps. No change to titles, canonicals, hreflang or JSON-LD. **Database:** two new tables, `JobAlert` and `JobAlertSent` (migration `20261110180000_add_job_alerts`); no existing table changes. Apply it with the "database migrate" workflow before switching alerts on.) **Asked** (2026-11-10): build job alert emails, taking from `Ubaidofficial/asap-jobs-automation` and going further. **Found there:** its matcher (`match_and_email.py`, deleted from the repo in Nov 2025, read from git history) kept subscribers in Google Sheets and **never sent anything** (its send function only printed "[DRY RUN]"). The ideas worth keeping were: daily, twice-weekly or weekly frequency with a "due" check; a SentJobs log so a job is never sent twice; cross-source dedupe by title + company; at most 3 jobs per company; and a personalised subject line. **Built**, keeping those (daily/weekly; at most 2 per company): (1) an alert is the listing page's own job query (`lib/alerts/filters.ts` whitelists and checks it), and the digest runs it through the same `buildWhere` and keyword plan as the site, so an email holds exactly the jobs that page lists. ASAP Jobs' separate role and location rules are not needed. (2) Double opt-in: only a confirmation email until the alert's page is used. Confirm, change frequency and unsubscribe are buttons (POST), never GET links, because mail scanners open every link. (3) Every digest carries RFC 8058 one-click unsubscribe headers (Gmail/Yahoo bulk-sender rules), a visible unsubscribe link, the reason it was sent, and a postal address (CAN-SPAM); the switch `alertsEnabled()` stays off until `ALERTS_ENABLED`, `EMAIL_SERVER`, `EMAIL_FROM` and `ALERTS_POSTAL_ADDRESS` are all set. (4) Abuse limits: 5 signups per visitor per 10 minutes, 3 confirmations per address per day, 10 alerts per address, a honeypot field, and the same answer whether or not an address already has alerts. Alert names in emails are built on the server from the checked filters, and keywords may not contain links or addresses; all employer text in emails is HTML-escaped and subjects are kept to one line. (5) A daily workflow (`.github/workflows/job-alerts.yml`, 08:43 UTC) runs `scripts/send-job-alerts.ts`. A manual run is a dry run by default: it prints the counts and sends and records nothing. Emails are not uploaded as an artifact, because they contain addresses and private links. `--dry-run` with `ALERTS_DRY_RUN_DIR` writes previews locally. An alert with no new jobs gets no email. **Checked:** 16 new unit tests (filters and keyword checks, labels, due timing, job picking, HTML escaping, header and subject injection, one-click headers, URLs); full suite (1,500 tests), typecheck and lint pass. Also checked end to end on a local Postgres with a local SMTP server and the dev server: a real browser signup on `/jobs/software-engineer` (message shown, invalid address refused), the confirmation email received, confirmed through the page (button), a second alert through the API, and the honeypot sending nothing. Digest run results: a dry run sent nothing and recorded nothing; the real run sent 2 digests with both List-Unsubscribe headers on the wire and the matching jobs logged; an immediate rerun found nothing due; a week later, nothing new (no repeat). A keyword alert with a typo ("data enginer") matched "Principal Data Engineer" as the site does. One-click unsubscribe POST returned 200 and the next run skipped that alert; GET on the same URL went to the alert's page; a bad token gave 404 and a bad action 400; "Subscribe again" reactivated it. Rendered: the form at 1280 and 390 px with no horizontal scroll, the digest email, and the alert page on a phone. `listingPagination.test.ts` now stubs the alert box, as it already stubs the other components next to `Pagination`. **Not checked:** sending through a real email provider and delivery to Gmail or Outlook; that needs the owner's sender and DNS.

- Public MCP server: assistants (Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI) can search our jobs, plus a `/mcp` page explaining how to connect (**SEO: one new indexable page, `/mcp`, with an absolute canonical and no hreflang, linked from the footer's Resources column; it is not added to any sitemap. No change to existing pages' titles, canonicals, robots, JSON-LD or sitemaps. Database: no change.**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): freehire.me offers an MCP server and the owner considers it a competitor; we should have one too. **Found:** freehire's MCP (`https://freehire.me/api/v1/mcp`, per their `/mcp` page) is public for search, with account tools behind OAuth. Ours had none. **Added:** `POST /api/mcp`, MCP over streamable HTTP, stateless, JSON responses only (GET and DELETE answer 405, notifications 202). It supports protocol versions 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26 and 2024-11-05 and adds no dependency (`lib/mcp/protocol.ts`, written to the spec). It has two read-only tools, and no account or cookie is read (`lib/mcp/tools.ts`): `search_jobs` (free-text query read the same way as the site's `/search`, with optional country, remote, minimum salary, sort and page; 10 jobs per page) and `get_job` (one open job's details, last-confirmed-open date, description clipped to 6,000 characters, and the employer's apply link). Job links carry `utm_source=mcp&utm_medium=ai_assistant`, so visits from assistants show up in analytics; job pages' canonicals are unaffected. Limit: 60 requests a minute per address; bodies over 64 KB are refused. Internal errors are never shown to the caller. Account tools (save, track, alerts) are not included, because accounts are off in production. **freehire-sourced jobs are included**, as on the site. freehire's Terms of Service (updated 2026-10-28) forbid redistributing their postings "as ... an API or feed whose purpose is to hand the postings on to other services". The owner reports that freehire's developer gave permission (2026-11-10); keep a written record of it. **Checked:** 13 new unit tests; typecheck and lint. Also checked end to end with the official MCP TypeScript SDK client (1.32.2) against a local server and seeded database: connect and initialize, list the tools, search (with and without filters, plus a bad argument), get one job, and a closed or unknown job. HTTP checks: 405, 202, 400 on bad JSON, and 429 after 60 calls. The `/mcp` page renders at 1280 and 390 px without horizontal scroll. **Not checked:** connecting from claude.ai, ChatGPT or Cursor themselves (that needs the deployed URL).

- Scrapers: a failed response's body is discarded before the error, so a closed connection can no longer crash the sync (**SEO: no change**). **Found** (2026-11-10): the freehire daily sync's "Sync direct employers" step died after 14 minutes with `AssertionError: assert(!this.paused)` in `undici/lib/dispatcher/client-h1.js` (`Parser.finish` from `onHttpSocketEnd`), right after Workday answered Capital One and Salesforce with HTTP 429. `fetchWithBackoff` already discards bodies between retries, but the last 429 is returned and `lib/scrapers/ats/workday.ts` threw on `!res.ok` without reading or discarding the body; a paused, unread response whose server then closes the socket makes undici crash the process. `undici` (via cheerio) moved from 7.16.0 to 7.30.2 in the security upgrade this morning (#83) and the previous run passed; whether the newer version makes this more likely was not established. **Changed:** the 16 places in `lib/` that threw or returned on a failed response without touching its body (Workday, Ashby, freehire, the worldwide boards, 4dayweek, Built In, Wellfound, Dice, Otta, We Work Remotely, Real Work From Anywhere, careers discovery, apply-URL discovery, job page details) cancel the body first. One-off scripts in `scripts/` are unchanged. **Checked:** 1 new test (a 429's body is cancelled before the error; fails on the old code); typecheck, lint, full test suite. The next daily sync will show whether the step completes.

- JobPosting `hiringOrganization.logo`: the logo the site shows, from the company's stored logo or website domain (**SEO: JobPosting JSON-LD on job pages gains `hiringOrganization.logo` for companies with a known website or stored logo; no other change**). **Found** (2026-11-10) researching Google for Jobs: Google recommends `hiringOrganization.logo` (third-party job sites may supply it); a live job page had only the company name, because the markup used only a stored `logoUrl`, which most companies lack. **Changed:** the markup uses `buildLogoUrl` (the logo the site shows), with no name lookup (a name can match another company, as the logo audit found), and same-origin `/logo/...` URLs made absolute. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite; 1 new test (a website gives `/logo/domain/<domain>` and `sameAs`; no website and no stored logo gives no logo).

- JobPosting `validThrough`: only the source's own expiry date, never an estimate (**SEO: JobPosting JSON-LD on job pages changes: `validThrough` is now left out unless the source gave an expiry date that is still in the future. No other markup, title, canonical, robots, hreflang or sitemap change.**). **Found** (2026-11-10) researching Google for Jobs: Google's JobPosting documentation says "If a job posting never expires, or you do not know when the job will expire, do not include this property." Our JSON-LD always had one: the stored `validThrough` (the source's date, else the ingest's estimate of posting date + 45 days), and for older jobs today + 45 days, which moved forward on every render; a live job page posted 2026-10-27 said 2026-12-11. The schema check (`validateJobPostingEligibility`) also treated `validThrough` as required, which Google does not. **Changed:** `buildValidThrough` publishes `expiresAt` (only ever set from the source) while it is in the future, else nothing; the eligibility check no longer requires it and flags one in the past. Expired jobs are still taken out by the 410 response and Indexing API URL_DELETED. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite; tests updated (no source date: no `validThrough`) and 1 added (future source date published, past or missing left out, the stored estimate never used).

- Sitemaps: the jobs shard and the hubs sitemap answer from memory instead of taking ~13 s (**SEO: same URLs and lastmod values; only how fast they are served. Every job URL Google learns about is in `/sitemap-jobs/1`, and Search Console's reader timed out on it.**). **Found** (2026-11-10, while researching job-board SEO): SEO Console's last read of `/sitemap-jobs/1` (09:22 UTC) failed with "Timed out after 10 seconds"; timed from here it took 12.7-13.7 s on every request (766 KB, 5,000 URLs), `/sitemap-hubs.xml` 13.9 s, every other sitemap under 1.2 s. Search Console's sample: 2 of 127 inspected URLs indexed, 119 "URL is unknown to Google". The shard reads up to 7,500 jobs with their descriptions for the quality gate on every request (`revalidate` does not cache a route with a dynamic segment); the hubs sitemap runs every evergreen page's indexability check. **Changed:** both keep their result in memory for 30 minutes (`memoAsync`, which serves the previous result at once and rebuilds in the background for up to an hour past that), the shard keyed by page and by the per-shard cap. The first request after a deploy still builds once. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite; 1 new test (a second request within the window does not query the database); existing sitemap tests clear the cache between cases.

- Job rows: the pay comparison uses the job's real role (**SEO: no change**). **Found** (2026-11-10) checking #88 live: rows said "Pays more than 80% of Other jobs", because most jobs are stored with the role `other`. **Changed:** the row resolves the role the way the job page does (`resolveJobRoleSlug`: the stored role, else the role read from the title when its words appear together in it) and shows no comparison when there is none. **Checked:** "Staff Software Engineer, Ads" resolves to software-engineer, "Don't See Your Role?" to none; typecheck, lint, tests.

- Feedback: screenshots are kept, the admin sidebar shows unread feedback on every page, and the public endpoint is rate-limited (**SEO: no change. Database: one additive, nullable column, `Feedback.screenshot` (migration `20261110140000_add_feedback_screenshot`); apply it with the "database migrate" workflow. The code works before it is applied: feedback is saved without the column and the screenshot write fails quietly, so nothing is lost but the image.**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): are we recording feedback, and make sure new feedback is shown every time; the owner chose to keep it in the admin pages rather than email, Slack or Discord. **Found:** feedback was stored (type, message, page, browser) and listed at `/ubaid93/feedback`, with a "New Feedback" count on the admin dashboard; the pasted screenshot was thrown away (only `hasScreenshot` was stored); `FEEDBACK_WEBHOOK_URL` is not set on Railway, so nothing was sent anywhere; and `POST /api/feedback` had no limit. **Changed:** (1) the widget shrinks a pasted screenshot to a JPEG at most 1600 px wide before sending; the endpoint accepts only an image data URL (JPEG, PNG or WebP, up to about 2 MB) and stores it in a second write; (2) the admin list loads without image data, and each item with a screenshot shows it from an admin-only route (`/api/ubaid93/feedback/:id/screenshot`; older feedback says the image was not stored); (3) a red count of new feedback on "Feedback" in the admin sidebar, on every admin page; (4) 6 messages per 10 minutes per visitor (Cloudflare's visitor address when present), and type, page URL and browser string capped in length. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite; 4 new tests (screenshot saved in a second write, feedback kept when that write fails, a non-image data URL ignored, the 7th message in 10 minutes refused). Not checked here: the admin pages against the live database.

- Job rows: key facts under the summary (**SEO: listing pages' HTML gains each job's facts inside the row's collapsed panel; no change to titles, canonicals, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemaps**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): enrich the short summaries; research what to add first. **Researched:** remote100k's job page shows posting age, salary, the remote country restriction, employment type, seniority and the company's HQ and job count; We Work Remotely's shows posted date, apply-before date, job type, category and region (the owner's screenshot). On 105 of our live postings: years of experience were stated in 66 (63%), visa sponsorship in none (the matches were "executive sponsors" and "company-sponsored events"), security clearance in 2, travel in 2, reporting line in 4, team size in 3, so only experience is read from the text. **Added**, each shown only when known: "Pays more than N% of <role> jobs" (where the pay sits among live listings for the same role and currency, the job page's existing measure, shown in the row when 50% or more), years of experience ("5+ years", "3–5 years"; the word "experience" must follow, so "founded 10 years ago" and "a 4-year degree" do not count), degree ("Degree required" / "Degree or equivalent experience", the no-degree page's classifier; nothing when the posting does not say), visa sponsorship (the existing `visaSponsorship` flag, now in the listing query), contract / part-time / freelance (from the employment type), and where Apply goes ("Apply on company site" or the board's name, as on the job page). Peer salaries are cached 10 minutes per role, so a page adds at most one query per role it shows. **Checked:** experience found in 67 of 105 postings, with values read against the text by hand on a sample; typecheck, lint, full test suite (2 new tests). Not checked here: rows rendered against the live database (none in this session).

- Homepage "Pay floor by market": the job count sits under the market name beside the copy (**SEO: no change; CSS only**). **Found** (2026-11-10) checking #86 live: with the new flags, the United States and United Kingdom rows wrapped "jobs" onto a second line at 1360-1500 px while the others stayed on one. **Changed:** from 1360 px (where the panel sits beside the copy and its column is narrow) every row puts the count on its own line under the market name; below that the panel is full width and unchanged. **Checked** on the live homepage with the rule applied, at 1633, 1440, 1360, 1280 and 1024 px: every row consistent, nothing overlapping (keeping the rows on one line with `nowrap` was tried first and made the count run into the pay floor at 1440 px).

- Homepage "Added in the last 24 hours" strip: hand-picked employers first, recruitment agencies left out (**SEO: no markup, metadata, canonical, robots or JSON-LD change; the strip's links change (fewer `/company/<slug>` links to agency pages), and the strip hides itself when fewer than 4 companies qualify, which is the case on today's data**). **Found** checking the live homepage after the strip shipped: its top entries were recruitment agencies, TROVA Personal- und Managementberatung (+29), rocket match / notificAI GmbH (+9) and Jobot (+4), ahead of real employers, the same thing the logo wall was changed to avoid (#70). **What:** `shapeNewRolesCompanies` (`lib/jobs/newRolesByCompany.ts`) lists the employers in `TOP_COMPANY_SLUGS` first (by new jobs), then the rest by new jobs, and drops a company outside that list when `looksLikeAgency` says so: its name has staffing, recruit-, headhunt-, manpower, talent, "executive search" or a German agency form of "Personal" (Personalis and Personal Capital stay), or its slug is in a short known list (Jobot and rocket match, which have no such word). This is a keyword guess, not a classification: it will miss some agencies and may drop a real company named with one of those words; add slugs to `KNOWN_AGENCY_SLUGS` as they turn up. **Checked** with the six companies on the live strip on 2026-11-09 (TROVA +29, rocket match +9, First Citizens +7, Jobot +4, General Dynamics IT +2, Cisco +1): the strip would list First Citizens, General Dynamics IT and Cisco, three companies, below the 4 it needs, so it would be hidden until more real employers add jobs in a day. If you want it visible more often, a wider window (the last 7 days) is the obvious lever; it was not changed. 15 new tests.

- Flags and region marks wherever the site lists countries or remote regions (**SEO: no change to titles, canonicals, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemaps; flags are decorative images with empty alt text next to the country name, so link text is unchanged**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): add country flags and other emojis across the site. **Found:** flags (`CountryFlag`, images in `public/flags`, not emoji: Windows shows flag emoji as two letters) were on job rows, cards, the job page, the `/jobs` country list, the homepage Top Locations and the salary guides, but not on the homepage "US · UK · EU · Canada · Australia" line, "Pay floor by market", the `/remote` region chips, the sidebar's Remote region list, the company page's Top locations or the footer's Remote Jobs and By Location columns; there was no flag for Europe. **Changed:** each remote region has a `mark` in `lib/jobs/remoteRegions.ts` (a flag code, or 🌍 for Anywhere in the world, Middle East and EMEA, 🌏 for Asia-Pacific), shown by a new `RegionMark`; `public/flags/eu.svg` from the same package and version as the other flags (country-flag-icons 1.5.19, MIT; `CountryFlag` accepts `EU`, job data's country parsing does not); flags added to all the places above, and 🌍 on the homepage Location list's worldwide option (a native list, which cannot show images). The flag guard test allows the one non-country image. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, tests; a production build run locally, footer screenshotted at 1280 px (all 14 flags and the globe load and line up). Not checked here: the homepage, `/remote` and company pages, which need the database.

- Job rows and job pages: a short summary of the role and the benefits the posting names, for every job (**SEO: listing pages' HTML gains each job's summary and benefit labels inside the row's collapsed panel; job pages without AI data gain a "Benefits" list of the benefits their own description states. No change to titles, canonicals, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemaps.**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): the short description is gone from the job rows; it should summarise the job and list all its benefits. **Found:** rows showed only the AI-written snippet, and the listing query does not load descriptions; AI enrichment is paused (`AI_ENRICHMENT_PAUSED` in the daily workflow), and `/api/stats/enrichment` reported 63 of 14,969 live jobs enriched (0.4%). On live `/remote`, 0 of 12 rows had a summary. Nothing writes `benefitsJson`, so benefits showed nowhere either. **Changed:** `lib/jobs/jobSummary.ts` reads the description: the role section when the posting has one ("About the role", "The opportunity", "What you'll do"…), otherwise the first sentences that are about the job rather than the company (at most 280 characters); and the benefits it names (equity, bonus, commission, health insurance, 401(k), pension, unlimited / paid time off, parental leave, remote stipend, learning budget, wellness, relocation, meals, visa sponsorship), ignoring "diversity, equity and inclusion", "pay equity" and "bonus points if…". `queryJobs` adds both to each job on the page with one extra query for those jobs' descriptions (the descriptions are not sent to the browser); an AI snippet still wins when there is one. Rows show the summary and a Benefits line of chips; the job page's Benefits list falls back to the same labels and shows up to 10. **Checked:** on 45 live postings (Modal, Baseten, Pure Storage, Abridge, LlamaIndex, Midjourney…) every one got a summary; 36 had benefits, and the 9 without were read by hand and name none (Anyscale's "salary and equity, plus health/dental/vision" is found). 9 new tests. Not checked: the rows rendered against the live database (none in this session).

- Role pages (`/jobs/<role>`) and salary pages (`/jobs/100k-plus` … `/jobs/400k-plus`): "🌍 Anywhere in the world" in the Location filter, and the Remote region filter, as on `/jobs` (**SEO: no change to indexable pages; a URL with `?remoteRegion=` is a filtered state and already `noindex, follow` with the clean canonical, through `hasNonPaginationQueryParams`**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): where is worldwide / anywhere in the Location filter (screenshot of `/jobs/software-engineer`). **Found:** the sidebar shows the row only when the page passes remote-region counts, and only `/jobs` did; role and salary pages also ignored `?remoteRegion=`, so the filter could not have worked there. **Changed:** both templates read `remoteRegion`, pass it to the job query and count each region for the sidebar (cached 5 minutes with the other counts); the region list moves from `app/jobs/page.tsx` to `LISTING_REMOTE_REGIONS` in `lib/jobs/remoteRegions.ts`, shared by all three. **Checked:** typecheck, lint, full test suite. Not checked against the live database here (no database in this session).

- Homepage: less empty space between the floating header and the hero on desktop (**SEO: no markup change; one CSS padding value**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): reduce the white area at the top so 100% zoom looks like 90%. **Found:** the gap was the header's 12 px bottom padding plus the page's 40 px top padding (`pt-10`). **Changed:** from 1024 px up the homepage's top padding is 16 px (`lg:pt-4`); phones and tablets unchanged. **Checked** on the live homepage with the new padding applied: the gap from the header capsule to the hero goes from 53 px to 29 px at 1470 px (a 13" MacBook Air at 100%) and 1633 px (90%).

- Security: Next.js 16.0.10 → 16.4.6 and other dependency fixes from `npm audit`; admin login rate-limited; admin sessions expire (**SEO: no markup, title, canonical, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemap change**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): run the unit and security tests, check that others cannot scrape or attack the site, and say what happens at 100k visitors at once. **Ran:** Jest (142 suites, 1,422 tests pass), `tsc --noEmit` and ESLint clean, `npm audit --omit=dev`: 17 vulnerabilities (1 critical, 14 high, 2 moderate), among them Next.js advisories for unauthenticated remote code execution in the image optimizer and several middleware/proxy bypasses and DoS issues fixed after 16.0.10. **Changed:** (1) `npm audit fix` within the existing semver ranges (lockfile only; `package.json` unchanged): next 16.4.6, axios 1.21.1, zod 4.6.7, postcss 8.5.23 and transitive packages. 5 remain (4 high, 1 moderate), all with no published fix: nodemailer (used only by the email sign-in, which is off unless `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` is set; it is not set on Railway) through next-auth/@auth, and a build-time browserslist helper. (2) `/api/ubaid93/login` had no limit on password guesses: now 5 attempts per IP and 50 across all IPs per 15 minutes (429 with `Retry-After`), in memory like the sign-in limiter (`lib/admin/loginRateLimit.ts`); someone hammering the endpoint can keep the admin out for up to 15 minutes. (3) Admin session tokens carried their issue time but it was never checked, so a copied cookie worked forever; `verifySessionToken` now rejects tokens older than the 7-day cookie lifetime. **Checked:** tests, typecheck, lint and a production build pass on the new versions; on the built server, login attempts 1-5 reach the handler, the 6th gets 429 with `Retry-After: 900`, another IP is unaffected. **Not changed, reported:** the public `/api/jobs` JSON API has no limit or cache (every job, 24 per page, by `?page=`), `/api/stats/enrichment` and `/api/log-error`/`/api/feedback` are open and uncached, Cloudflare does not cache HTML (`cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC`), production runs one replica, and `ADMIN_JWT_SECRET` is not set (the session key is derived from `DATABASE_URL`). 5 new tests (2 fail on the old code).

- Homepage polish, second pass: salary tier cards, role links, section headings, footer feed buttons (**SEO: no change to titles, H1s, canonicals, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD, sitemaps or links; the salary tier cards' text changes from "$100k–$199k Jobs" to "$100k–$199k" plus a share line**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): do the next steps proposed after PR #82. **Changed:** (1) Browse by Salary Tier: the faint gradient cards with large empty space now show the range, the job count large, a bar with that tier's share of US-dollar listings ("43% of US-dollar listings") and a coloured top edge per tier (greens for $100k and $200k, ambers for $300k and $400k). (2) "Explore Six Figure Jobs by Role": a section heading like the others (it was small grey text) and chips with a hover lift. (3) Top Locations, Browse by Salary Tier and Explore Premium Roles headings start at the left edge like every other homepage section (three were centred). (4) Footer "All jobs feed" / "Remote jobs feed" buttons stack at full width on phones up to 480 px ("Remote jobs feed" wrapped onto two lines). **Checked** locally with screenshots at 1470 and 390 px.

- Homepage Location list offers "Anywhere in the world", picking a country on the homepage works again, and the homepage gets a tighter, more finished look (**SEO: no change to titles, H1s, canonicals, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemaps. The FAQ heading changes from "FAQs about Six Figure Jobs" to "Questions people ask" and its answers sit in `<details>`, still in the HTML. Top Locations gains a link to `/remote?remoteRegion=global` (noindex, as all query URLs there) and drops a country card while that country has no jobs. `/jobs?country=<remote region>` (no such links existed before) redirects to `/jobs?remoteRegion=<region>`, also noindex.**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): there is no global / anywhere-in-the-world choice in Location, and check the whole homepage so it looks like a modern, premium job board. **Found:** (1) the worldwide choice lived in a separate "Remote region" select next to Location; (2) the homepage Location list sent country slugs (`country=united-states`), which `/jobs` ignores (it reads ISO codes), so choosing a country on the homepage changed nothing; (3) "New jobs, live" kept an empty 56 px slot for its "Show N new jobs" button, and its twelve short rows used about a third of a full-width card; (4) Top Locations showed "Netherlands 0 jobs" and "Most in: —"; (5) the FAQ was five questions and answers as one block of small text. The hydration warning seen on the homepage in development does not occur in a production build (checked with `next build` + `next start`, three loads, no errors; the logo 404s there come from the local test database, the live `/logo/name/...` URLs redirect). **Changed:** (1) one Location list: a Remote group ("Anywhere in the world (remote)", then Remote · US, Canada, ...) above the countries, which now submit ISO codes; "Remote Only" is covered by that group; `/jobs` moves a region given as `country` to `remoteRegion`, so the sidebar shows it picked. (2) `/jobs` sidebar: "Anywhere in the world" with its count at the top of Location (the same filter as Remote region > Anywhere in the world). (3) Live feed: the button sits beside "All new jobs" in a row that is always there (still no layout shift when it appears); two columns from 1024 px. (4) Top Locations: countries with jobs only, an "Anywhere in the world" card (worldwide remote count, after the US), six cards at most; the "Most in" line only when a city is known. (5) FAQ as an accordion under a section heading. **Checked** locally: homepage Location "Anywhere in the world" lands on `/jobs?minSalary=100000&remoteRegion=global` with the sidebar row picked (31 jobs), "Canada" on `/jobs?country=CA&...` (26 of 313 jobs), "Remote · Canada" on `remoteRegion=canada`; screenshots at 1470 and 390 px, no horizontal scroll.

- Homepage numbers band: compact, dark, right after "Top Companies Hiring Now" (**SEO: on the indexable homepage the "6figjobs today" label becomes an H2 (it was a paragraph) and the section moves up the page; its numbers and note are unchanged except that the typical-salary chip and the catalogue's company count are gone. Title, H1, canonical, robots and JSON-LD unchanged.**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): the "We screen / You see" section should be more optimized and overhauled, and sit before or after Top Companies Hiring Now. **Was:** two tall panels joined by an arrow near the foot of the page (about 380 px at 1470 px wide), seven chips, the typical salary repeating the hero's. **Now:** one dark band under the companies strip: "We screen 4,877,712 open jobs from N sources, N hiring platforms and N careers sites" -> a line with "about 1 in N makes the list" -> "You see N $100k+ jobs with published pay" in lime, then three facts (companies hiring, new this week, countries) in a row, and the same one-line note. 210 px tall at 1470 px; two rows below 1100 px; stacked on phones (checked at 1470, 1024 and 390 px, no horizontal scroll). Still renders the final numbers on the server (AnimatedNumber), still leaves out a number whose query failed.

- Worldwide-remote jobs from We Work Remotely and Himalayas, applying on those boards (**SEO: more job pages through the normal ingest; no template, URL or robots change**). **Asked** (2026-11-10): add We Work Remotely's and Himalayas' anywhere-in-the-world jobs; the owner chose "Apply on <board>" for them, since neither board gives the employer's link. **Found:** freehire carries 54 WWR and 3,318 Himalayas jobs, none tagged worldwide at $100k+. WWR's pages are behind a bot check but its RSS feeds are not (main feed plus 11 category feeds: 420 items labelled "Anywhere in the World", 199 with a yearly salary in the text). Himalayas' search API has `worldwide=true` (812 jobs; pay given as annual, monthly or hourly). WWR's "Anywhere in the World" label is mostly wrong at this pay: its descriptions open with "Headquarters: ...", which holds the job's location ("Remote - USA", "San Francisco, CA or Remote, US", "Remote - Canada" for Lattice), and others say "only considering candidates located in ... the United States" (Anthropic, Bitovi, Knock, Cambridge Information Group). **Built:** `lib/scrapers/worldwideBoards.ts` reads both through the board ingest, run by a new step in the freehire daily workflow (`npm run sync:worldwide-boards`, `continue-on-error`). A job counts as worldwide (location "Remote - Worldwide") only when the location line, the text and the title name no country, region or time zone; otherwise it is imported as a US remote job ("Remote - United States") or plain "Remote", never as worldwide. Salary: WWR from the text (a `$` range, bonuses and small figures ignored), Himalayas only when yearly. `lib/jobs/applyUrl.ts` keeps a link to the board a job was imported from (`OWN_BOARD_APPLY_HOSTS`: WWR and Himalayas) through ingest, refresh and the data-quality audit and self-heal; any other job's link to those boards is still dropped. **Checked** locally against the live feeds: WWR 159 jobs at $100k+ (11 worldwide, 116 US, 32 other), Himalayas 19 (all worldwide; 15 of the 21 that pass are micro1 roles at $300k-$1.5M, kept as asked); "Anywhere in the world" went from 5 to 34 on the local data; job pages say "Apply on We Work Remotely" / "Apply on Himalayas". 18 new tests.

- freehire sync: a daily worldwide-remote pass, so "Anywhere in the world" lists the worldwide jobs freehire has (**SEO: more job pages through the normal ingest; no template, URL or robots change**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): why does "Anywhere in the world" have only 6 jobs; aren't most remote jobs global? **Checked live** (all 3,594 remote jobs on the site, through `/api/jobs`): most six-figure remote jobs are tied to a country. 2,132 are US, 332 Canada (some of them the "City, CA" mix-up fixed earlier, corrected as jobs are seen again), 109 UK, Europe and elsewhere, 865 name a place without a stored country (mostly US or Canadian: "Remote (US)", "San Francisco HQ", "Toronto", "Canada - Remote (ON, AB, BC, or NS Only)"), 149 say only "Remote", and 7 say worldwide or global (6 pass; "Anywhere in the United States" correctly does not). The descriptions of the 149 "Remote"-only jobs add none: 62 name a country or time zone, 63 say nothing, and the rest use "worldwide" about the company ("reaches 170 million people worldwide") or say "work from anywhere in the 50 United States". **But the site was also missing worldwide jobs:** freehire's own `regions=global` facet had 57 jobs at $100k+ whose location says worldwide, and the site had 5 of them; 37 of the other 52 pass every rule of ours (the rest are stale on freehire). The daily sync reads only 1.5 days (`FREEHIRE_LOOKBACK_DAYS`), so a job was lost for good when a run was missed (2026-11-05 was cancelled) or when freehire found it days after its posting date; and the USD query alone has 63,751 jobs, of which a run can reach the newest 10,000 (back to 2026-11-02). **Changed:** `FREEHIRE_MARKETS` gains four worldwide queries (`regions=global` in USD, EUR, GBP and CAD), each reading 30 days whatever the daily window (`lookbackDays`): about 270 jobs at the pay floor, a few requests a day. A dry run against the live API found 44 publishable USD worldwide jobs and 2 GBP; jobs it brings in that do not say worldwide are listed as usual and do not count as worldwide. Test data in two completeness tests moved past the new 30-day window and off real timers (10 markets x 700 ms waits passed Jest's 5 s limit). 1 new test.

- New indexable page `/jobs/4-day-week`: four-day work week jobs paying $100k+, imported daily from 4dayweek.io's public API (**SEO: one new indexable hub, added to `ALWAYS_INDEXABLE_HUBS`; indexable on page 1 and in the hubs sitemap only while it has at least 5 jobs (`FOUR_DAY_WEEK_MIN_JOBS_TO_INDEX`), so a noindex state is never advertised; page 2+ is noindex, follow with a self canonical. Absolute canonical, no hreflang. BreadcrumbList, ItemList and FAQPage JSON-LD; imported jobs get the usual JobPosting on their own pages. Linked from the homepage's popular searches and footer links, the site footer and /jobs/no-degree. No database migration.**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): a 4-day work week page that is indexed; the owner chose to import 4dayweek.io's jobs after the numbers below. **Found:** freehire carries about 3,200 4dayweek.io listings but without their schedule, and 4dayweek.io lists compressed weeks (40 hours in four days), 9/80s and the like as well: of its 35,880 live listings 643 are labelled `4_day_week` (reduced hours at full pay), and of freehire's ~130 with a salary only 3 were, 1 at our salary floor. The old `lib/scrapers/fourdayweek.ts` read the HTML list page, whose markup had changed, and found no jobs (and its workflow has been off since 2026-10-08). **Built:** (1) `lib/jobs/fourDayWeek.ts`: reads the labelled listings from `https://4dayweek.io/api/v2/jobs?schedule=4_day_week` (free, no key, 60 requests a minute; 7 requests for all of them), drops any of 40 hours or more (4 of 643 were), and matches our jobs to them by posting URL or by company and title (the same job from the employer's own careers system); the list is cached 6 hours and the last good one is kept if 4dayweek.io is down. No column marks a job, so no migration and the label is always current. (2) The scraper now imports those listings through the normal board ingest (pay given in cents, Markdown descriptions to HTML, location text our parser reads), run by a new step in the freehire daily workflow (`npm run sync:fourdayweek`; independent of the other steps, `continue-on-error`); a listing that disappears expires with the existing 7-day rule. (3) The page: count, remote count, most common week (32 hours: 443 of 643 listings; most others 34-37), roles with the most jobs, the list (highest pay first, 40 a page), what counts as a four-day week versus a compressed week or 9/80, FAQ, a credit link to 4dayweek.io (their API asks for a link back), related searches. **Checked:** locally the import created 53 jobs, all salary-validated (Head of Sales North America at AWIN $290k-$350k, Kickstarter, Buffer, Signifyd, Ghost, D'Youville University...), the page lists 52 (29 remote), page 1 is `index, follow`, page 2 `noindex, follow`, title 52 characters, one H1, and the hubs sitemap lists it; an imported job's page says "Apply on 4dayweek" and has a complete JobPosting. Until the first daily import runs in production the page has few or no jobs, so it stays noindex and out of the sitemap on its own. 13 new tests.

- Header, homepage hero and job filters: a floating header, a hero without empty bands at wide sizes, an "Anywhere in the world" filter that means it, and region filters that work (**SEO: no change to titles, H1s, canonicals, robots, hreflang, JSON-LD or sitemaps. `/jobs?remoteRegion=…` and `/remote?remoteRegion=global` are noindex (any query parameter makes these pages noindex) and now return different jobs; footer and hero links change from `/remote?region=…` (ignored by the page) to `/remote?remoteRegion=…`.**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): at 70% zoom on a MacBook Air the homepage shows a lot of empty space; make the header slick, make the job filters look fine, and add an anywhere-in-the-world / global filter. **Found** (local run with ~220 seeded jobs, screenshots at 2100 px = 70% of a 13" Air, 1470, 1360, 1280, 1024 and 390 px): the hero's right-hand panel was one narrow column about twice as tall as the copy, which sat centred beside it with ~170 px of empty green above and below; the "Top Companies Hiring Now" strip was capped at 72rem, inset ~190 px from everything around it; the header's search stopped ~90 px short of the content edge. `/jobs` ignored `remoteRegion`, so the homepage "Remote region" dropdown (including "Global") changed nothing, and `/remote` reads `remoteRegion` while the hero's EU link, its Europe pay-floor row and the footer's five remote links sent `region=`, so they all showed every remote job. "Worldwide" meant remote jobs with no stored country: on the live site those 1,021 jobs read "Remote (US)", "San Francisco Bay Area or Remote (U.S.)", "New York City" and the like. **Changed:** (1) Header, 1024 px and up: one floating capsule as wide as the page content, pinned 12 px below the top edge, with the five links as a segmented pill (the current page raised on it), the search at the right with its "/" hint; phones and tablets unchanged. (2) Hero: the panel's two halves (salary snapshot | pay floors and job types) sit side by side; from 1360 px beside the copy, below that under the search at full width; the companies strip is as wide as the rest. (3) Filters: `/jobs` reads `remoteRegion` and its sidebar has a Remote region section with counts (empty regions hidden, the picked one kept; picking one clears a hybrid or on-site choice); the homepage dropdown uses the same regions and labels; "global" is labelled "Anywhere in the world" and needs the posting to say so ("worldwide", "anywhere", "global") with no country and nothing that limits it (a country, a region such as EMEA or North America, or a time zone) (`isWorldwideLocationText`, one rule for the query and the Remote hub count). Sidebar look: Work type as four chips in a 2 x 2 grid, option lists as plain rows with the picked one tinted, the salary slider as a light track filled in brand green with a white thumb (it was near-black with a blue thumb). **Checked:** locally `/jobs?remoteRegion=global` lists exactly the 5 "Remote - Worldwide" jobs and none of the 4 "Remote (US)" or 15 "Remote" ones, the sidebar reads "Anywhere in the world 5 · US 25 · Europe 24", and `/remote?remoteRegion=global` agrees; no horizontal scroll at any width checked. Not checked: production data; the live count of worldwide jobs is unknown until deployed, and the Remote hub hides the chip if it is 0.

- No page names freehire any more (**SEO: the homepage's visible stats note changes and its outbound link to freehire.me is removed; JobPosting JSON-LD `identifier` on every job page changes from `{name: <source>, value: <stored id>}` to `{name: "Six Figure Jobs", value: <the id at the end of the job's URL>}`. Titles, canonicals, robots, hreflang and sitemaps unchanged.**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): don't mention freehire on any page of the site. **Checked live:** 406 pages (the homepage, every hub, salary, company and remote page, and 400 job pages) as text and as HTML. Visible text named it only on the homepage ("Screened jobs from freehire's live catalogue", linked to freehire.me). The JobPosting `identifier` named the source on 9 of 400 job pages (`board:freehire`, and the stored id `board:freehire:…`), and the Jooble feed's `<job id>` carried the same id (339 times, some naming Himalayas too). **Changed:** the note now reads "Screened jobs counted across the sources and hiring platforms we read; listed jobs counted from our own database, refreshed every few minutes."; the JobPosting identifier and the Jooble `<job id>` use the job's public short id (Jooble will see each job's id change once). **Not changed, reported:** list and job pages still send each job's stored `id` and `source` to the browser for their interactive parts, so `board:freehire` stays readable in the page source (not on the page) for jobs found there; stored ids are primary keys used by saved jobs and dedupe. Server code, comments and the scraper keep the name. 3 tests changed or added.

- Technical SEO audit: job country and posting date fixed at the source, JobPosting only on job pages (**SEO: JobPosting JSON-LD on job pages changes for affected jobs: `addressCountry`, `applicantLocationRequirements`, `jobLocationType` (no longer TELECOMMUTE for hybrid jobs) and `datePosted` (Greenhouse jobs now carry the employer's first-published date). Company pages stop emitting up to 10 JobPosting blocks each (Organization, BreadcrumbList, ItemList, CollectionPage and FAQPage stay). Remote role, remote role + city and slice pages: `speakable` moves into a WebPage node instead of a bare SpeakableSpecification. Visible: affected jobs' location chip and salary currency (a San Francisco job shown as "C$148k" shows "$148k"), and which country pages list them. Titles, canonicals, robots, hreflang and sitemaps unchanged.**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): check the site's technical SEO, and that jobs have JobPosting and other pages the right schema. **Checked live:** 59 URLs across every sitemap family plus 250 random job pages, fetched as Googlebot. Every page returned 200 with one H1, OG tags, an absolute self-canonical and no hreflang; unknown URLs return 404 + noindex; every job page had JobPosting with title, description, datePosted, hiringOrganization, validThrough, employmentType and a location. **Found and fixed:** (1) 22 of 250 job pages said the job was in Canada, including San Francisco, Foster City, Menlo Park, Redwood City, Sunnyvale, Berlin and Paris jobs, some showing CAD salaries. Cause: the location normalizer read the state code in "San Francisco, CA" (and "US-CA-Menlo Park") as Canada, and also "DE" (Delaware) as Germany and "IN" (Indiana) as India. After a city, those three codes now name a country only for a city we know ("Toronto, CA", "Berlin, DE") or when the pay currency agrees (CAD, EUR, INR); otherwise the US-state reading wins through the existing city/currency rules, and nothing is guessed. A city written alone ("Berlin") is read as a city, not as a country called "Berlin". Re-scraping a job now corrects a stored country the source text contradicts; an unknown never clears one. (2) The JSON-LD read the employer's country before the job's own place, so Cohere's (Toronto) remote Berlin job was offered to applicants in Canada; the job's text and city now come first. (3) Greenhouse jobs had no posting date: the scraper read `first_published_at`, but the board API's field is `first_published` (367 of 367 Waymo jobs have it, none have the other), so `datePosted` fell back to the day we stored the row (105 of 250 sampled jobs said 2026-11-09; a Waymo job first published 2026-06-26 was one). Re-scraping fills a missing date and never moves a stored one. Freshness and expiry read `lastSeenAt` first, and `isJobTooOld` is unused, so no live job is hidden by its real date. (4) Hybrid jobs were marked TELECOMMUTE, the value for jobs worked remotely 100% of the time, without applicant location requirements (5 of 250); they are now listed at their office. (5) Company pages repeated JobPosting for up to 10 of their jobs; Google asks for JobPosting only on the page for a single job, and `lib/seo/itemListJsonLd.ts` already said listing pages must not emit it. **Not changed, reported:** the Phase 1 sitemap caps (5,000 jobs, 500 companies; `INDEXING_PHASE`); Search Console's sample shows 2 of 127 inspected URLs indexed, 119 "unknown to Google". The jobs sitemap gives 3,725 of 5,000 jobs today's lastmod (their `createdAt`; not checked whether they are new rows or recreated). `/salary/[role]` emits a top-level AggregateOffer for a salary range. Existing rows are corrected as the daily scrape sees them again; nothing was written to the database from here. 9 new tests (6 fail on the old code).

- Homepage served from its 5-minute cache again instead of being rendered on every request (**SEO: faster homepage; no markup change. The homepage response goes back from `private, no-store` to `s-maxage=300`**). **Found** (2026-11-09) re-timing pages after #77: the homepage answered in 4-8 s with `cache-control: private, no-cache, no-store`, while `/salary`, `/high-paying-jobs` and `/remote/companies` were cache hits. Railway's request log shows homepage requests at 5-23 ms between 20:13 and 20:36 UTC and 3-8 s from 20:45 on, and the hourly 90th percentile went from under 100 ms to 2.4-5.7 s from 18:00 UTC. The cause is the freehire catalogue fetch added in #74 (`lib/jobs/freehireCatalog.ts`) with `cache: 'no-store'`: when a render runs that fetch (after each deploy, and when the hourly in-memory copy expires), Next treats the whole page as dynamic and stops serving it from the cache. **What:** the fetch uses `next: { revalidate: 3600 }` (the same hour the numbers were already kept for). **Checked:** a production build with the database reachable, run locally: without the fix the homepage is `private, no-store` and rendered on every request (7.1 s, then about 0.8 s on the small local database); with it, `x-nextjs-cache: HIT`, `s-maxage=300`, 8-72 ms. 1 new test (fails on the old code).

- New page `/high-paying-jobs`, and `/jobs/no-degree` now lists only jobs whose posting does not require a degree (**SEO: two indexable hubs, added to `ALWAYS_INDEXABLE_HUBS`; in the hubs sitemap only while they pass their own index rule (5+ ranked roles; 1+ job), so a noindex state is never advertised. Absolute canonicals, no hreflang. `/high-paying-jobs`: BreadcrumbList, ItemList and FAQPage JSON-LD. `/jobs/no-degree` was noindex (phase 1) and becomes indexable; its title, H1, meta description and FAQ change. Linked from the homepage's popular searches.**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): build pages for the searches with real volume: "high paying jobs" (about 201,000 US searches a month) and "six figure jobs without a degree" (about 1,600), per DataForSEO via OpenSEO. **What:** (1) `/high-paying-jobs` ranks role pages by the median of their live US-dollar listings (the salary guides' method: midpoint of each published range, at least 20 listings per role, seniority folded into the role, `lib/jobs/highPayingJobs.ts`), shows the ten highest-paying open jobs, and answers four questions from the same numbers; cached 30 minutes. (2) `/jobs/no-degree` used to list every software, DevOps, data and sales job as "jobs that don't require a college degree" without reading them, and quoted unsourced figures (an average bootcamp salary, named companies that "hire purely based on skills") and a "Direct apply" badge. It now lists jobs whose description names no degree, or names one and accepts equivalent experience (`lib/jobs/degreeRequirement.ts`: one list of degree phrases and one of "or equivalent experience" phrases, used by a tested classifier and by the database query); the role cards and FAQ are counted from those jobs. Reading every description takes about 4 seconds, so the database pass runs at most hourly in the background and the page filters by its result (jobs added since the last pass wait for the next one). Role labels keep acronyms in capitals ("HR Director", `roleLabelFor`). **Checked** on a local database of 20,223 live jobs: the classifier and the database query agree on every job; 6,459 require a degree, 4,353 accept equivalent experience, 9,411 do not mention one; postings checked by hand, which led to adding "BS in", "or related experience" and professional degrees (a Lead Veterinarian requiring a "Doctor of Veterinary Medicine degree" had been listed); the no-degree page query takes about 0.3 s. Both pages render with status 200, the titles are 53 and 59 characters, and there is no horizontal scroll at 1280 px or 390 px. 13 new tests.

- Homepage: "high-paying" and "6-figure" in the meta description and the hero line (**SEO: the indexable homepage's meta description and the visible sentence under the H1 change; title, H1, canonical, robots and JSON-LD unchanged**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): are we targeting the variants (six figure jobs, 6 figure jobs, 6 fig jobs)? **Found:** the homepage used "six figure jobs" / "six-figure jobs" nine times but never "6 figure" or "high paying" in visible text. US monthly searches (DataForSEO via OpenSEO, 2026-11-09): "high paying jobs" 201,000; "six figure jobs", "6 figure jobs" and "six-figure jobs" 5,400 each (the identical numbers show they are counted as one search); "6fig jobs" no measurable volume. The homepage averaged position ~56 over 480 days in Search Console, so wording alone is not expected to move it much. **What:** the description now starts "N+ high-paying 6-figure jobs ($100k+) from N+ companies."; the hero line reads "N high-paying 6-figure roles at N companies."; the homepage's popular searches link to the two new pages below. Test updated.

- Workday: a retirement plan ("401k", "401(k)", "403(b)", "457(b)") is no longer read as pay (**SEO: no markup change; keeps jobs with a false six-figure salary out of the job pages, sitemaps and JobPosting JSON-LD**). **Found** (2026-11-09) building the high-paying jobs ranking on a local copy of the new employers: Chewy's veterinary technicians and client concierges showed "$401K+". Their postings say "The hourly range for this role is $0.00 - $0.00" and offer a 401k; `lib/scrapers/ats/workday.ts` reads any number followed by "k" in the pay text, so "401k" became $401,000. Chewy is one of the Workday employers added in #73 and had not been synced live yet. **What:** plan names are removed from the pay text before numbers are read. **Checked:** re-syncing Chewy locally kept 41 jobs, none at $401k (52 before, 11 of them false); real ranges next to a 401(k) are still read. 2 new tests (one fails on the old code).

- Homepage: "Top Companies Hiring Now" moved right under the hero and made compact; the two rows of stats became a "We screen → You see" funnel (**SEO: no title, meta, canonical, robots or JSON-LD change; the 20 company links move up in the HTML and each now appears once (the phone layout rendered a second copy of every link); the stats keep every number in the server HTML, with new short text ("We screen", "You see", "about 1 in N makes the list")**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): put Top Companies Hiring Now at the top, and redesign the stats (nine equal big numbers in two rows). **What:** (1) `FeaturedCompaniesCarousel` sits under the hero, as `docs/DESIGN_UX_SPEC.md` §10 orders it (hero, company strip, jobs); one list, two wrapped rows on wide screens, one swipeable row on phones (the old phone layout was a seven-row grid, about 1,400 px, and would have pushed the jobs far down), smaller heading with a "See all companies" link. (2) `SiteStatsStrip`: two panels joined by an arrow, "We screen" (freehire's open jobs, companies, sources and platforms, plus our directly read careers sites) and "You see" (our $100k+ jobs, companies, new this week, countries, typical salary), one big number each, the rest as small chips, and the ratio between the two big numbers; without the catalogue only "You see" shows. **Checked** in a real browser against a local database at 1440 px and 390 px: order hero → companies → live feed → latest jobs → stats, no horizontal scroll, the phone company strip 146 px tall. Tests updated (4 for the stats).

- Deploys build again: the production build gets 1.5 GB of JavaScript memory (**SEO: no change by itself; it lets #75's `/remote/companies` page and the rest of that change go live**). **Found** (2026-11-09): Railway's deploy of #75 failed twice at "Running TypeScript" with "JavaScript heap out of memory"; Node's default heap on the build machine is about 760 MB, and the type check alone uses about 725 MB on the commit before and 731 MB on #75 (`tsc --extendedDiagnostics`), so the project was at the edge and #75 tipped it over. **What:** `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=1536` on `next build` in the `build` script. **Checked:** the same build with the heap capped at 760 MB fails with the same error; with the new setting it completes. The machine's total memory is not known here; if a build is ever killed for memory with no heap error, the next step is to skip the build's own type check (CI already runs `npm run typecheck` on every pull request).

- Real company logos for the directly-read employers, and logo lookups no longer stuck on a fallback after a hiccup (**SEO: no markup, title, canonical or robots change; company logos in the company directory, the remote-companies page and job rows are real logos more often**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): make sure real logos show, not placeholders. **Found:** (1) companies created by the employer sync had no website, so their logo came from a name lookup, which can match another company with the same name (Close, Ghost, Medium, Summer); (2) on live `/companies`, 46 of 48 logos were real, but Scale AI and Relativity Space had stored logo.dev URLs for guessed domains (`scale-ai.com`, `relativity-space.com`) that have no logo, so a letter showed; (3) the `/logo` route treated any failed check of logo.dev (rate limit, server error, timeout) as "no logo", sent icon.horse instead, and remembered that for a day with a week-long cache header; a burst of checks on 2026-11-09 got icon.horse for about 250 companies logo.dev does have (Intel, Docker, Zapier). **What:** every direct employer but two now carries its domain (`website` in `lib/constants/directEmployers.ts`): from freehire's company record, or the employer's own Ashby board (which also showed that the `ateam` Ashby board is a dating app, not A.Team, so A.Team is removed), or a domain whose homepage names the company; shorteners and job-board hosts were rejected, and all 367 domains were checked to have a logo on logo.dev. Pure Storage (its logo was a guessed `pure-storage.com`) is added as a direct employer, board `purestorage` (named Everpure, its new name), 362 jobs. Lifted and Ghost are left without one (unsure which company the board is) and ZipRecruiter too (the site treats its domain as a job board; its name lookup finds its logo). The sync (`companyUpdatesFor`, `lib/companies/directEmployerCompany.ts`) fills a missing website from it and drops a stored logo.dev logo for a different domain. The `/logo` route remembers a fallback for a day only when logo.dev said it has no logo (404); after a rate limit, server error or timeout it keeps the fallback 5 minutes and asks again. **Checked:** a local sync gave OpenAI, Docker, Sardine and Intel their websites and domain-based logo URLs. 9 new tests.

- New page `/remote/companies`: "Remote companies hiring $100k+ roles" (**SEO: a new indexable hub, in the hubs sitemap and in `ALWAYS_INDEXABLE_HUBS` next to `/companies` and `/remote`; absolute canonical, `index, follow`, no hreflang, BreadcrumbList, CollectionPage, ItemList and FAQPage JSON-LD; one new internal link to it from `/remote`. This adds one URL to the phase-1 indexable set, as asked.**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): publish a page like the remote-company directories of remote100k.com and others, for companies offering $100k+ roles. **What:** every company with an indexable company page (5+ public jobs, the `/companies` rule) that has at least one open remote job, with its number of remote jobs, most first (`loadRemoteCompaniesDirectory` in `lib/jobs/remoteCompanies.ts`, cached 5 minutes); remote is the remote-only filter of the `/remote` hub, structured fields only. It reuses the `/companies` grid with search (`CompanySearch` takes a `noun`, here "remote job"), a short explanation with links to `/remote` and `/companies`, the five companies with the most remote openings, and four questions answered with live numbers. One page, no pagination. **Checked** against a local database: 200, title "180 Remote Companies Hiring $100k+ Roles | Six Figure Jobs" (58 characters), canonical `https://www.6figjobs.com/remote/companies`, four JSON-LD blocks; desktop and 390 px phone with no horizontal scroll. 2 new tests.

- 229 more employers read straight from their careers systems: the remote companies on remote100k.com that had few or no jobs here (**SEO: more job pages with employer apply links, through the normal ingest; no template change**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): check that the companies on remote100k.com, We Work Remotely and NoDesk are covered, in freehire or our own sources. **Found:** remote100k lists 588 remote companies; 135 had live jobs here, 554 are in freehire's catalogue, but 453 had none on our site (freehire's import reaches only the newest jobs per currency). We Work Remotely answered 403 to our fetch (a bot block, not worked around) and NoDesk's robots.txt disallows Anthropic's crawlers, so those two lists were not read. **What:** each company without jobs here (or with under 5 here and 5+ there) was tried on Greenhouse, Ashby and Lever under its name; a board was accepted only when the board's own company name matched. 236 matched; 7 were left out as possibly a different company with the same name (Agent, Circle, Engine, Warp, Fable, Brilliant) or a stale board (Hightouch). The other 229 are added to `lib/constants/directEmployers.ts` (371 in all), mapped to the existing company page where there is one. **Checked:** dry run read all 229 boards, 6,741 jobs, no failures; a local run of the real ingest kept 2,534 (1,733 remote) at 169 companies in 34 seconds.

- Ashby hybrid jobs are no longer stored as remote (**SEO: job pages of hybrid Ashby jobs stop claiming remote work: `remote`/`remoteMode` drive the job's remote labels, the /remote pages and the remote fields of the JobPosting JSON-LD; those jobs leave /remote listings and counts. No template, title, canonical or robots change.**). **Found** (2026-11-09) building the remote-companies page: OpenAI came first with 423 "remote" jobs while most of its jobs are in San Francisco. Ashby's posting API sets `isRemote: true` on hybrid jobs too (OpenAI: 533 of 814 jobs had `isRemote: true` and `workplaceType: "Hybrid"`), and `lib/scrapers/ats/ashby.ts` trusted `isRemote`. **What:** the Ashby adapter reads `workplaceType` (`readAshbyWorkplaceType`: Remote, Hybrid, OnSite) and uses it when present; it is passed to ingest as `workplaceType`, which `processLocation` lets decide remote, hybrid or on-site. Because a job seen again normally keeps its stored location, `refreshJob` now rewrites `remote` and `remoteMode` when the source gives a workplace type, so jobs already stored wrongly are corrected on their next sync. **Checked** on a local database: re-syncing OpenAI took it from 423 remote jobs to 21 remote and 404 hybrid. 4 new tests.

- Homepage: a second row of big numbers, "The catalogue we screen, today", with freehire's live catalogue figures (**SEO: four numbers with short labels, one sentence and one external link (to freehire.me/open) added to the indexable homepage's stats section; no title, meta, canonical, robots, hreflang or JSON-LD change**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): show freehire's catalogue figures on our site, since our import reads from it ("over 700k websites"), as big numbers like freehire's own, not in a paragraph. **Found:** freehire publishes the figures live for quoting at `GET https://freehire.me/api/v1/stats/catalog` (its /open page: "Every number, live, with the endpoint behind it"); on 2026-11-09 it gave 4,871,976 unique open jobs, 697,981 companies, 438 sources and 179 ATS platforms. **Wording:** these are companies, not websites, and just under 700k; freehire collects the jobs and we keep only those with published six-figure pay. So the row sits under its own heading below our own counts, the jobs figure is labelled "open jobs screened", and the line under it reads "Collected by freehire (live numbers), plus 142 employers' careers sites we read directly. We list only the jobs whose published pay clears our six-figure line." **What:** `loadFreehireCatalog` (`lib/jobs/freehireCatalog.ts`) reads the endpoint (2.5 s timeout), uses `unique_open_jobs` (not `open_jobs`, which counts the same job on several sources), and is cached an hour, a failed read included, so a freehire outage cannot slow the homepage; the row is left out when the numbers cannot be read. `SiteStatsStrip` draws it with the same grid, icons and count-up as the row above. The employer count is `DIRECT_EMPLOYERS.length`. **Checked** in a real browser on a throwaway page at 1280 px and 390 px: four columns on desktop, two on a phone, no horizontal scroll. 6 new tests.

- 45 more employers read straight from their careers systems, NVIDIA, Salesforce, Cisco, Intel, Adobe, CrowdStrike, PayPal, Snap, Spotify, Block, Elastic, Toast and Zoox among them (**SEO: more job pages with employer apply links, through the normal ingest; no template change**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): which top tech companies the site lacks, then add them. **Found:** of 153 well-known tech companies, 60 had 5+ live jobs, 31 had 1-4, 62 had none. **Added** to `lib/constants/directEmployers.ts`: 25 with a Greenhouse, Ashby or Lever board (Lucid Motors, Elastic, Toast, Zoox, Block, Riot Games, Ripple, Spotify, 1Password, Carta, Mercury, Supabase, Faire, Benchling, SoFi, Peloton, Snyk, Checkr, Linear, HP, Nextdoor, Squarespace, Miro, Lattice, Airtable; Mercury and HP boards checked to be the fintech and HP Inc.) and 20 on Workday, each site verified against Workday's job-search endpoint (NVIDIA, Salesforce, Cisco, Intel, Micron, Broadcom, Adobe, CrowdStrike, Workday, PayPal, eBay, Snap, Zoom, Zillow, Expedia, Etsy, Chewy, Visa, Mastercard, Capital One). Left out: Autodesk (its site answered with 4 jobs, likely the wrong one) and Target (mostly store jobs). Not covered: companies with custom careers sites (Apple, Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Netflix, Tesla, Uber, DoorDash, Shopify, Atlassian, Intuit, Booking.com) and ones whose board was not found (AMD, Qualcomm, Palo Alto Networks, Dell, ServiceNow, Oracle and others). Test updated for Workday URLs.

- Workday careers sites can be read again (**SEO: no change by itself; it lets the employer sync read Workday boards**). **Found** adding Workday employers (2026-11-09): every Workday board failed with HTTP 400. Workday's job search accepts at most 20 jobs a page (checked on zoom.wd5: 20 -> 200, 21 -> 400) and `lib/scrapers/ats/workday.ts` asked for 100; it also reports the real total on the first page only and `total: 0` after it, which would have ended the loop after the second page. Page size is now 20 and the first non-zero total is kept. Local check: Zoom 83 jobs read (33 kept), Etsy 69 (56), CrowdStrike 389 (111), Snap 214 (129). 1 new test (fails on the old code). **Also:** the job id is now the Workday tenant plus the requisition number (`capitalone:R1005314`), not the requisition number alone. Requisition numbers are counted per employer, and the job row id is source + external id, so a second Workday employer with the same number would have refreshed the first one's row (keeping it alive) instead of creating its own. No live rows carry the old form, since every Workday board was failing before this fix. The test checks the id.

- `CLAUDE.md`: finished work always gets a pull request into `main` without waiting to be asked (**SEO: no change; instructions for assistants, nothing the site serves**). **Found** when the owner said so on 2026-11-09. **What:** a standing rule under Project Context (open the PR right away, subscribe to its activity, do not merge unless told), and the old line "Always push to `main`" now says never to push to `main` directly, since the two instructions contradicted each other.

- Homepage: the hero's salary bands can be picked, a strip of companies that added roles today, numbers that count up when seen, and a shorter hero sub-headline (**SEO: indexable homepage; the H1, title, meta description, canonical, robots and JSON-LD are unchanged. The sub-headline loses the sentence "Pay thresholds are set per currency" (the per-market floors are still in the panel beside it). New crawlable internal links: up to about a dozen job pages in the hero panel (in the HTML but `hidden` until their band is picked, so Google may weigh that text less) and up to 24 `/company/<slug>` pages in the new strip (companies with 5 or more jobs only, the same rule as the companies directory; the strip's looping copy is a second, inert, `aria-hidden` set of the same links). Every number is still in the server HTML; the count-up only runs in the browser, on numbers that start below the fold. Watch the homepage's impressions and the crawl stats in Search Console after this ships.**). **Found** comparing with a competitor's homepage from full-page screenshots (the site itself is behind a bot checkpoint and was not fetched, so what its motion actually does is not known): a live demo in the hero, a strip of companies with new roles, numbers that read as counted, short punchy copy. Our hero panel was static, our "Top Companies Hiring Now" logo row does not move (its CSS wraps a centred row; it has no marquee), and the stats strip printed finished numbers. **What:** (1) `HeroSalaryBands` (client leaf) turns the four salary-band bars into buttons: the picked band is bold, the others fade, a line says how many roles pay in it (the same counts as before) and up to three real recent roles are listed with a "See all" link. The roles come from `loadBandSamples` (`lib/jobs/salaryBandSamples.ts`): the 300 newest $100k+ jobs, USD only, sorted by the same midpoint rule as the counts, so a listed role is one the band's count includes; cached 10 minutes; the highest band can have fewer than three roles or none. It starts on the band with the most roles. (2) `NewRolesStrip` shows companies by new jobs added in the last 24 hours (when we first saw the job, like the live feed), each with a "+N" chip, sliding left in CSS only (no JavaScript, paused on hover and focus, stopped and scrollable by hand under reduced motion); `loadNewRolesByCompany` (`lib/jobs/newRolesByCompany.ts`) is cached 5 minutes, and the strip is left out when fewer than 4 companies qualify. (3) `AnimatedNumber` (`components/ui/AnimatedNumber.tsx`) counts a number up the first time it scrolls into view, only if it started below the fold, and animates the hero count between bands; under reduced motion or without JavaScript it shows the final number. The stats strip uses it and gains "Counted from our own job database, refreshed every few minutes." I used JavaScript and not the CSS `animation-timeline: view()` pattern of `reveal-on-scroll` because CSS cannot print "3,874" or "$165k" from a number. (4) Sub-headline: "3,874 roles at 912 companies. Every listing shows its pay range. Every apply button says where it goes: the employer's own site, or the job board the role was posted on." The H1 already had the short two-line shape with the pay promise, so it was left alone. Both new loaders fail soft: an error hides the example roles or the strip, not the page. **Checked** in a real browser against a throwaway page with fixture data (24 checks, 0 hydration warnings): band click moves the highlight and counts 910 to 40 (374 mid-way); all four band panels are in the DOM with one visible; the marquee moves and pauses on hover; a below-the-fold stat starts at 0, passes through intermediate values and ends exactly on 3,874; reduced motion and no-JavaScript both show the final numbers at once; no horizontal scroll at 390px. **Not checked:** the real homepage against the live database (no database in the sandbox), so the real band samples and the strip's contents are untested outside the unit tests; the 24-hour window can leave the strip empty on a slow day. 26 new tests.
