- Jobs straight from 97 employers' own careers systems, OpenAI, Anthropic, SpaceX and xAI included (**SEO: many new job pages, with employer apply links; they join the existing company pages where those exist (80 of 97) and enter the job sitemaps and Indexing API queue through the normal ingest; no template change**). **Asked** (2026-11-09): all jobs from OpenAI, Anthropic, SpaceX, xAI etc., and the jobs on aijobs.ai. **Found:** the site had 35 OpenAI, 25 Anthropic, 32 SpaceX and 11 xAI jobs while their boards list 813, 677, 2,908 and 304; OpenAI's stored board (Greenhouse) answers 404, it moved to Ashby. aijobs.ai disallows crawling its job pages (robots.txt `Disallow: /job/`) and re-lists employers' jobs, so its listings are not copied; its employers are covered from their own boards instead. **What:** `lib/constants/directEmployers.ts` lists 97 employers whose Greenhouse, Ashby or Lever board answered on 2026-11-09 (AI labs and AI infrastructure, the AI employers seen on aijobs.ai, defense and space, and large tech), each mapped to its existing company page slug where there is one. `scripts/sync-direct-employers.ts` scrapes each board and ingests through `upsertJobsForCompanyFromAts` (salary gate: only published pay reaching the six-figure floor; roles and countries; dedupe; indexing queue). It is a new step in the daily freehire workflow, independent of freehire (runs when freehire fails), with expiry, remediation, slices and the indexing drain running if either sync succeeded; job timeout 150 minutes. Jobs still listed get `updatedAt` refreshed, so run-expiry closes only those that came down. Local run of the real ingest: OpenAI 643 of 813 accepted, Anthropic 512 of 677, xAI 92 of 304 (the rest publish no salary). Not covered: employers with custom careers sites and no public board (Google, Apple, Meta, Microsoft, Amazon, Tesla). 3 new tests.

- `/jobs/visa-sponsorship`: meta description under 160 characters and a heading above the job list (**SEO: the page is noindex, so no ranking effect; the meta and og/twitter description lose ", with links to apply"; no URL, canonical, robots or JSON-LD change**). **Found** in the OpenSEO audit of 2026-11-09: "meta description too long" (162 characters) and "heading levels skip" on this page, the job titles (h3) sat directly under the h1. **What:** the description ends at "...engineering manager roles." (144 characters even with a 5-digit count), and the job list section has a visible h2, "Open roles with visa sponsorship", styled like the page's "Related job searches" heading, which also labels the section (`aria-labelledby`).

- Google Indexing API worker sends new job pages before removal notices (**SEO: no markup, metadata, canonical or robots change; changes which job URLs Google is told about first. More new job pages should reach Google within days instead of waiting behind removals; removals of expired jobs still go out, at most about a fifth of the day's quota unless new pages leave quota unused.**). **Found** reading the 2026-11-09 run of the manual indexing workflow: the backfill queued 180 new job pages (`URL_UPDATED`), but the worker read its queue oldest first whatever the type, so the removal notices (`URL_DELETED`) that pile up daily as jobs expire were sent first. With 60 sent in the last 24 hours it had 120 of the 180 cap left, and all 99 notifications sent were removals (21 more skipped as expired) while the 180 new pages stayed queued. Many new job pages are still "URL is unknown to Google" in Search Console, which this likely contributes to (an inference). **What:** `selectIndexingBatch` (`lib/indexing/queueSelection.ts`, pure and tested) picks each run's batch: new and updated pages first, newest first; removals oldest first up to a reserved share of the daily limit (`INDEXING_API_DELETE_SHARE`, default 0.2, so 36 of 180) minus the removals already sent in the last 24 hours; quota the pages do not use goes to removals. `scripts/process-google-indexing-queue.ts` uses it and logs the split. The 180 a day cap and the retry and safety-gate logic are unchanged (Google's real quota still has to be checked in Google Cloud Console). **Checked** by running the worker in dry-run mode against a scratch database shaped like 2026-11-09 (60 sent, 300 old removals and 180 new pages pending): the batch is the 84 newest pages first, then 36 removals; before the change it would have been the 120 oldest records, all removals. 9 new tests.

- Real numbers and questions answered on `/salary` and `/jobs`, and a wrong claim removed from `/salary` (**SEO: adds visible text, two data tables and a `FAQPage` JSON-LD block to the indexable `/salary`, and a country table to the plain `/jobs`; titles, meta descriptions, canonicals and robots are unchanged. The `/salary` FAQ and its JSON-LD are built from the same items, so they cannot disagree. Google shows FAQ rich results only for a few kinds of site, so expect the benefit to be the added page text, not a rich result.**). **Found** after Search Console reported "Some fixes failed" for "Crawled - currently not indexed": `/jobs`, `/salary`, `/remote`, `/jobs/software-engineer` and `/blog` are allowed, fetch fine and have matching canonicals, so the likeliest reason is that Google sees little on them that other sites do not already have (this is an inference, Google does not say). **What:** (1) `/salary` gets a "Salary at a glance" table (role, median, middle half, listings) with one sentence naming the highest- and lowest-paying role, and a "Salary questions" FAQ whose answers quote the same numbers, the minimum-listings rule (`MIN_LISTINGS` is now exported and quoted, not typed) and where the listings come from. With fewer than two roles, or when all medians are equal, the ranking sentence and question are left out. (2) The "How we build" text said the guides were "scraped directly from company career pages" and that "lowball postings" were removed; about half of jobs apply through a job board, so it now says postings are collected from employers' career pages and job boards, and that only postings at or above the six-figure threshold count. (3) `/jobs` shows "Where the six-figure jobs are": the six countries with the most open jobs, with typical pay (the median) and the middle half in local currency, from the same `loadListingSalaryStats` the header card uses and cached with it. A country with too few listings to quote a median is left out. Plain `/jobs` only, like the category row. `RoleMedian` now carries the 25th and 75th percentile. The pure logic is in `lib/salary/indexGlance.ts`; 8 new tests. **Checked and left alone:** the sitemap warning that 52% of dated URLs share 2026-11-05 comes from the job-page sitemap, where the date is the later of when we added the job and when the source posted it (`lib/seo/jobLastmod.ts`, deliberately so, with tests). About 2,300 jobs really were created on 5 November in one import, and the posting dates behind them run from 30 October to 5 November, so switching to posting dates would only spread them over a week.

- Three salary-reading fixes: Greenhouse yearly pay read as daily, Ashby pay never requested, Lever's structured pay ignored (**SEO: no markup, metadata, canonical or robots change; more real six-figure jobs become eligible, so expect more job pages, JobPosting JSON-LD and sitemap URLs after the next scrape; all three only add jobs that were wrongly dropped or missing a salary, none loosens the salary threshold**). **Found** by running our own ATS adapters and salary pipeline over 45 live boards and comparing with freehire.me, which reports a qualifying salary for every job we compared: only 36 of 84 (43%) of those jobs passed our gate from the ATS data alone. **What:** (1) `parseGreenhouseSalary` (`lib/ingest/greenhouseSalaryParser.ts`): on the "pay-range" markup the interval was read from the whole posting, so "billions of requests per day" (Box) or "daily stand-ups" (Hasbro) made $198,500 a daily rate, about $51M a year, rejected as `too_high`. It now reads the range and the 60 characters after it, not the text before it ("three days per week." above a range did the same). On six live boards, annual-sized ranges labelled "day" went from 38 of 1,181 to 0. (2) Ashby (`lib/scrapers/ats/ashby.ts`): the posting API was asked without `includeCompensation=true`, which returns no pay at all (0 of 814 OpenAI jobs, 648 with it); the adapter now asks for it and reads the `Salary` component of `summaryComponents` (interval `1 YEAR` / `1 HOUR` etc., currency, min, max), keeping the summary text in `salaryRaw`. (3) Lever (`lib/scrapers/ats/lever.ts`): the adapter read pay only from text and ignored the posting's `salaryRange {min, max, currency, interval}`; it now uses it. Both structured readers return nothing, leaving the text parser to work, when the interval is unknown or the currency is missing. **Result** on the same 45 boards (freehire's qualifying jobs that pass our salary gate from ATS data): 70 of 75 (93%), up from 36 of 84 (43%): Greenhouse 46/51 (was 35/58), Ashby 19/19 (was 1/20), Lever 5/5 (was 0/6). 11 new tests (`__tests__/ingest/greenhouseSalaryParser.test.ts`, `__tests__/ats/atsStructuredSalary.test.ts`). Jobs already rejected come back only after the next scrape.

- One opening imported twice under two company records is expired too (**SEO: removes duplicate job pages; once the next remediation run expires them they leave the job sitemaps and the job page answers as expired jobs do. No change to any template, title, canonical, robots or JSON-LD.**). **Found** in the OpenSEO audit of 2026-11-09: a duplicate `<title>` on two GDIT job pages for "Senior System Administrator - DISA E-ICAM Program", the same requisition (RQ231857) from GDIT's employee-referral and public Workday career sites, filed under the companies "gdit" and "General Dynamics Information Technology". The cross-source check grouped copies only within one company record. **What:** `duplicateJobIdsToExpire` also groups live jobs by the employer's account on the applicant tracking system (`atsTenantKey`: the Workday, Recruitee, Teamtailor, BambooHR or Breezy subdomain, or the Greenhouse, Lever, Ashby or Workable path), with the same title and place, joining overlapping groups. Of copies on employer sites, the public career site is kept over an employee-referral or internal one. Run on the 5,039 live jobs from the public API: 4 more copies expired (3 GDIT referral copies, 1 Optro copy in London under a second company record), none before this change. 4 new tests.

- Shared queries on `/jobs`, `/companies` and job pages are cached for 5 minutes (**SEO: no markup change; these pages answer faster**). **Found** in the same audit and re-timed with warm caches: `/jobs` ~4 s, `/companies` ~2.2 s, job pages ~2.7 s, while role pages, whose queries were cached in an earlier change, answered in ~0.3 s. All three are rendered per request. **What:** `memoAsync` (stale-while-revalidate, as the role pages use) around the `/jobs` list, its total in the metadata and its salary-band links; the job page's "similar jobs" query (keyed by role, title words and country, so shared by every job of that role and country); and the site stats and companies directory in `lib/jobs/publicStats.ts`, which were cached per request only. The job page itself is not switched to ISR: a database error would then serve its "temporarily unavailable" page for up to an hour.

- Lint fix in `scripts/freehire-overlap-report.ts` (**SEO: no change; a read-only script**). `main` CI failed on it since the overlap report shipped: five `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-expressions` errors from comma-joined statements (`a++, b.add(x)`). Each is now two statements; the script does exactly the same.

- Every `<title>` fits in 60 characters, keeping the salary on job pages (**SEO: `<title>` text gets shorter on pages whose title was 61-65 characters, and on blog posts, which were up to 74; no URL, canonical, robots, hreflang or JSON-LD change. Indexable pages affected include `/jobs` (drops " | Six Figure Jobs"), `/remote`, role pages with long role names, job pages and blog posts, so expect a re-crawl of their titles.**). **Found** in the OpenSEO site audit of 2026-11-09 (500 pages): 21 "title too long" warnings, all over 60 characters, e.g. "209 Senior Software Engineer Jobs Paying $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs" (65) and "Software Engineer Salary 2026: Guide by Level & Location | Six Figure Jobs" (74). The shared cap was 65 and several templates (skills, industry, remote, blog) appended the brand without any cap. **What:** `TITLE_MAX` in `lib/seo/metaText.ts` is 60 and `jobMeta` uses it instead of its own 65. Skills, industry, remote and blog titles go through `fitTitle` with their existing wording, dropping the brand first, then the count. Job titles try, in order: full with brand, without brand, the salary without "/yr" ("$200k–$250k"), without the company; a role too long even then is shortened at a word so the salary (the click reason) stays. Tests updated to the 60 cap and the new job-title order, 1 new test.

- Job rows on every remaining job list, a "Browse N jobs" button on city, category and remote pages, and shorter city and category titles (**SEO: (1) the homepage, `/jobs/skills/<skill>`, `/jobs/industry/<industry>`, `/salary/<role>` and `/company/<slug>` now show the same single-line rows, so the same caveat as the earlier rows entry applies to them: each row's summary, skills and `/jobs/skills/<slug>` links are in the HTML but hidden until the row is opened, which Google may weigh less; the homepage is indexable, so watch its impressions. (2) City and category `<title>` text changes shape, e.g. "$100k+ jobs in Austin (1,234 openings) | Six Figure Jobs" becomes "1,234 $100k+ Jobs in Austin | Six Figure Jobs"; both page types are noindex until their phase gates open, so there is no ranking effect yet. Canonicals, robots, JSON-LD and descriptions are unchanged.**). **Found** after the first rows shipped: the company page showed rows for its first jobs but old cards under "Show more jobs", and pages using `JobList` still had the old cards. **What:** `CompanyJobsMore`, `JobList` (skills, industry, salary and search pages) and the homepage `LatestOpportunities` use `JobRow`/`JobRowList`; `JobRow` takes an optional `className` (the homepage list animates its rows in). City, category and remote pages get the "Browse N jobs" button and wrap their list in `ListingLayout` so the button has a `#jobs` target. They do not get the remote/hybrid/on-site links: those pages cannot filter by work type, so a link would open a page with a different count. City and category titles now come from `buildCityTitle` / `buildCategoryTitle` in `lib/seo/metaText.ts` (same shape as role pages, held to 65 characters for every city and category by tests). They already carried the live count before this change; the old city and category wording could pass 65 characters ("Engineering jobs paying $100k+ (1,420 openings) | Six Figure Jobs" is 66). The median salary card was already on the hub, role, tier, country, city, category and remote pages.

- Listing pages show jobs as single-line rows that open in place (**SEO: the job title stays a real `<a href>` to the job page and every row keeps its summary, pills and skill links in the HTML (the panel is `hidden`, not removed), so crawlable links and job URLs do not change; but text and the `/jobs/skills/<slug>` links that used to be visible on every card are now hidden until a visitor opens the row, and Google may give hidden text less weight. No change to metadata, canonical, robots or JSON-LD. Watch skill-page impressions in Search Console after this ships.**). **Found** comparing with spainjobs.io, whose rows are one calm line with an expand chevron; ours were large cards with the same ten pieces of information on every job. **What:** new `JobRow` / `JobRowList` (`components/jobs/JobRow.tsx`) replace `JobCard variant="homepage"` inside `JobRows` (role, salary-tier, country, category and other listing pages) and the `/jobs` infinite list. A row shows logo, title, company · posted (or "New" for jobs under 3 days old), the salary in green, and the location; the chevron button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, labelled with the job title) opens the summary, work-type and seniority pills, skills and a "View job" button. Clicking a row opens it instead of navigating; the title link and "View job" are the way to the job page. The homepage, company pages, search and job page still use `JobCard`, unchanged except that its small helpers are now exported for reuse. 5 new tests; `listingPagination.test.ts` now stubs `JobRow` instead of `JobCard` (same assertions).

- Category icon row on `/jobs` (**SEO: adds eight crawlable internal links, `/jobs/category/<slug>`, to the plain `/jobs` page only; no change to metadata, canonical, robots or JSON-LD. Category pages are still noindex until their phase gate opens, so this passes link equity to them but does not make them indexable**). **Found** comparing with spainjobs.io, whose hub leads into its categories with an icon row. **What:** `CategoryRow` shows Engineering, Product, Data, Design, DevOps, ML / AI, Sales and Marketing as icon tiles with their job counts, sorted by count, scrolling sideways on phones. It uses the existing category definitions (`lib/seo/jobCategories.ts`) and `lucide-react` icons (already a dependency), and each count is the category page's own number (same roles, same `isHundredKLocal` filter), cached like the other hub facets. It shows only when no filter or search is active and the page is the first, and a category with no jobs is left out.

- Role, salary-tier and country pages get the same header as `/jobs`, and the `/jobs` and salary-tier titles carry the live count (**SEO: titles and meta descriptions change on indexable pages, so expect a re-crawl and a short ranking wobble on `/jobs` and `/jobs/100k-plus`…`400k-plus`; canonicals, robots, JSON-LD and noindex rules are unchanged; the new filter links are `rel="nofollow"` and every `?remoteMode=` variant already canonicalises to the clean URL**). **Found** comparing with spainjobs.io, whose pages lead with a count and a one-line description of what the page is. **What:** (1) `ListingHeader` on `/jobs/<role>`, `/jobs/100k-plus`…`400k-plus` and `/jobs/location/<country>` now says what the page is ("Every Software Engineer job here shows a published salary of $100k or more · average …") and gets the "Browse N jobs" button and "N remote / hybrid / on-site roles" links, built by the shared `workTypeStats`. (2) `/jobs` title is now "3,874 Six-Figure Tech Jobs — $100k+ Openings | Six Figure Jobs" and its description opens with the count; salary-tier titles go from "Top $100k–$199k Jobs" to "1,234 $100k–$199k Jobs with Published Salaries". Titles and descriptions come from `buildJobsHubTitle` / `buildJobsHubDescription` / `buildSalaryTierTitle` / `buildSalaryTierDescription` in `lib/seo/metaText.ts` (kept inside the 65 / 130–155 character limits by tests). The count in the meta is the same `total` the page shows, so the two cannot disagree. Filtered and deep pages are noindex and skip the extra count query. Role, company, country, city and home pages already had count-led titles; they are unchanged.

- `/jobs` header copy and action row (**SEO: `?q=` searches are already `noindex, follow`, so their new heading has no ranking effect; on the unfiltered `/jobs` only the visible summary sentence changes and a button and three filter links are added; title, meta description, canonical, JSON-LD and robots are untouched. The filter links are `rel="nofollow"`.**). **Found** comparing with spainjobs.io: a search read "Results for “seo”" with the lowercase query, and the header had no way to jump to the results or narrow by work type. **What:** a search now reads "SEO jobs paying $100k+" (long queries: "$100k+ jobs matching “…”", no results: "No jobs matching “…” yet"); the summary says what the page is ("Every listing shows a published salary. Browse N open roles by role, country, remote work or seniority."). `ListingHeader` takes two optional props, `cta` ("Browse N jobs", jumps to `#jobs`) and `stats` (counted links such as "11 remote roles", from the work-type counts the page already computes), so other listing pages are unchanged until they opt in. `ListingLayout`'s results column now has `id="jobs"`. Search words are cased for the heading by `displayKeyword` ("seo" → "SEO"); 4 new tests.

- Overlap report for dropping freehire (**SEO: no change; a read-only script, nothing the site serves**). `scripts/freehire-overlap-report.ts` counts the active `board:freehire` jobs by where their apply link points and how many belong to boards we already scrape (`CompanyATS`), need only a board token we can read from the link, or need a lookup first. Run it with a read-only `DATABASE_URL`; it only selects. Workable short links (`apply.workable.com/j/<id>`) count as needing a lookup, since they name no board.

- Edge cases from the site audit (**SEO: listing pages past their last page now answer 404 instead of an indexable empty page; unknown `/salary/<slug>` answers 404 instead of redirecting to the software engineer guide; no other change**). **Found** probing odd URLs on 2026-11-08: (1) `/jobs/software-engineer?page=999` and `/jobs/100k-plus?page=500` were "index, follow" with no jobs, a soft 404 to Google; role, salary tier, country, category and remote-role listings now call `notFoundPastLastPage` in their loader (`withRuntimeFallback` re-throws it). `/jobs` already marked deep pages noindex; `/companies` shows its last page. (2) `/api/jobs?page=abc` answered 500 (`Number('abc')` is NaN); the page is now a whole number from 1. (3) `/salary/xyz` and `/salary/mechanic` 308-redirected to `/salary/software-engineer`; a slug that matches no role is now a 404 like `/jobs/<role>`, close matches still redirect (`/salary/data` -> `/salary/data-engineer`). Also checked: search escapes `<script>` input; unknown job, company, role, country and blog URLs already 404. 2 new tests.

- Phone menu: the header's slide-in menu has a screen-reader title (**SEO: no change**). **Found** in the 2026-11-08 audit: opening it logged Radix's error "`DialogContent` requires a `DialogTitle`", so screen readers announced an unnamed dialog. It now has a visually hidden "Menu" title and no empty description reference.

- One opening imported twice is now listed once (**SEO: duplicate job pages are expired, so they leave the job sitemaps and become noindex; the employer's own copy stays**). **Found** in the 2026-11-08 site audit: pairs of identical job pages, e.g. iwoca's "Applied AI Engineer" from its Ashby page ("London") and again from Adzuna ("London, UK"). The ingest dedupe key is company + title + full location text, so the two did not match. **What:** a new step in `scripts/remediate-data-quality.ts` (after every sync and in Data Quality Guard) groups live jobs by company, normalized title and city (remote jobs by their whole location, so "Remote, US" and "Remote, Canada" stay apart) and expires all but one copy: the one whose apply link goes to the employer rather than a job board, else the newest. Different cities (Google's Sunnyvale and Cambridge postings) are left alone. `lib/jobs/crossSourceDuplicates.ts`, 3 new tests; checked on a local Postgres (board copy expired, employer copy kept, second run changes nothing).

- Apply buttons say where they go (**SEO: copy only; the `/jobs` meta description drops its apply clause, the homepage `og:description` drops "Apply on the employer's own page"; no URL, canonical, robots or JSON-LD change, and JobPosting never claimed `directApply`**). **Found** in the 2026-11-08 site audit: in a sample of 696 live jobs about half the apply links go to a job board, not the employer (XING 115, Adzuna 98, Himalayas 36, WhatJobs 31, Built In 28, jobs.ch/jobup.ch/SEEK 19), freehire collects from boards. Yet every job page said "Apply on company site" and "Opens <company>'s own application page", and the hero, about, jobs, remote, companies, company, tier, country and visa pages said every listing links to the employer's application page. **What:** `boardNameFor` (`lib/scrapers/utils/boardHosts.ts`) names the board an apply link points at; job pages then say "Apply on XING" and "Opens <company>'s listing on XING". The other pages now say each apply button says where it goes, the employer's own page or the job board it was posted on. jobs.ch and jobup.ch join the known board hosts. 2 new tests.

- No more slow first visit after a cache expiry: the in-process cache serves stale-while-revalidate (**SEO: no output change; cached numbers and listings can be one refresh older, at most 5 minutes plus the time a refresh takes**). **Why:** after the role-page caching shipped (2026-11-08), repeat visits took 0.24-0.5s but the first visit after each 5-minute expiry still took 1.7-3.3s, waiting for every query again. **What:** `memoAsync` (`lib/cache/memo.ts`) returns an expired entry at once (up to `MAX_STALE_MS`, 1 hour past its TTL) and runs one refresh in the background; a failed refresh keeps the old value and is retried by the next visit. Older entries, and keys never loaded (the first visit after a deploy), still wait. Used by 20 files, all public listing data (role, tier, country, remote, jobs hub pages, feeds, suggest API, site counts). 3 new tests.

- Homepage "Latest" list: English jobs with a real role come first, then the jobs taken to fill the list (**SEO: no URL, metadata or JSON-LD change**). **Found** checking the live homepage after the previous change (2026-11-08): the list still opened with two German "(m/w/d)" DevOps jobs and two Facharzt jobs. Featured jobs were picked first, but the per-country cap let only a few through, the rest were filled with other jobs, and sorting the whole list by date put the newer fill on top. The live feed was already right. Test updated.

- Homepage lists lead with English jobs that have a real role (**SEO: no URL, metadata or JSON-LD change; the same jobs, in a different selection on the homepage**). **Decided** by the owner (2026-11-08). The live feed and "Latest" list take jobs with a role page and no German gender tag in the title ("(m/w/d)", "(w/m/d)"; English "(m/f/d)" is not matched) first, then fill with the rest; each list stays newest first, with its employer and per-country caps. Before, German doctor and construction agency ads (role "other") were the newest jobs and topped both lists. `isHomepageFeatured` in `lib/jobs/latestSelection.ts`. 3 new tests.

- Non-English job postings are no longer offered to Google (**SEO: job pages whose description is detected as a language other than English become `noindex, follow`, leave `sitemap-jobs.xml` and the Indexing API queue, and lose their JobPosting JSON-LD on company pages; the Jooble feed also leaves them out. Pages stay live and in site search. No hreflang on job pages.**). **Decided** by the owner (2026-11-08): keep importing every six-figure job, but ask Google to index English postings only. **Why:** most of the newest jobs were German postings on an English site (41 of the newest 96 had a German gender tag like "(m/w/d)"), many near-identical agency ads per city (Assistenzarzt in Cottbus, Gera, Schleiz, Hamburg...), a likely reason the site stays "Crawled - currently not indexed". **How:** `evaluateJobIndexability` (`lib/jobs/qualityGate.ts`), the one gate behind the page's robots tag, the job sitemaps, the Indexing API safety checks, company-page JobPosting and the Jooble feed, now fails `non_english` when `detectJobLanguage` names another language. An undetected language counts as English, as on the job page. 1 new test.

- Homepage on phones: about 28% shorter (**SEO: CSS only; every link and job stays in the HTML, items past the fifth are hidden on phones**). **Found** in the 2026-11-08 audit: on an iPhone the homepage was ~13,070px tall, two job lists one after the other (live feed ~1,790px, "Latest" ~2,060px) and one tall card per row in Top Locations and Premium Roles (~1,600px each). **What** (phones only): the live feed (up to 480px wide) and "Latest" list (up to 767px) show 5 jobs each, with their "All new jobs" / "Explore all" links; Top Locations and Premium Roles use two compact columns, without the role illustrations (they covered the role names at that width) and the corner arrows (the whole card is the link). Previewed by injecting the same CSS into the live page on an iPhone 13: ~9,380px.

- Footer: the "Get weekly six-figure jobs in your inbox" form is replaced by links to the public RSS feeds (**SEO: two footer links to `/feeds/jobs.rss` and `/feeds/remote-jobs.rss` (noindex) replace a form; no other change**). **Found** in the 2026-11-08 audit: the form only saved the address in the visitor's own browser (`localStorage`) and then said "Subscribed (saved on this device)"; nothing was stored or sent. Real email alerts need a sending provider, a subscriber table, double opt-in and unsubscribe, which wait on the owner's choices. Until then the footer says alerts are coming and offers the feeds, which work today.

- Role pages (`/jobs/<role>`) respond faster: their role-wide queries are cached (**SEO: no output change; the numbers on the page can be up to 5 minutes old, the same freshness as the facets already cached there**). **Why:** the 2026-11-08 audit measured 1.3-1.6s to first byte on repeat visits and 2.1-3.1s cold (software-engineer, product-manager, data-engineer, devops-engineer). Hub pages render per request (the build cannot reach the database), and the role page ran, on every request and one after another: up to 2,000 rows of skills, a top-employers `groupBy`, a company lookup, the job list, and a count and average in `generateMetadata`. **What:** with `memoAsync` (5 minutes, in process, the pattern the page already used for facets and insights): skills and top employers in one entry per role, the two queries in parallel; the job list per exact filter set; the metadata count and average per role.

- Job JSON-LD: a euro job whose location ends in a euro-area country code gets that country instead of the Germany fallback (**SEO: changes `jobLocation.address.addressCountry` for euro jobs with no stored country whose location ends in a code like "HR"; all other JobPosting output unchanged**). **Found** in the 2026-11-08 audit: "Kerestinec, HR" (Croatia) was published to Google as DE by the last-resort "euro means Germany" guess. Removing the guess would drop `jobLocation` (and Google Jobs eligibility) for jobs it gets right, like "Schwetzingen, Rhein-Neckar-Kreis", so it stays as the last resort. Two-letter codes are still not read from free text in general. 1 new test.

- Site audit fixes (**SEO: no URL, canonical, metadata or JSON-LD change; visible salaries now match the salaries the site already publishes**). **Audit** (2026-11-08): Playwright crawl of 26 key pages at desktop and iPhone widths (console errors, failed requests, overflow, headings, meta, JSON-LD), all 667 internal links (all 200; one redirect), screenshots. **Fixed:** (1) React hydration error #418 on `/` and `/remote`: `JobCard` renders "Posted 15 hours ago" from the clock, so cached server HTML and the browser disagreed; the label now has `suppressHydrationWarning`. (2) The "Top Companies Hiring Now" logo wall was filled up with the companies that post the most jobs, which put recruitment agencies on it ("rocket match powered by notificAI GmbH", TROVA, Orion Placement, True Talent...); it now shows only the two hand-picked lists (one query fewer). (3) Salaries hidden on cards: `buildSalaryText` kept its own old minimums (EUR 90k, GBP 85k) while the site publishes from EUR 80k (from EUR 60k at the bottom under the top-of-range rule) and GBP 75k, so a EUR 68-81k job showed no pay and EUR 70-90k showed "Up to EUR 90K". It now uses `getSalaryFloorAnnual`, the publication gate's floor. (4) Job pages scrolled sideways on phones (63-81px): the grid had no column rule below `lg`, so its one column grew to the sidebar's untruncated company name; it is now `minmax(0,1fr)`. 5 new tests.

- Jobs get a real role and country far more often (**SEO: changes which listing pages jobs appear on — role, role+country, country and city pages gain jobs. No URL, canonical, metadata or JSON-LD template change. Under indexing phase 1 (a hand-picked list) no new pages become indexable.**). **Asked:** fix the role and country labels for all jobs and for new ones. **Found** (2026-11-08, via `/api/jobs`): 4,496 of ~5,000 live jobs had the catch-all role `other`, and many on-site jobs had no `countryCode` (Hamburg, Chicago, Irvine, Redwood City...). **Roles** (`lib/normalizers/role.ts`): the whole title had to be a role page, so "Lead Data Engineer" (`lead-data-engineer`) and "Product Manager" (`manager-product-manager`, "manager" read as a seniority) were `other`. Now it falls back to the base role, then to a word classifier that maps titles to existing role pages only. Leadership and manager titles map only to leadership and manager roles ("Director, Data Analytics" stays `other`). "Member of Technical Staff" is no longer read as the staff level. Titles with no role page (physicians, construction, mechanical and electrical engineering) stay `other`. On 600 sampled `other` titles: 390 now get a role. **Countries** (`processLocation` in `lib/ingest/index.ts`): the location parser took the last segment as the country ("Irvine, California" → "California" → none). For on-site and hybrid jobs it now also reads the location text the way job pages do, then a city we have a page for, then a national pay currency (USD, CAD, GBP, AUD, CHF). City and currency must agree ("Birmingham, Jefferson County" in USD stays empty), and the euro never names a country. Remote jobs are unchanged. This replaces the 2026-11-07 rule "no country from a city or currency alone". On 146 sampled on-site jobs without a country: 132 filled, none disagree with the country their job page's JSON-LD already states. **Existing jobs:** `scripts/remediate-data-quality.ts` fills missing roles and countries on live jobs with the same functions. It only fills gaps, never changes an existing label, and does not touch `updatedAt` (run-expiry uses it). It now runs before "Refresh slices" in the freehire sync so the slices include the new labels. Tested on a local Postgres: correct rows changed, a second run changes nothing. 12 new and updated tests.

- Homepage "Latest" list: the per-country cap now uses the job JSON-LD's country inference (**SEO: no URL, metadata or JSON-LD output change; `inferCountryFromJob` in `lib/seo/jobJsonLd.ts` is only exported**). **Found** checking the live homepage after the previous fix deployed (2026-11-08): it still opened with 4 German jobs. Via `/api/jobs`, three had `countryCode` DE and one had none with location "Hamburg, Hamburg", which names no country, so reading the country from the location text was not enough. Many of the newest jobs have no `countryCode` (Hamburg, Chicago, San Francisco, Palo Alto...). `marketOf` now calls `inferCountryFromJob` (stored code, location text, known US cities, currency, city page, euro), the same function that gives these job pages their JSON-LD country. Simulated on the live newest jobs: at most 3 per country. Test updated with the live cases.

- Homepage "Latest" list: the 3-per-market cap now also counts jobs whose country is only in their location text (**SEO: no URL, metadata or JSON-LD change**). **Found** checking the live homepage after the cap shipped (2026-11-08): the list opened with 4 German doctor jobs. Two had `countryCode` DE and two had no `countryCode` (location "Cottbus, Brandenburg, Germany", "Hamburg ... Germany"), so they counted as a separate "unknown" market. `marketOf` now falls back to `countryFromText(locationRaw)`, the same parser the job JSON-LD uses. 1 new test.

- Lessons from WeWorkRemotely: public RSS feeds and a market-balanced homepage list (**SEO: feeds are `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`; adds two `<link rel="alternate" type="application/rss+xml">` tags to every page's head; no canonical, metadata or JSON-LD change**). **Checked** 2026-11-08: weworkremotely.com pages answer automated visitors with a Cloudflare check / 403 (not circumvented), so the comparison uses its public RSS feed (`/remote-jobs.rss`): its newest 100 jobs are exactly 10 from each of 10 categories, every job states where you may work from ("Anywhere in the World" 63, "USA Only" 28, "Latin America Only" 6) and its type (Full-Time 88, Contract 12), and it publishes per-category feeds. **Added:** `/feeds/jobs.rss` and `/feeds/remote-jobs.rss` (RSS 2.0, the 100 newest public jobs, items titled "Company: Job title" with pay, place and work arrangement, linking to the job page; cached 30 minutes, CDN 1 hour; 503 if the database is unavailable), linked from every page's head for feed readers. **Homepage:** "Latest Six Figure Opportunities" now takes at most 3 jobs per market (country; remote counts as one) as well as 2 per employer, newest first; if the newest jobs are too uniform it fills the rest without the market cap so the list is never short. The homepage reads the newest 80 jobs (was 40) to have variety to choose from. Selection moved to `lib/jobs/latestSelection.ts`. 7 new tests. **Not done (needs data first):** category-balanced sections and "where can I work from" labels: about 4,500 of ~5,000 jobs have the catch-all role `other`, and remote jobs carry no reliable region field yet.

- "Top roles" rows no longer link the catch-all "Other" role (**SEO: removes one internal link per listing header, to `/jobs/other`; no other change**). **Found** checking the live pages after the rows shipped: "Other" (stored `roleSlug` `other`, a canonical slug) was the first chip on `/jobs`, `/jobs/100k-plus`, `/jobs/location/canada`, `/remote` and `/companies`, because it holds the most jobs. It is not a role anyone browses for, so `rankTopRolePages` now skips it. 1 new test.

- Workflows: a manual "google indexing (manual)" workflow (`.github/workflows/google-indexing.yml`) that sends job URLs to the Google Indexing API without running a freehire sync (**SEO: job URL submissions only; no page output changed**). **Why:** the indexing queue was only drained after a successful freehire sync, so on 2026-11-08 a sync stopped by freehire's API quota (10,000 requests a day, used up by a second manual sync whose closed-job check looks up every live job) also stopped the indexing test after the service account was added as a Search Console owner. **What:** optionally queues the newest live job URLs from the job sitemap (`scripts/notify-google-indexing.ts`, default 180, the queue skips duplicates and unsafe URLs), then drains the queue (`scripts/process-google-indexing-queue.ts`, at most 180 a day, only this step gets the Google credentials). Shares the freehire concurrency group, so it never runs during a sync.

- Thin pages: more real content on `/about`, `/companies` and `/remote/<role>` (**SEO: adds on-page text, internal links and one FAQPage JSON-LD on `/companies` page 1; titles, descriptions, canonicals and robots unchanged**). **Why:** an on-page audit of 104 live URLs (2026-11-08) found titles, meta descriptions, canonicals and H1s complete (one H1 per page; all descriptions present and 70-160 chars; 3 blog titles slightly over 65 chars), but `/about` (99 words), `/companies` (109 words, an always-indexable hub) and the remote role pages (~280-310 words, about half of `/jobs/<role>`) were thin. **`/about`:** where listings come from (company career sites and their applicant tracking systems, daily), what counts as $100k+ per currency (a table rendered from `HIGH_SALARY_THRESHOLDS`, so it cannot drift), how listings stay current (closed and 7-day-stale jobs removed, nothing older than `MAX_DISPLAY_AGE_DAYS`), and a fixed "Last updated" date instead of one that always showed today. **`/companies`** (page 1 only, so `?page=N` does not repeat it): an "About this directory" paragraph with live totals, the six largest employers by open jobs, a "Browse by role" row, and a 3-question FAQ with matching FAQPage JSON-LD. **`/remote/<role>`:** a "Remote <role> jobs at a glance" section with live numbers (remote jobs of all jobs for the role, the top employer countries), a link to the role's hybrid/on-site page, related remote roles from the same field, and the typical-salary card for remote roles. Every number comes from live counts (cached); on failure the section is left out.

- Listing headers: a "Top roles" link row on salary tier, country, city, level, category, skill, remote and `/jobs` pages (**SEO: adds internal links from these hubs to existing canonical role pages (`/remote/<role>` on remote pages); no new URLs, canonicals, metadata or JSON-LD**). **Asked:** list top roles as internal links in the header of pages like `/jobs/100k-plus`. **What:** `lib/roles/topRolePages.ts` groups the page's own listings (same filters, no paging) by role, folds seniority variants into the base role ("senior-software-engineer" counts toward Software Engineer), keeps only roles with a canonical page and 2+ jobs, and shows the top 8 as "Top roles at $100k–$199k", "Top roles in Canada", "Top Senior roles", "Top roles using Python", "Top remote roles" and so on. The links carry no count, because a role page lists all of that role's jobs, not only this page's slice. One cached `groupBy` per page variant; on failure the row is left out. Also: the "Typical salary" card leaves out empty bands (on the $100k–$199k page it showed "$200k–$300k 0" and "$300k+ 0"). 3 new tests.

- Homepage numbers: salary tier cards now show what their pages list, and the salary panel recounts hourly (**no SEO change: tier pages, their URLs, titles and metadata are unchanged**). **Asked:** make sure the numbers are true and up to date on every page. **Checked live (2026-11-08)** against each page's own count: totals (homepage 5,033 / `/jobs` 5,044 while a sync was adding jobs), country rows (US 2,455 vs `/jobs/location/united-states` 2,483, UK 149/151, Canada 414/421, Australia 27/27), work types (Remote 1,106/1,123, Hybrid 50/50, On-site 3,527/3,617; no job is counted in two types), salary bands (1,107 + 1,357 + 1,125 + 232 = the 3,821 listings of the median) and `/companies` (172 companies with 5+ jobs, 5,044 jobs at 2,035 companies) all agree apart from timing. **Wrong:** the "Browse by Salary Tier" cards said "$100k+ Jobs 4,846" and "$200k+ Jobs 2,207" but open `/jobs/100k-plus` ("$100k - $199k Jobs", 1,818) and `/jobs/200k-plus` ("$200k - $299k Jobs", 1,550): the cards counted everything at or above the level in any currency (C$200k counted as "$200k+"), the pages list one USD band each, by the top of the pay range. **Now** both use `tierQueryInput()` (`lib/jobs/salaryTiers.ts`) and the cards are labelled with the page's range ($100k–$199k, $200k–$299k, $300k–$399k, $400k+), so each card's number is its page's number. The panel's open "$300k+" band opened the $300k–$399k page (leaving out $400k+ jobs); it now opens `/jobs?minSalary=300000`. The country and job-type counts (homepage panel, Remote hub chips) are recounted every hour instead of every 6, so they trail the live pages by at most an hour. 3 new tests (`salaryTierQuery.test.ts`); the site-counts test follows the 1-hour refresh.

- Workflows: the freehire daily sync now also refreshes the listing slices, drains the Google Indexing queue (at most 180 URLs a day) and runs the data-quality self-heal (**SEO: restores job submissions to the Google Indexing API, which had stopped; no page output changed**). **Why:** those three steps lived in the "Scheduled Job Scraper" workflow (`daily-scrape.yml`), which GitHub disabled for inactivity on 2026-10-08. Job intake had already moved to freehire, so no job was being sent to Google and the slices were not being rebuilt. The old scraper stays off (freehire covers job intake; running both risks duplicates). Only the indexing step receives `GOOGLE_INDEXING_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`; each new step runs whenever the sync succeeded and has a 10-minute timeout; the job timeout goes from 45 to 60 minutes. **Known blocker:** in the scraper's last run the Indexing API refused every request with "Quota exceeded ... Publish requests per day" for Google Cloud project 267439231629, so submissions will keep failing until that project's quota and the service account's Search Console ownership are checked.

- Added Google Analytics 4 (gtag.js) to every page via `next/script` (`afterInteractive`) in `app/layout.tsx`, switched on by the env var `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA_MEASUREMENT_ID` (**no SEO change**: no metadata, canonical or JSON-LD touched; the existing CSP already allows https scripts and connections). Nothing loads until the variable is set to a `G-` id; any other value is ignored, so nothing else can be written into the page. The variable is inlined at build time, so set it in Railway and redeploy. Also added the IndexNow key file `public/b18913791d2cd3d249bb2becc6d3cf15.txt` (served at the site root, as IndexNow requires) so SEOConsole can submit new and changed job URLs to Bing and the other IndexNow engines; Google does not use IndexNow.

- Added Microsoft Clarity tracking (project `zagoogkx9a`) to every page via `next/script` (`afterInteractive`) in `app/layout.tsx` (**no SEO change**: no metadata, canonical or JSON-LD touched; the existing CSP already allows `https://www.clarity.ms`).

- Layout fixes for tablets, foldables and short landscape screens (**no SEO change: CSS/class changes only**). From an audit of 11 pages on 21 viewports (Galaxy Z Flip 7 and Z Fold 7 inner and cover screens, iPhone 17 / 17 Pro Max, Pixel 10, iPad Mini, iPad Pro 11, Galaxy Tab S9, portrait and landscape, plus desktop): no page overflowed sideways, but: (1) **Feedback button over the Apply button** on job pages from 768 to 1023px (iPads, Fold inner screen, landscape phones): the mobile Apply bar shows below 1024px while the compact, tuck-away Feedback icon only applied below 768px, so the 116x40 pill covered ~2/3 of "Apply"; the compact rules now apply below 1024px and on screens under 500px tall (`FeedbackWidget.module.css`). (2) **`/search` form pinned over most of short screens** (69% of a Flip 7 cover screen turned sideways, 26% on desktop): sticky only from 1024px wide and 800px tall, under the pinned header (`app/search/page.tsx`). (3) **Job page Apply sidebar slid under the header** (stuck 24px from the top under a 59-81px header): now 72px / 96px (`app/job/[slug]/page.tsx`). (4) **Company names cut mid-letter on `/companies`** on 360-420px phones ("Microsof", "Anthropi"): one tile per row up to 420px, words wrap if still too long (`CompanySearch.module.css`). (5) **Header pinned on short landscape screens** (with the Apply bar ~45% of a 308px-tall screen): the header scrolls away below 500px tall (`SiteHeader.module.css`). (6) **`/search` filters misaligned at 768-1023px** ("Work arrangement" wrapping): 3 columns there, 5 from 1024px, selects bottom-aligned (`SearchForm.module.css`). Also: listing chips and pagination are 44px tall on any touch screen (`pointer: coarse`), not only below 768px; the filter help text goes from 11px to 12.5px. Checked on the production build: Feedback is the 44px icon on iPad Mini and Fold 7, the header is not pinned on a sideways Flip 7, desktop unchanged; the new Tailwind classes are in the built CSS.

- Homepage: well-known companies first in the logo wall, a "6figjobs today" numbers strip, and no job shown twice (**no SEO change: no URLs, metadata or JSON-LD touched; links go to existing company pages**). **Why:** the owner asked for big-company logos (a saved list, not recomputed daily) and freehire-style numbers. **Logos:** `lib/constants/topCompanies.ts` holds 30 hand-picked well-known employers that have a 6figjobs company page (checked against /companies on 2026-11-08: Google, Microsoft, OpenAI, Anthropic, LinkedIn, SpaceX, Ford, ServiceNow, Snowflake, Databricks, ...). They lead "Top Companies Hiring Now", each only while it has live $100k+ jobs (one company lookup + one job count; a failure just leaves them out), followed by the existing priority and by-volume companies. The list is not labelled "Fortune 500" because membership was not verified. **Numbers:** `SiteStatsStrip` under the logo wall shows $100k+ jobs, companies hiring, new this week, countries with jobs and typical (median) salary, all from counts the page already loads; a number whose query failed is left out. Lucide icons per number. **Duplicates:** "Latest Six Figure Opportunities" now skips jobs already in the "New jobs, live" feed above it (the same job appeared in both, once as "7 hr ago" (added) and once as "Posted 4 days ago"). Strip checked at 1280px, Galaxy Z Fold 7 and Z Flip 7 cover screen: no overflow, numbers aligned. Full jest suite and `next build` pass.

- Search: live suggestions in the header too, role pages in the suggestions, and header and homepage searches now agree (**SEO: no indexable output changed; `/search` stays as it was for robots/canonical**). **Why:** the owner asked for freehire-style live search. A review found the header search was a plain form and returned different results from the homepage box: "react" gave 1 job on `/search` and 84 on `/jobs?q=`. **Root cause:** `/search` turned free text into guessed role filters (`parseSearchQuery`: "react" -> frontend + mobile engineer, "python" -> backend engineer, "data engineer" -> software engineer + data engineer + recruiter) and dropped the words. **Now** `/search` keeps its country, remote, seniority, salary and contract filters and sends the remaining words to the same keyword search as the homepage box (`leftoverTokens(q, [])`: "senior react developer remote" -> keyword "react developer", senior, remote); only an explicit `?role=` filters by role. **Suggestions** (`HeroSearchInput`, now also in `SiteHeader`): up to 3 matching role pages with job counts above the jobs ("prod man" -> Product Manager), company logos on job rows (white tile, initials fallback), the 6 biggest roles when the empty box is focused, and "/" focuses the box (the hero on the homepage, the header elsewhere) with a key-cap hint on desktop. `/api/jobs/suggest` returns `roles` (same counts as the role pages; one cached query moved to `lib/roles/roleSlugCounts.ts`) and `logo` per job. **Checked** in Chromium against the production build with stubbed suggestions: "/" focuses, empty focus lists popular roles, typing shows Roles / Jobs / See all, ArrowDown+Enter opens the role page, Enter submits to `/search?q=`; no horizontal overflow on iPhone 17 or Galaxy Z Flip 7 cover screen. 3 new tests (role matching, suggest roles); full jest suite passes.

- Role pages: a "Trending in <field>" chip row under Senior / Staff / Principal (**SEO: adds internal links between existing canonical role pages; no new URLs, canonicals, metadata or JSON-LD changed**). **Why:** the owner asked for related roles from every field, not just seniority levels for software engineering. **What:** `/jobs/<role>` shows up to 8 other roles in the same field (e.g. Marketing roles on marketing pages, Sales roles on sales pages), each with its live job count, ordered by jobs posted in the last 30 days on 6figjobs (our own listings, not an outside trend source). Only canonical role pages with at least 3 live jobs get a chip (the role-page index floor), and counts use the same `roleSlug` match as the role page, so a chip's number matches the page it opens. **How:** `SEARCH_ROLE_OPTIONS` entries now carry the field they were already grouped under (`category`, previously unset; the only reader, `RoleTypeahead`, is unused); options that are not canonical slugs link to their canonical page via the label (`ml-engineer` -> `machine-learning-engineer`) or a fuzzy match only when it extends the same slug (`product-marketing` -> `product-marketing-manager`; loose matches such as `art-director` -> `cto` are rejected); 18 indexed roles with no search option (SRE, MLOps, CTO, UX Designer, ...) are listed explicitly, so all 62 tier-1 role pages get a row. One cached `groupBy roleSlug` query (all time + last 30 days) serves every role page; if it fails the row is left out. "Other specialized roles" (a mixed group) get no row. 7 new tests in `__tests__/lib/relatedRoles.test.ts`.

- Motion: borrowed shadcnspace's hover/press/scroll-reveal patterns as plain CSS, with no new dependency (**no SEO change**). **What:** JobCard gets press feedback (`scale: 0.99` on `:active`), homepage job rows get a background tint on hover, and a new `reveal-on-scroll` utility in `app/globals.css` fades in the homepage job rows and the Top Locations / Salary Tier / Premium Roles modules as they scroll into view. **How:** the reveal is a CSS scroll-driven animation (`animation-timeline: view()`) inside `@supports`, so browsers without support show content as is, anything on screen at load is fully visible (no LCP or hidden-HTML risk), and it is off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. It animates `translate`, so the existing hover lift still works. **Checked:** in Chromium against the production-built CSS: on-screen element opacity 1 at load, below-fold element 0 → 1 when scrolled into view, hover still lifts 3px, reduced motion leaves everything static. `npm run typecheck`, eslint on changed files and `next build` pass. **Docs:** motion rules and the twice-monthly design-refresh process added to `docs/DESIGN_UX_SPEC.md` §11–12. shadcnspace is MIT licensed; no code was copied.

- Job sitemap: the database read now follows the cap (**no change at the default setting; SEO effect only once `SEO_CORE_JOB_URLS_PER_SHARD` is set**). **Why:** the job sitemap lists the newest `SEO_CORE_JOB_URLS_PER_SHARD` indexable jobs (default 20,000, not set in production), but `app/sitemap-jobs/[page]/route.ts` always read the newest 20,000 rows, each with its full description, and only then cut the list to the cap. With a cap of 5,000 the read would still have been 20,000 rows, and the same read took 4.6 s for about 4,000 jobs on 2026-11-07, so it grows with the job count (the earlier sitemap timeouts and 502s were this kind of load). **Now** the read is `min(20,000, 1.5 x cap)` rows, the 1.5 leaving room for rows the indexability checks drop (about 5% were dropped on 2026-11-07: 4,054 listed of 4,283 published). The list is still the newest jobs by `updatedAt`, cut to the cap. 2 new tests: the cap keeps the newest and lists no more than it allows; the read size is 7,500 for a cap of 5,000 and 20,000 for the default or a cap of 20,000 (the read test fails with the old fixed read). **Not changed:** the cap only limits what is *submitted* in the sitemap; job pages stay `index, follow` and stay reachable through the paginated job lists, so Google can still find jobs beyond the cap, and keeping them out of the index would need `noindex`, a separate change; phase-2 sharding is unchanged (each shard is still read as a page of 20,000 or fewer rows, so a cap below 20,000 per shard already left gaps between shards, as before). **To use it:** set `SEO_CORE_JOB_URLS_PER_SHARD` (suggested 5,000, a little above today's 4,054, so nothing changes today and the sitemap stops growing when the freehire paging fix adds jobs).

- Fix the freehire sync stopping after the first short page, which hid most European and Swiss jobs (**SEO: no markup, canonical, hreflang or robots change, but it adds many job URLs to the job sitemap; read Limits before merging**). **Found by:** measuring freehire's public API on 2026-11-07. Its pages are routinely shorter than 100 rows with many pages still to come (EUR query: 99, 95, 100, 100, 100 rows back to 23 Oct; CHF: 96, 100, 99, 99, 98, 99, 95, then 13; Spain: 99, 99, 100, 98, 96, 100, 35). `collectFreehireJobs` ended a market at the first page with fewer than 100 rows, so each market was read one page deep: the 8-day sync of 2026-11-07 logged EUR `fetched=99`, CHF `fetched=96`, AUD `fetched=77` for windows that hold many times that. **Now** a market ends on an empty page, on reaching the total freehire reports, or after two pages made up only of jobs older than the window. **Measured with correct paging, last 30 days, jobs that pass freehire's freshness checks and the euro rule above:** CHF 584 (the sync had read 96), the whole EU region 2,638 (it had read 99), and for 9 euro countries 2,691 (Germany 1,691, France 590, Netherlands 124, Ireland 97, Spain 71, Italy 30, Portugal 26, Austria 45, Belgium 17). For comparison the site lists Germany 54 and Switzerland 94 today. These are pools before our own checks, so the site will list fewer. 3 tests rewritten or added (keeps paging past a short page, stops after two all-old pages, completes at the reported total); the paging test fails with the old rule. **Limits and risks:** (1) **SEO and indexing:** the job sitemap lists every indexable job (one shard, up to 20,000 URLs), so this takes the site from about 4,400 jobs towards 7,000 or more; `lib/seo/indexingPhase.ts` records that Google previously crawled about 20,000 URLs and indexed only the homepage, and that adding crawlable pages made "Crawled - currently not indexed" worse, so consider capping what the sitemap lists before or with this change; (2) the daily sync reads a 1.5-day window, which is now at most a few extra requests per market, but a backfill reads far more than before and an 8-day run already took 18 minutes, so a 30-day backfill can exceed the 45-minute workflow limit and should be done in steps; (3) the page-size rule was also used by my own earlier measuring scripts, so the figures in the earlier freehire entries (96 Swiss jobs, 79 euro jobs, the per-country tables) are lower bounds, not the true pool; (4) not run against production yet; the first sync after the merge is the real test, and the real collector cannot be run in the development sandbox (its proxy rejects axios).

- Euro jobs now count when the TOP of their salary range reaches the threshold (**SEO: no canonical, hreflang, robots, sitemap or JSON-LD change; more euro job pages and country-page listings, see Limits**). **Why:** the owner asked for more European jobs, and most European employers publish a range, not a flat figure; a job paying EUR 70-95k never qualified at EUR 80k because only the bottom of the range was checked at publication. **What was already true:** every later stage already accepted either end of a range (the quality gate says so in a comment, `checkHighSalary` and `validateHighSalaryEligibility` do it, so does the eligibility query), and ranges wider than 3x are still rejected by validation. The only blocks were `getMinimumSalaryRejection` (the publication gate, which looked at the bottom only) and freehire's own mapper and query. **Now:** `lib/currency/thresholds.ts` defines the rule once: `TOP_OF_RANGE_CURRENCIES` (EUR only) and `TOP_OF_RANGE_MIN_SHARE` (0.75), so a euro range qualifies when its top is at least EUR 80,000 and its bottom is at least EUR 60,000; every other currency still needs the bottom of the range at its threshold. The gate takes `salaryMax` (ingest now passes it, so it applies to every source, not only freehire), and freehire's query uses the lower floor (EUR 60,000) because it can only filter on the bottom. **Measured on freehire's public API, jobs posted in the last 30 days that pass freehire's own freshness checks, with the real mapper:** 9 euro countries 1,670 -> 2,691 (Germany 1,138 -> 1,691, France 286 -> 590, Netherlands 67 -> 124, Ireland 66 -> 97, Spain 38 -> 71, Italy 15 -> 30, Portugal 14 -> 26, Austria 35 -> 45, Belgium 11 -> 17). **Correction:** the freehire figures in two earlier entries (96 Swiss jobs; 207 euro jobs; the per-country tables) were lower bounds, because my measuring loop stopped at the first page shorter than 100 rows, and so does the real scraper (fixed in a separate change). These are pools before our own checks (company-name repair, duplicate handling, indexability), so the site will list fewer. 5 new gate tests and 3 new freehire mapper tests; the EUR case in the old bottom-only freehire test now says the range top counts. **Limits:** euro only (GBP, CHF and the Nordic currencies are unchanged, not measured for this); the 0.75 floor is my choice, not the owner's (in my first, undercounted sample about a quarter more qualified without it, but a range like EUR 40-90k would then be listed); a job is still listed only with a published, validated salary, and the displayed range is the employer's, so a EUR 70-95k job shows as EUR 70-95k.

- More European jobs from freehire: Swiss jobs are fetched, and jobs whose location has no country name now get a country (**SEO: no canonical, hreflang, robots, sitemap or JSON-LD change**; Germany's and other country pages list more jobs, Switzerland's page stays `noindex` because it is outside the phase-1 country list). **Found by measuring** freehire's public API on 2026-11-07 for jobs posted in the last 30 days that pass our rules (published yearly salary at or above the currency threshold, not closed, not flagged stale): (1) `CHF` was not one of the markets fetched, although it has a threshold (CHF 90,000): 96 listable Swiss jobs, none ingested, which is why `/jobs/location/switzerland` showed 0. Added as a sixth market (`lib/scrapers/freehire.ts`). Sweden, Norway and Denmark have thresholds too but freehire had 1, 1 and 1 listable jobs, so they are not added. (2) The country was only read when the location ended in a country name. Of 79 listable EUR jobs, 49 got no country code (`Nürnberg, Bayern`, `Heidelberg, Baden-Württemberg`, `Frankfurt am Main, Hessen`), and all 96 CHF jobs would have got none (`Zürich, Kanton Zürich`, `Dällikon`), so those jobs never appeared on a country page. New `lib/location/subdivisionCountry.ts`, used by `processLocation` in `lib/ingest/index.ts` only when no country name is found: a German or Austrian state or a Swiss canton (`Kanton X`, `Canton of X`, `Canton de X`) gives its country, and a Swiss-franc salary with an unplaceable location gives Switzerland. Re-measured on the live data with the fix: EUR jobs with a country 30 -> 59 of 79, CHF 0 -> 96 of 96. Only the last comma segment is read and ambiguous names (`Berlin`, `Vienna`, `Bremen`, `Hamburg`, `Munich` on their own) are left alone, so no US town is turned into a German one. `processSalary` keeps its own country lookup unchanged, so Greenhouse salary parsing is not affected. `processLocation` is now exported for its test. 40 new tests (35 for the helper, 5 for `processLocation` end to end); 3 of the 5 `processLocation` tests fail without the fix, the other 2 guard unchanged behaviour; the freehire test that said CHF is skipped is replaced by two: unsupported currencies are still skipped, and CHF is published from 90,000 and not below. **Limits:** existing jobs that already have no country get one only when they are re-read (freehire's daily sync re-reads about 36 hours; older ones need a backfill, not run here); about 20 of 79 EUR jobs still have no country (bare `Vienna` and `Munich`, French regions, and the `"Munich, Germany; Germany"` format); the real `collectFreehireJobs` could not be run in the development sandbox (its HTTPS proxy rejects axios), so the Swiss flow was checked with an equivalent fetch-based run plus unit tests, and the first production sync is the real test; freehire's 30-day Swiss pool (96) is the ceiling, so expect on the order of 100 Swiss jobs, not thousands. **Found, not changed:** the existing location normalizer returns Germany for `Berlin, NH` (a US town).

- Manual workflow `add european companies` (`.github/workflows/add-european-companies.yml`) to run `scripts/addEuropeanCompanies.ts` from GitHub Actions, so it can be run without the Railway command line. **Dry run unless the `apply` box is ticked**; it uses the same production-database fingerprint guard and `DATABASE_URL` secret as the migrate, freehire and company-publishing-report workflows, runs only when started by hand, and writes nothing in dry-run mode. No site code, SEO markup or schema change.

- Script to onboard European employers' job boards: `scripts/addEuropeanCompanies.ts` (list and decision logic in `lib/companies/europeanBoards.ts`). **Why:** European coverage is thin because only boards already in the Company table are scraped; the production table cannot be written from the development environment, so this is a script to run. **Does:** adds 11 Greenhouse boards (Elastic, Deliveroo, Airbnb, Okta, Algolia, Grafana Labs, SumUp, GoCardless, Monzo, Pinterest, Asana), chosen because a measured sample of their public postings on 2026-11-07 had European-located jobs with a salary figure in the text (rough text match, not the app's salary check). It checks each board live first, creates a Company row (or sets the board on an existing row that has no ATS yet), skips one that is already set up, and never overrides a row that points at another board or ATS. **Dry run by default; nothing is written without `--apply`:** `railway run --service six-figure-jobs npx tsx scripts/addEuropeanCompanies.ts`, then again with `--apply`. Run with a stubbed database against the live Greenhouse API: with Elastic already set up, Grafana Labs with no ATS and Monzo on Lever, it planned 8 creates, 1 attach and 2 skips and, with `--apply`, made exactly those writes. 18 new tests for the list and the decision logic. **Limits:** not run against the production database, so which of the 11 already exist there is unknown; the salary rule is unchanged, so a posting is still listed only with a published, validated salary at or above its currency threshold, and most of these boards' European postings will not pass it; the next daily ATS scrape fetches the new rows, and a row's jobs appear only after that run.

- European postings stay in their original language, with a "Translate to English" link (**SEO: no canonical, hreflang, robots, sitemap or JSON-LD change**). **Why:** the owner asked for jobs from across Europe in their own language (Spanish jobs in Spanish, and so on) plus a way to read them in English. **Now:** (1) new `lib/jobs/language.ts` tells which language a description is written in from its common words (English, Spanish, German, French, Dutch, Italian, Portuguese, Polish, Swedish, Danish, Norwegian; text mostly in a non-Latin script comes back as `other`; anything else, or text too short to judge, comes back as nothing and the page treats it as English). No dependency and no database column, so no Prisma migration; nothing is translated or stored. (2) On `/job/<slug>`, a non-English posting gets "This job is posted in <language>. Translate to English" at the top of the article, and its description is wrapped in `lang="<code>"` (no `lang` for `other`). The link opens Google Translate on the same public page in a new tab (`rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"`), so the requirements are translated with the rest; it needs no JavaScript and works in any browser. The page's own English headings keep the page language. (3) Belgium, Austria and Portugal now have country pages: Belgium and Austria were already in `TARGET_COUNTRIES` but had no slug, so the country-page list skipped them (Poland, which has no slug either, still returns 404; Belgium answered with a redirect I did not trace before this change, and Belgium, Austria and Portugal all answer 200 after it); Portugal is added to both lists. These pages follow the existing rule (indexable only with enough jobs and in the phase-1 country list), so they are `noindex` and stay out of the sitemap for now. **Checked on real data, not only samples:** run on the public Greenhouse boards of 36 European employers, it labelled Scout24 18 of 19 German, SumUp's French (39) and Italian (48) postings, and Deliveroo's Paris postings (an English company blurb followed by a French body) as French; this found and fixed an Italian and a Portuguese word-list gap and added the non-Latin result. 24 new tests (language, plus the notice's link, target and rel). The salary rule is unchanged: a job still needs a published, validated salary at or above its currency threshold, which is why European counts stay modest (checked live: Germany 38, France 26, Ireland 8, Netherlands 7, Spain 6, Sweden 0, Switzerland 0). **Limits:** Finnish, Croatian and other languages not in the list get no label or button; the language is read from the description only, so a posting whose body is English under a foreign title is treated as English; the requirement bullets and AI summary above the description are not wrapped in `lang`, only the full description is; the Translate link depends on Google Translate's web-page translation, which I confirmed redirects to `translate.goog` and answers 200 today but is a Google product that can change; Poland and the Czech Republic are not added because the threshold table has no PLN or CZK entry (their jobs can never pass the salary rule) and a threshold is the owner's call. **Found, not changed:** the site-wide exclusion `title not contains 'intern'` is a substring match, so titles with "International", "Internal" or "Internet" are dropped (none of the 4,054 job URLs in the live job sitemap has "intern" in its slug).

- Fix the 502s on company and role Open Graph images, and stop the pSEO sitemap timing out right after a deploy (**no SEO markup, canonical, hreflang, robots or JSON-LD change; the sitemap contents are unchanged**). **Found from:** Search Console showed Google barely crawling the site (0 impressions in 56 days; the homepage's last crawl was a 5xx), and Railway's logs showed two separate server faults. (1) **OG images:** `/company/<slug>/opengraph-image` and `/jobs/<role>/opengraph-image` threw `Expected <div> to have explicit "display: flex"... if it has more than one child node` while the response was streaming (logged as `failed to pipe response`, answered by the proxy as a 502 after `connection closed unexpectedly`). Cause: text such as `{jobCount} open roles`, `~${avgSalaryK}k avg salary` and `{countryCode} · Tech company` is several child nodes in a div without `display: flex`; each is now one template string. Checked by rendering the real company, role and job route functions with the real `next/og` (database mocked): the original company and role routes fail with that error, the fixed ones return a PNG, the job route was already fine. No committed test for this: the renderer is ESM-only and jest cannot load it without changing the test setup. (2) **`/sitemap-batch/1.xml`:** it rebuilds about 100 count queries into an in-process cache that is empty after every restart, and the site deployed 24 times on 2026-11-06 and 5 times by midday on 2026-11-07; the 2026-11-06 02:22 UTC 502 landed in the same second as the old deployment `5144020d` being removed, and the app's own sitemap read timed out at 10 s on 2026-11-07. `collectPseoBatchEntries` now runs one rebuild at a time (concurrent requests share it), serves the expired list while it rebuilds behind the request, and keeps the old list if a rebuild fails; only the first request after a restart can still wait or fail (as before, the route then serves an empty urlset for 60 s). 4 new tests; 3 of them fail against the old logic. **Not changed:** `/sitemap-jobs/1` (already cached for 24 h, about 4.6 s uncached; its quality gate reads `descriptionHtml`) and `/sitemap.xml` (about 0.5 s). **Limits:** the cause of the homepage 5xx Google recorded on 2026-07-23 is unknown (logs that old were not checked); the many deployments are the likelier reason for any 5xx Google sees, and this change reduces the damage but does not reduce the number of deployments.

- SEO indexing fixes (**SEO risk flagged**: more pages become indexable; all reversible by env or revert). (1) **Real `lastmod`**: job sitemap entries use the job's posted/created date instead of the sync time, and the salary, slice, level, category, browse, evergreen, skill, remote, country and city sitemaps use the newest job's `createdAt` instead of `updatedAt`, so Google stops seeing one identical date on 97% of URLs; company sitemap unchanged. (2) **Crawlable paths to every job**: `/jobs?page=N` is indexable with a self-canonical (title gets "(Page N)") up to page 250, any filter or sort parameter is still noindex, and the pager now shows numbered links with jumps so any page is at most 8 clicks from page 1. (3) **Country pages unlocked in phase 1** for US, GB, CA, DE and AU (still subject to the 5-job gate), and `sitemap-country.xml` is advertised in the index only when it has entries; other countries stay noindex until phase 2. New read-only `company publishing report` workflow and `scripts/company-publishing-report.ts` show why company pages fail the gate before any company unlock. New tests for lastmod, pagination rules and markup, the phase rule and the country sitemap.

- Billing hardening from a Stripe integration review (billing is still off): (1) new subscriptions are created in Stripe's **flexible billing mode**, the mode Stripe recommends for new subscriptions; (2) **disputes**: `charge.dispute.created` takes back, in full, the credits the disputed payment granted (capped at the balance, counted with any refund already taken for the same payment so nothing is taken twice), and `charge.dispute.closed` with status won gives them back as a positive reversal on the same payment reference, so a later refund still works; a lost dispute changes nothing more; (3) **past-due grace**: a `past_due` subscription (a failed renewal while Stripe retries the card) keeps counting for 3 days after it went past due, measured from the event that set the status, and never earns credits (those come only from `invoice.paid`); with no timestamp it does not count. The webhook endpoint now needs two more events, `charge.dispute.created` and `charge.dispute.closed`. 13 new tests, mutation-checked (unlimited grace, a dispute that takes nothing, and no flexible billing mode each make tests fail). `docs/billing-launch-checklist.md` lists the events, the tax-behavior and Smart Retries settings to make in Stripe before launch.

- Hosted auto-apply pilot, Greenhouse only (**off by default; nothing submits unless two switches and a worker are set up**). A signed-in user with a résumé sees "Prepare auto-apply" on the kit page when the job's apply link is a Greenhouse job; we read the employer's form from Greenhouse's public Job Board API, write a draft (free), the user reviews every answer on `/account/autoapply/<id>` and approves, and a worker fills the employer's form in a real browser. **Why:** the owner asked for hosted auto-apply with approve-each; Greenhouse is 33% of apply links in a 238-job live sample (Ashby 21%, Lever 8%, a third other). **Rules it keeps:** only the user's own data is filled (profile, résumé, kit cover letter); every other question, including work-authorization, clearance, sponsorship and conflict-of-interest questions (the Anduril form has five), is left to the user and blocks approval until answered, and the equal-opportunity questions are never answered; what is approved is hashed and the worker refuses to send anything that changed (answers, résumé); **credits are taken only when the employer's own page confirms the application**; a captcha, security code, sign-in wall, unknown required field, validation error or no confirmation after submit stops it, charges nothing and gives the user the employer's link; a worker that dies mid-submit is handed back as "unconfirmed" and never retried; one live application per job, 10 per user per day, 5 per employer per hour; only https Greenhouse links are accepted (no LinkedIn or Indeed); no captcha solving and no browser spoofing. **Switches:** `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` plus `AUTO_APPLY_ENABLED=true` shows the feature and the four `/api/autoapply/*` routes (404 otherwise); `AUTO_APPLY_LIVE=true` lets the worker (`npx tsx scripts/autoapply-worker.ts`) really submit, otherwise it only fills and reports (dry run); the price is `AUTO_APPLY_CREDITS` (placeholder 5). **Database:** new additive migration `20261106210000_add_auto_apply` (`ApplyProfile`, `AutoApplication`; no existing table changes), checked on a real Postgres for a clean apply and no drift; not applied to production. **Verified:** 61 new unit tests (plan, approval hash, charging, caps, crash recovery, API guards; mutation-checked), a browser driver tested against a local replica of the form that records what it receives (9 tests, run with `AUTOAPPLY_BROWSER_TESTS=1`, skipped in CI), and a 20-check end-to-end run on real Postgres and a real browser (real Greenhouse question schema, read only, through review, approve and cancel; the worker against the replica; both result pages). **Not verified:** the driver has never been run on a real employer's form (that would mean filling real application forms), so dropdown, phone and country widgets may need work; the live form uses reCAPTCHA Enterprise, so a hosted browser may be stopped often; whether employers' terms allow it. See `docs/auto-apply-launch-checklist.md` before turning anything on.

- Homepage hero, footer and internal links. **Hero:** every row in the stats panel is now a link (each salary band opens the jobs paying at least its lower edge: `/jobs/100k-plus`, `/jobs/200k-plus`, `/jobs/300k-plus`, or `/jobs?minSalary=150000` for the 150k band; each market opens its country page; Remote, Hybrid and On-site open `/remote`, `/jobs?remoteMode=hybrid` and `/jobs?remoteMode=onsite`) and so are the "US · UK · EU · Canada · Australia" items above the headline. Europe has no all-jobs page, so Europe and EU open `/remote?region=europe` (its remote jobs; the row's count is all Europe jobs). The empty green block under the filters came from the panel being about 300 px taller than the copy: the panel is tighter (job types are three tiles in one row, smaller gaps), the copy is vertically centred beside it, and the leftover is split evenly (measured 133 px at 1280 and 1440 wide, 60 px at 1100). **Footer:** rebuilt from one list (`components/layout/footerLinks.ts`) that both the desktop columns and the mobile accordions render, so they cannot drift again. New columns: Jobs (adds New Jobs and Top Companies), Remote Jobs by region (Worldwide, US, Canada, Europe, Australia), By Location (adds Netherlands, Ireland, Spain, France, Singapore), and Company gains Cookie Policy; the "For Job Seekers" link that only pointed at `/jobs` is gone and Senior-Level Jobs is added. Every footer target was checked against the live site first: `/post-a-job` is a redirect to `/jobs`, there are no city, skills or level index pages and no public RSS feed, so none of those are linked. **Link audit:** a crawl of 337 internal links on 13 live pages found none broken (no 404 or 5xx) and 17 that permanently redirect, from two places, both fixed: the `/salary` country shortcuts used two-letter codes (`/salary/software-engineer/ca`) that redirect to the slug (`/canada`), and the "popular role x salary band" chips on `/jobs` (from the precomputed `JobSlice` table) pointed at `/jobs/<role>/<band>-plus` pages that redirect to the role page when the live query finds no jobs; a chip is now kept only when its page would show jobs (the page's own query, cached 30 minutes; a failed check keeps the chip). **Guard:** `__tests__/guards/internalLinks.test.ts` scans every literal internal link in `app/`, `components/` and `lib/` and fails on a link to a missing page, to a redirect-only page or to a configured redirect, and checks the footer the same way (links built from variables are not covered). 16 new tests.
