- Homepage live feed: a "New jobs, live" section under the hero lists the newest jobs we added, each with how long ago ("5 min ago"), location with flag and salary, and says "Latest job added N min ago". It checks for new jobs once a minute while the tab is visible and, when some arrive, shows a "Show N new jobs" button instead of moving the list (nothing shifts under a reader's thumb); polling stops after 30 minutes on one visit and resumes when the tab is shown again. It replaces the one-line rotating activity ticker (removed with its CSS), which showed one job at a time. **Why:** the owner asked for a "last updated" signal and a live feed; a competitor review (Remote Rocketship on a phone) showed a feed with relative times and "updated" stamps near the top of the page is what makes a job board feel current, while its filters-before-jobs and tall hero are what to avoid. **Honest by construction:** times are when WE added each job (`createdAt`, the same date-first order the homepage list uses), not the source's posting date, and the "latest job added" line is the newest of those. **Cost and SEO:** the first list is server-rendered so it is in the HTML and the page stays static ISR (`revalidate = 300`); the poll goes to new `GET /api/jobs/latest?since=` which is cacheable at the edge for 60 s and loads from one shared 60-second in-process cache, so traffic does not multiply database work. Adds internal links to job pages already linked from the list below; no canonical, hreflang or JSON-LD change. 18 new tests.

- Application kit (paid, behind accounts): a signed-in user pastes their résumé, and for any job gets a cover letter, résumé bullets rewritten for that job and a list of requirements their résumé does not show; a tracker lists every kit with status (saved, applied, interview, offer, rejected) and notes. **Off by default** with the rest of accounts: `/account/applications`, `/account/kit/[slug]` and `/api/kit/*` return 404 unless `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED=true`; with accounts off the job page and header HTML are unchanged. **Why:** the owner asked for a paid application kit; this is phase 2 after the accounts and billing foundation. **How it charges:** the kit is written first and credits are spent only after a valid kit exists, atomically and under a key made of user, job and résumé version, so a failed or malformed AI answer costs nothing, a double click or retry costs once, and asking again for the same job with the same résumé returns the saved kit free; changing the résumé makes the next kit a new charge. The price is `APPLICATION_KIT_CREDITS` (placeholder 1 until the owner sets it). **Safety:** résumé and job text go to the model as delimited data with a no-invention instruction and the answer must match an exact JSON shape; it is shown as text only and triggers nothing; every endpoint is same-origin, session-bound, rate-limited and scoped to the signed-in user's own rows; the résumé can be deleted and the tracker keeps the title, company and apply link if a job expires. **Does not apply anything**: the user opens the employer's page themselves; hosted auto-apply is a separate, later phase. **SEO:** one `nofollow` link ("Write a cover letter for this job") added to the job page sidebar, rendered only when accounts are enabled at build time; the new pages are noindex with no canonical or hreflang. **Database:** new migration `20261106180000_add_application_kit`, additive (two new tables `Resume`, `ApplicationKit`, no existing table changed), checked on a real Postgres for a clean apply and zero drift against the schema; not applied to production, the owner runs it through `db-migrate.yml`. **Needs an AI provider key** (`DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY`, as the enrichment jobs already use); without one a kit fails with "try again" and is never charged. Résumé upload is paste-only for now: PDF/DOCX parsing needs a new dependency and is left for the owner's decision. 44 new tests.

- Foundation for paid job-seeker features: user accounts (Google and emailed magic link) and billing (Stripe subscription, credit packs, customer portal, webhook, credit ledger). **Everything is OFF by default.** **Why:** the owner decided on sign-in with Google and a magic link, and a monthly subscription plus one-off credit packs; nothing else can be built on until accounts and billing exist. **Inert unless configured:** it switches on only with `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED=true` plus `AUTH_SECRET` (and `AUTH_URL` in production); billing needs `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, the webhook also `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. Disabled, `/sign-in`, `/account`, `/api/auth/*` and `/api/billing/*` return 404 for every HTTP method, `/pricing` is still the same static redirect to `/jobs`, and the header HTML is unchanged. **What:** (1) Auth.js (`next-auth` 5.0.0-beta.32 with `@auth/prisma-adapter`), JWT sessions in an HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Secure cookie lasting 14 days; JWT because the header asks `/api/auth/session` on every page load and that costs no database query, and the token holds only the user id (plan and balance are always read from the database; `/account` and every billing action re-check the user still exists). Magic links go through nodemailer over `EMAIL_SERVER` / `EMAIL_FROM`, expire in 15 minutes, and are limited to 3 per address and 10 per IP per 15 minutes (in memory per server instance, the same limiter `report-job` uses). Provider tokens from Google are not stored; Google accounts with an unverified email are refused; errors are logged by name only (no addresses). (2) `prisma/migrations/20261106120000_add_accounts_billing`, additive only (six new tables, no change to an existing one): `User`, `Account`, `VerificationToken`, `Subscription`, `CreditLedger` (append-only, unique idempotency key), `StripeEvent`. Not applied anywhere; the owner runs it through `db-migrate.yml`, and no public page or import touches these tables, so deploying before the migration is safe. (3) `lib/billing/*`: Checkout sessions (subscription and packs), customer portal, webhook, `spendCredits`, `getBalance`, `hasActiveSubscription`, `requireEntitlement`. Price ids and credit amounts come from env (`STRIPE_PRICE_*`, `BILLING_MONTHLY_CREDITS`, `BILLING_PACK_SMALL_CREDITS`, `BILLING_PACK_LARGE_CREDITS`); the three credit amounts have placeholder defaults (100, 50, 250) that the owner must replace; `/pricing` (when enabled, noindex, per request) reads amounts from Stripe. The webhook verifies the signature on the raw body, records each event id in the same transaction as its effects (a failure rolls back and Stripe retries), and handles `checkout.session.completed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`, `invoice.paid`, `customer.subscription.created/updated/deleted` and `charge.refunded` (a refund takes back the matching share of credits, never below a zero balance). `spendCredits` takes a row lock on the user inside one transaction, so concurrent spends cannot overdraw. (4) Pages `/sign-in` and `/account`, both `noindex, nofollow` with no canonical or hreflang; a small client component in the header shows "Sign in" (or "Account" after a session check that runs in the browser after load). The server never reads the session in a layout or public page. (5) `docs/billing-launch-checklist.md`: everything the owner must do before launch. **New dependencies, justified:** `next-auth` + `@auth/prisma-adapter` (OAuth, magic-link tokens, CSRF and cookie handling are security-critical and not worth writing by hand; v5 builds and runs under Next 16 here, checked with a real build and a browser run; v4.24.15, the stable line, would also have been possible but is built around `getServerSession` and options objects rather than the App Router `auth()` and lazy config used here), `nodemailer` (required by Auth.js's email provider, SMTP so any provider works), `stripe` (official SDK; webhook signature verification), `@types/nodemailer` (dev). **SEO:** no canonical, hreflang, sitemap, robots or JSON-LD change; the new pages are noindex and not linked from the sitemap; the `/api/` robots disallow already covers the new API routes. `next.config.js` gains an `env` entry that inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` (empty unless accounts were on at build time) so the header knows whether to show a link; `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` therefore has to be present at build and run time. **How verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; 129 new jest tests (6 suites; the whole suite is 958 passed, 1 skipped) (webhook idempotency and signature rejection, credit spend under 25-way concurrency against an in-memory fake that models the row lock, entitlement, checkout, env gating with the real route modules returning 404, sign-in rate limit); a real `npx next build --webpack` with accounts off and again with accounts on (placeholder values): the route table differs from a build of the unchanged code only by the added routes (`/account`, `/sign-in`, `/api/auth/*`, `/api/billing/*`), `/` and every existing route keep their type, and `/pricing` stays static `○` when off and becomes dynamic only when on; a production `next start` driven by Chromium against a local Postgres engine (PGlite) with the new migration applied and a local SMTP sink: Google button redirects to accounts.google.com with our client id and redirect URI, magic link e-mail delivered and signs in, link works once, cookie flags checked, 4th link request refused, signed-event deliveries credited exactly once including 10 parallel redeliveries, refund claw-back, checkout and portal refuse foreign-origin and signed-out requests, sign-out; page screenshots viewed at 1280 and 390 px. That run found and fixed one real bug (the sender threw on a plain SMTP result that has no `pending` field). **Not verified (no secrets, no network to them):** real Google sign-in, real Stripe Checkout, portal, webhook delivery and API-version payload shapes, real e-mail providers and deliverability, true multi-connection Postgres lock contention (PGlite serves one connection), and the live Terms/Privacy pages, which this change does not touch and which need legal review before launch. **Limits:** the sign-in rate limit is per instance and resets on deploy; JWT sessions cannot be revoked before they expire; `spendCredits` exists but no feature calls it yet; disputes and account deletion are not handled; a corporate mail scanner can use up a magic link.

- Remote hub: a region with no remote jobs is no longer shown as a chip. **Asked:** "don't show 0 jobs posts like middleeast". The previous change showed such a region muted and not clickable; it is now left out entirely (Middle East has 0 remote jobs on the live site today; the other six regions all have jobs), and it comes back by itself when a job in that region appears (counts are recounted about every 6 hours). The region you are viewing always keeps its chip. If the counts cannot be loaded, all chips show. The unused muted-chip support is removed from `LinkChips`. `visibleHubRegionSlugs` in `lib/jobs/remoteRegions.ts` holds the rule, with tests. The `/remote/<role>` region list has no counts and is unchanged. No canonical, robots or JSON-LD change.

- Remote hub regions that work, Europe and Middle East apart, Canada and Australia added; real job counts by country and job type, recounted every 6 hours; clearer logos and company names in job rows. **Asked:** "europe & middle east should not be together, also mention AUS, CAN as well like US", "can you actually count this after every 6-12 hours for job type and country instead of just placeholder", "make sure the logos and company names look clear". **Found first:** every regional chip on `/remote` returned **0 jobs** on the live site (checked: `?remoteRegion=` global, us-only, canada, emea, apac, uk-ireland all "0 remote jobs from 0 companies"; "All remote" 973). Cause: the filter compared the stored `remoteRegion` column to the slug, and that column is only ever "Global" (capitalised): ingest stamps it on every remote job that has no region (`lib/ingest/index.ts`), including 273 US-based jobs in the snapshot. **Now** (`lib/jobs/remoteRegions.ts`, applied in `buildWhere`, so the hub, `/remote/<role>`, `/search` and the homepage dropdowns all use it): a region is a set of countries and a remote job counts when its country is in the set (a job stored with the slug itself still matches). Chips: All remote, Worldwide, US, Canada, Australia, Europe, Middle East, Asia-Pacific; each shows its count, and a region with no jobs right now is muted and not a link instead of a dead end. The old combined `emea` slug still works (Europe + Middle East + Africa) for existing links and dropdowns but is no longer a chip. **Definitions to know:** Worldwide = remote jobs with no country listed (not the stored "Global", which is a default); US/Canada/Australia = the job's country; Europe includes the UK and Ireland; Middle East = AE, BH, EG, IL, IQ, IR, JO, KW, LB, OM, PS, QA, SA, SY, TR, YE. On the 2,813-job snapshot the remote counts are Worldwide 196, US 274, Canada 70, Australia 2, Europe 36, Middle East 0, Asia-Pacific 2, so the Middle East chip shows muted with 0 today. **Counts:** `lib/jobs/siteCounts.ts` counts live jobs per country and by job type (remote, hybrid and on-site that is not remote) from the database over the public-job filter, and caches the result for 6 hours in process (each server instance recounts at most every 6 hours; a restart or deploy recounts). The homepage "Pay floor by market" rows now show how many jobs each market has (United States 1,291, United Kingdom 112, Europe 77, Canada 253, Australia 16 on the snapshot; Europe there excludes the UK, which has its own row), a new "Jobs by type" list (Remote 579, Hybrid 22, On-site 2,085) and a line saying when it was counted; the three types do not add up to the total because 127 of 2,813 jobs state none of them. **Rows:** in list rows (hub, company pages, homepage lists) the logo tile is 52px (46px on phones) with a visible edge and a larger mark, and the company name is 15px in the main text colour at weight 500, where it was small grey regular text beside a bold title. **SEO flags:** region URLs that returned nothing now return jobs. The hub's own region URLs are `noindex` (query parameters), but I did not measure how many region pages feed `/remote/<role>` or the slice sitemap (`lib/seo/slicesSitemap.ts` passes `remoteRegion` through), so the sitemap may list more URLs than before; each page keeps its existing indexability gate. Not verified: the real database (counts above are from the 2,813-job snapshot), the Middle East or Australia chips with real traffic, and whether `lib/salary/aggregates.ts` (a raw SQL path that still compares `remoteRegion` to the slug) should follow the same rule.

- Feedback button on phones: out of the way while reading, gone while a menu is open. **Why:** live-site audit at 390 px: the lime "Feedback" pill sat over headings, mobile-menu rows and job-description text, and on job pages the icon above the Apply bar still overlapped text. **Now (phones, under 768 px):** the button is a 44 px icon-only circle (label hidden, still `aria-label="Send feedback"`) 12 px from the corner, instead of a wide pill. `FeedbackWidget.tsx` tracks scroll direction (one passive listener, `requestAnimationFrame`-throttled): after scrolling down more than 24 px it slides out of view (`.toggleTucked`: translated off-screen, opacity 0, `pointer-events: none`); scrolling up more than 8 px, being within 120 px of the top, or keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`) brings it back. While a Radix dialog or sheet is open (`body:has([role='dialog'][data-state='open'])`: the mobile menu, the jobs filter sheet) it is hidden at every width; before it floated above the menu sheet because its z-index is 1000. The existing job-page behaviour is kept and generalised: `MobileBarShell` still sets `document.documentElement.dataset.applyBar`, and while it is `shown` the button sits 84 px up (it was 96 px) so it clears the Apply bar; the `usePathname()` check for `/job/` is gone because that attribute only exists on job pages and is removed when the page unmounts. A leftover `max-width: 480px` rule that forced `bottom/right: 16px` on the button (it would have overridden the new offsets) is removed. Desktop is unchanged (pill with label, 24 px corner, never hidden by scroll). Reduced-motion users get no slide. **Verified:** real `FeedbackWidget`, real `MobileNav` sheet and real `MobileBarShell` (with a 69 px mock bar and a hero element for its observer) in Chromium at 390x844 with touch emulation and the real site CSS: at the top the button is visible (44x44, 12/12); after scrolling down 700 px it is opacity 0 and off-screen with `pointer-events: none`; scrolling up 60 px brings it back at 84 px above the bottom, 14 px clear of the Apply bar (bar top y=774, button bottom y=760); with the menu open it is opacity 0 and returns after Escape; tabbing to it while tucked brings it back (`:focus-visible`); the feedback dialog still opens from it; at 1440 px it stays a 117x40 pill after scrolling 900 px. **Limits:** after a scroll up the button is visible again and can still sit over a line of text at the right edge until the next scroll down (by design; a 44 px circle in the corner is the smallest tap target I would ship); only Chromium was tested; `:has()` needs a 2023+ browser, older ones keep the button visible under an open menu (as today); the soft keyboard is not handled. No SEO effect: client-only widget, no markup that crawlers index changes.

- `/search` on phones: filters behind a "Filters" disclosure, same look as the homepage form, H1 followed by H2. **Why:** live-site audit at 390 px: six stacked native selects pushed the first result about 1,100 px down (the audit's figure, measured on the live page with its header), the button was the old dark green and the selects were unstyled native boxes, unlike the homepage form; and the headings went H1 -> H3 because job titles are `<h3>` while the page has no H2. **Now:** new `app/search/SearchForm.tsx` + `SearchForm.module.css` (server component, CSS Module, no JavaScript). The search box and lime "Find $100k+ roles" button always show. The five selects (location, work arrangement, minimum salary, remote region, seniority) sit in a native `<details>` titled "Filters", closed by default; its summary shows "N active" in a lime pill when filters are set, or the hint "location, work type, salary" when none are. From 768 px up the summary is hidden and the panel is always visible (`::details-content { content-visibility: visible }` inside `@supports selector(::details-content)`; in a browser without that selector the summary simply stays visible on desktop, so the filters remain reachable). Same field names and plain `GET /search` as before (`q`, `location`, `remoteMode`, `minSalary`, `remoteRegion`, `seniority`), and closed selects are still submitted. Selects use one style with the homepage's chevron, 48 px tall, 16 px text so iOS does not zoom. "Clear filters" now also appears when only work arrangement, region or seniority is set (before, it ignored those). The empty-state "Explore newest opportunities" button is lime too. Headings: `JobList` and `JobCard` take an optional `headingLevel` (default 3, so every other page is unchanged); `/search` passes 2, giving H1 -> H2. **Verified:** rendered the real `app/search/page.tsx` (real `JobList`, `JobCard`, `SearchForm`; only the database loaders stubbed with 8 live-snapshot jobs) with the real compiled site CSS in Chromium 153. At 390 px with no header or ticker in the harness, the first result starts at y=746 before and y=415 after (same query, two filters set); at 1440 px 436 before, 415 after. Heading order H1,H2,H2,H2... (8 H2, 0 H3; before 0 H2, 8 H3). No horizontal scroll at 390 or 1440. Closed disclosure: Tab reaches the summary, Enter opens and closes it, `FormData` still holds all six fields and builds `/search?q=engineer&location=GB&remoteMode=remote&minSalary=200000&remoteRegion=&seniority=senior`. At 767 px the selects are hidden, at 1440 px visible with the summary hidden. Screenshots at 390 closed, 390 open, 1440 and the empty state looked right. **SEO:** none expected, confirmed in the diff: `/search` keeps `robots: { index: false, follow: true }` and the same canonical, title and description; no JSON-LD or hreflang on it; job links are unchanged anchors; only the title tags changed from h3 to h2 on this page. **Limits:** only Chromium was tested (no Firefox or WebKit here), so the desktop "always open" rule relies on `::details-content` support (Chrome 131+, Safari 18.4+, Firefox 143+ by my recollection of the release notes, not re-checked) with the visible-summary fallback otherwise; the desktop filter bar is still `md:sticky`, as before.

- Company logos: no more job-board or ATS logos on employers, and white logos no longer render as blank tiles. **Why:** live-site audit. EMC Adam, KA Resources, 1a-Aerztevermittlung and BS Menzel showed XING's logo; Profusion Group, Victorian Ports Corporation, SunWater and IBC Recruitment all showed the same Seek tile; Cadremploi, BetterTeam, ADP (`workforcenow.adp.com`), careers-page.com and rocket-match logos appeared on unrelated employers; Okta and CesiumAstro were empty white squares. **Cause:** a job's company "website" is often the board or ATS host the job was scraped from, and `buildLogoUrl` turns any website into `/logo/domain/<host>`, i.e. the vendor's logo. `isKnownBoardHost` (`lib/scrapers/utils/boardHosts.ts`) did not list these boards, `INVALID_COMPANY_WEBSITE_HOSTS` (`lib/companies/website.ts`) did not list the ATS portals, and a stored `img.logo.dev/<host>` or Clearbit URL skipped the website check entirely. **Now:** (1) boards added to `KNOWN_BOARD_HOSTS`: seek.com.au, seek.co.nz, xing.com, cadremploi.fr, francetravail.fr, free-work.com, welcometothejungle.com, betterteam.com, arc.dev, startup.jobs, kubernetesjobs.com, workwise.io, arbeitnow (.com/.nl/.co.uk), rocket-match.de, remote.com, civilservicejobs.service.gov.uk, careers.vic.gov.au and Adzuna's 16 country domains (only adzuna.com was listed). (2) hosted ATS and payroll portals added to the website blocklist (now split into `SOCIAL_HOSTS` and `ATS_PORTAL_HOSTS`): `workforcenow.adp.com`, `myjobs.adp.com`, UltiPro, Paylocity recruiting, saashr, Kronos, UKG `rec.pro.ukg.net`, Dayforce, EnterTime, Paycor, Paycom, Gusto jobs, Rippling ATS, Oracle Cloud HCM, Taleo, SuccessFactors, SAP HR cloud, Workday sites, JazzHR (`applytojob.com`), Zoho Recruit, Jibe, iSolved, PrismHR, careers-page.com, onlyfy.jobs, Personio jobs, Loxo, Dover, Trinet Hire, CareerPuck and about 20 smaller ones. Only portal hosts are listed, never the vendors' own sites (`adp.com`, `paylocity.com`, `ukg.com`, `gusto.com` stay valid employer websites). Each host was taken from the live snapshot, where it was the "website" of unrelated employers. (3) `buildLogoUrl` also ignores a stored logo.dev or Clearbit URL keyed to such a host (`isJobPortalHost`), and `inferWebsiteFromUrl` in `lib/companies/upsertFromBoard.ts` no longer turns an ATS apply link into the employer's website for new jobs. A company left with no trustworthy logo gets `/logo/name/<name>` as before; `CompanyLogo` already shows coloured initials when that 404s (checked, see below). (4) White logos: `CompanyLogo` puts a dark slate tile (`bg-slate-800`) behind a short list of companies whose logo is white on transparent (`needsDarkTile` in `lib/companies/logoFallback.ts`: Okta, CesiumAstro). Chosen over runtime pixel detection (needs a CORS-clean canvas read of a cross-origin image, which logo.dev does not allow) and over a grey tile for everyone (white-on-light-grey is still unreadable, and every other logo would lose its clean white tile). Add a name to that set when a blank tile turns up. **Verified:** replaying the 2,813-job live snapshot through the old and new `buildLogoUrl`: 340 of 1,264 companies change (684 jobs), none of the remaining logo URLs is keyed to a portal host; `__tests__/companies/portalHosts.test.ts` classifies about 50 board/ATS hosts and 20 employer look-alikes (`xingfoundation.org`, `seekingalpha.com`, `adp.com`, `okta.com`...) both ways. In Chromium with the real site CSS at 390 and 1440 px: board-keyed companies show initials, a 404 logo falls back to initials through `onError`, Okta/CesiumAstro show on a dark tile, an ordinary logo stays on white. **Limits:** the stored `website` values in the database are not cleaned (run `scripts/normalizeCompanyWebsites.ts` in dry-run to see them); the fix is applied when the logo is built, so it takes effect without a data change. logo.dev serves Okta white at `size=64` (what the stored URL gets) but black at `size=128` (what `/logo/domain/okta.com` gets), so Okta on a dark tile is right for the stored URL only; the dark list is keyed by name, not by variant. A recruiter whose name matches some other company can still get that company's logo from the name lookup (not changed here). SEO: no canonical, hreflang, noindex or JSON-LD change; `jobJsonLd.ts` and `companyJsonLd.ts` use `normalizePublicCompanyWebsite`, so `hiringOrganization.sameAs`/`url` no longer point at a board or ATS host, which is a correctness gain, but job pages' `JobPosting` fields (title, description, datePosted, hiringOrganization name, location) are unchanged.

- /salary index: plain wording, correct grammar, and no median from a handful of listings; "1 jobs" fixed on the homepage. **Asked (live-site audit):** the Product Manager card showed "—" and "Live median refreshes on each ISR window"; cards said "1 data points"; homepage role cards said "1 jobs". **Method change:** the index computed each role's median the old way, pooling every posting's minimum and maximum into one list and taking the middle element (all rows were already filtered to `currency: 'USD'`, so the currency mixing was not present here, but the pooling was), and "data points" was half of that pooled list. It now uses the same helpers as `/salary/[role]`: each USD listing counts once at `salaryMidpoint(min, max)` and `summarizeSalaries` decides whether there is enough to quote (`lib/salary/indexMedians.ts`, a pure function). The query and its WHERE are unchanged. **Threshold:** `summarizeSalaries` needs 20 listings, not the 5 asked for; I kept the shared rule so that a role's card here and the headline on its own guide page can never disagree (a card showing a median that the guide page declines to show would be worse than a stricter cut). Below it the card says "Not enough data yet" (the top stat tiles also say "Too few listings to call it a median"); with a median it says "Median of 40 USD listings" (grammar through the new `pluralize()` in `lib/utils/number.ts`). If the database is unreachable the tiles say "See the guide". The "Source method: ATS-backed" tile (jargon) now reads "Live listings: Built from current job postings that show their pay". Homepage "Explore Premium Roles" and "Top Locations" cards use `pluralize()` so 1 reads "1 job". **Not touched:** the numbers and wording on `/salary/[role]`, the JSON-LD and metadata of `/salary`. **Verified:** `__tests__/jobs/salaryIndexMedians.test.ts` (midpoint not pooled endpoints, thin sample gives `median: null` with the real count, bigint and one-ended ranges, other roles ignored, `pluralize`); the real `app/salary/page.tsx` bundled with esbuild against a stubbed Prisma (fixture listings: 40 software engineer, 22 data scientist, 20 senior software engineer, 3 product manager, 1 data engineer) rendered "$170k / Median of 40 USD listings", "Not enough data yet" for Product Manager and the empty roles, and no "ISR", "data points" or "ATS" text, with no horizontal scroll at 390px; viewed at 1280 and 390px. `tsc`, `eslint` and the whole jest suite pass. **Not verified:** how many of the 12 roles clear 20 listings on the live site (no database in the sandbox); if many show "Not enough data yet" the answer is more listings or a lower shared threshold, not a local exception. SEO: no canonical, robots, hreflang or JSON-LD change.

- Homepage search no longer leaves empty parameters in the address, and /jobs says what was searched. **Asked (live-site audit):** searching "data engineer" on the homepage landed on `/jobs?q=data+engineer&country=&remoteRegion=&remoteMode=&minSalary=100000`, and the page still said "All $100k+ Jobs" with nothing showing the query. **Now (a):** `HeroSearchInput` adds a `formdata` listener to the hero form that removes blank fields and trims the query as the browser builds the submission, so Enter, the Search button and the instant list's "See all N jobs" all go to `/jobs?q=data+engineer&minSalary=100000` (chosen role, location, region and work arrangement are kept). The form is still a plain `method="GET" action="/jobs"` form: without JavaScript it submits as before, empty fields included, which `/jobs` ignores. The $100k+ default is still sent (it is a real pill choice, and dropping it would widen results to other currencies; unchanged). **Now (b):** when `q` is present `/jobs` shows `Results for "data engineer"` as the H1 (query cut at 80 characters, and set at 0.7em on phones with word-breaking allowed so a long query cannot push the sort control off the screen) and a "Clear search" link to `/jobs` after the count; without `q` the page is exactly as before ("All $100k+ Jobs"). The keyword search itself and the "Showing results for ... / search instead" correction notice are untouched. **Verified:** the real `Hero` and `HeroFilters` bundled with esbuild and driven in Chromium (Playwright), 9 checks: query only gives `/jobs?q=data+engineer&minSalary=100000`; padded query is trimmed; role+country+remote+$200k+ are all kept and the unchosen remoteRegion is dropped; empty box gives `/jobs?minSalary=100000`; "See all" drops blanks too; the form is a GET to `/jobs`; no page errors. The real `app/jobs/page.tsx` rendered with a stubbed database at `?q=data engineer&minSalary=100000` gives H1 `Results for "data engineer"` with "Clear search", and `/jobs` and `/jobs?sort=relevant` still give "All $100k+ Jobs"; viewed at 1280 and 390px. **SEO (unchanged, checked):** the real `generateMetadata` for `/jobs?q=data engineer&minSalary=100000` returns `robots: { index: false, follow: true }` and canonical `https://www.6figjobs.com/jobs`; plain `/jobs` returns `index: true`, same canonical. No hreflang, sitemap or JSON-LD change (the CollectionPage JSON-LD on a noindex search page still says "All $100k+ jobs"). **Not verified:** a real `next dev` run (no database), JavaScript-off behaviour of the real hero (checked with a hand-written form that has the same method/action, because the no-JS path is the browser's own), iOS Safari. **Known, not mine to fix here:** on phones the header puts the sort control beside the heading (`.headerTop` in `components/listing/Listing.module.css`, which another change owns), so the heading column is only about 107px wide and plain `/jobs` already scrolls sideways by 7px at 360px and below; "Results for" inherits that squeeze. Letting the header wrap on small screens would fix both.

- "New this week" now counts jobs posted this week, and the company counts say what they count. **Asked (live-site audit):** the homepage hero, /remote and the category pages said "2,813 new this week" with 2,813 jobs in total; and "95 companies" (homepage, /companies) sat next to "334 companies" (/remote) and "1,223 open jobs" (/companies) next to 2,813. **Cause 1:** the count matched `lastSeenAt`/`updatedAt` (and on /remote `updatedAt`), which the daily sync refreshes on every live job, so every live job was "new". **Now:** `lib/jobs/postedRecently.ts` has one filter, used by `loadPublicSiteStats` (homepage hero), `/remote` and the category pages: `postedAt >= 7 days ago`, or (no `postedAt`) `createdAt >= 7 days ago`, the posting date and the day we first saw the job. Same query count and the same caching as before (React `cache` for the stats, the existing `memoAsync` on /remote, ISR on the pages); only the WHERE changed. A page whose count is 0 still hides the phrase. **Cause 2:** `loadPublicSiteStats().totalCompanies` only counts companies with at least `MIN_COMPANY_INDEXABLE_JOBS` (5) live jobs, because that is the set that gets a company page and a spot in the /companies directory; the homepage then quoted it next to the total job count. **Now:** the stats also return `totalCompaniesHiring` (every company with at least one public job, from the same `groupBy` that was already there, so no extra query). The homepage hero sentence ("N roles at M companies"), the hero panel's Companies figure, the homepage meta/Open Graph description and the homepage JSON-LD use it, because those sentences are about all jobs. /companies keeps the directory number but says what it is: "95 companies with 5+ open $100k+ jobs, 1,223 jobs between them. Across the whole site: 2,813 open jobs at N companies." (the 5 is read from `MIN_COMPANY_INDEXABLE_JOBS`, so it cannot drift), and its meta description and CollectionPage JSON-LD description now say "companies with 5+ open $100k+ tech jobs (N jobs between them)". /remote's "579 remote jobs from 334 companies" was already truthful (remote jobs, any company with one) and is unchanged; it will not equal the homepage figure because it counts only remote jobs. **Verified:** `__tests__/jobs/postedRecently.test.ts` (clause shape, no `lastSeenAt`/`updatedAt`, `createdAt` only without `postedAt`); the real `app/companies/page.tsx` and `loadPublicSiteStats` bundled with esbuild against a stubbed Prisma (fixture numbers: 2,813 jobs, 95 big companies with 1,223 jobs, 380 small) rendered 95 / 1,223 / 2,813 / 475 in the new sentence at 1280 and 390px without horizontal scroll, and the logged `job.count` WHERE for "new this week" contained only the `postedAt`/`createdAt` OR; the real `Hero` showed "2,813 roles at 475 companies" and "120 new this week" (fixture). `tsc`, `eslint` and the whole jest suite pass. **Not verified:** real numbers (no database in the sandbox), so I cannot say how many live jobs actually have a `postedAt`; jobs with neither a recent `postedAt` nor a recent `createdAt` are not new, which is the point, but if a source stamps `postedAt` with the scrape time the count will still run high. **Not changed:** the /companies `<title>` still reads "95 Companies Hiring $100k+ Roles" (title files were out of scope; it is the directory count, an undercount of companies hiring); the job cards' "Posted N days ago" falls back to `updatedAt` when `postedAt` is empty (`components/jobs/JobCard.tsx`), which the daily sync refreshes, so those cards can say "Posted today" for an old job; worth a follow-up. SEO: no canonical, hreflang, robots or sitemap change; two meta descriptions and one JSON-LD description changed wording as described.

- Role pages: the seniority and skill chips go to real pages again, and listing heroes fit phones. **Found by** a Chromium crawl of the live site (UI audit). **1. Dead chips.** On `/jobs/software-engineer` the Mid/Senior/Staff/Principal/Lead chips and the ~16 skill chips did nothing: each linked to `/jobs/software-engineer?seniority=senior` or `?skill=python`, and `proxy.ts` (`normalizeLegacyRoleQuery`, `LEGACY_ROLE_QUERY_KEYS`) answers those with a 308 back to the same URL (the role page itself also `permanentRedirect`s a `seniority` param). The strip is deliberate: those params are legacy and would create duplicate, un-canonicalised variants of the role hub, so `proxy.ts` is unchanged. **Now** (`app/jobs/_components/RoleTemplate.tsx`): the header chips are the per-level role pages that exist (`/jobs/senior-<role>`, `/jobs/staff-<role>`, `/jobs/principal-<role>`, from `roleCareerPath` and only when the slug is a canonical role, so no chip is a 404); Mid and Lead are dropped because no `mid-<role>`/`lead-<role>` page exists (live: 308). The skill chips link to `/jobs/<role>/skills/<skill>` (existing route, in the sitemap) for skills that have a landing page in `SKILL_TARGETS`, including stored spellings (`nodejs` goes to `node-js`, `go` to `golang`); skills without one (ai, api, cloud, ci-cd, azure, agile...) are no longer chips, and the section is hidden if none remain. The chips are normal links now (no `rel="nofollow"`, default scroll) and `buildToggleMultiHref` is deleted. **2. Phone hero.** `.headerTop` in `components/listing/Listing.module.css` was a no-wrap row, so on `/jobs` and role pages the Filters/Sort toolbar sat beside the title and squeezed it to 108-130px (H1 on 3-4 lines, summary on 6) and "Most Recent" stuck out of the green panel; under 1024px it is now a column (title full width, toolbar underneath). **3. Tap targets.** Related/seniority/skill chips and Previous/Next (`.chip`, `.pageLink`), the homepage "Popular" links (`Hero.module.css`), the homepage role chips, `/salary` country chips and "$100k+ jobs" band links, `/salary/[role]` band chips and Previous/Next, and `/search` "Clear filters" are 44px high at 768px and below; desktop sizes are unchanged. Class names only on the salary pages (no change to their numbers). The breadcrumb "Home" link is left alone (inline text). **Verified:** real `ListingHeader`, `JobsToolbar`, `LinkChips`, `Pagination` and `Hero` CSS rendered with esbuild and Chromium. Before at 390px: H1 130px wide on 4 lines, select right edge 387 vs panel 374, page scrolled to 387px at 320px. After: H1 322px wide (2 lines on a role page, 1 on `/jobs`), no overflow at 390 and 320 (`scrollWidth` equals viewport), chips and Previous/Next 44px, desktop (1440) heights identical to before (chips 36, Previous/Next 39). The real `RoleTemplate` rendered with a stubbed database gives `/jobs/senior-software-engineer`, `/jobs/staff-software-engineer`, `/jobs/principal-software-engineer` and `/jobs/software-engineer/skills/{python,aws,react,typescript,node-js,postgresql,csharp,docker,golang,kubernetes}`; each of those URLs returns 200 on the live site, and the live chip counts (python 13, aws 29) match the landing pages' counts. `tsc`, eslint on the changed files and jest pass. **SEO:** no canonical, robots, hreflang or JSON-LD change. New internal links go to pages that already exist; `/jobs/staff-*`, `/jobs/principal-*` and most role x skill pages are `noindex, follow` today (low volume), so those chips pass link equity only to pages that choose not to be indexed. **Limits:** the "Other levels" section further down the role page still links every level to the base role page (`baseSlug`), which is the same dead-end in a different form and was left as it is; the skill chips link to a page whose count can differ from the role's sampled count if the landing page's own filter (`isHundredKLocal`) excludes some jobs; the homepage, search and salary class changes were checked in a harness, not on the deployed pages; no real database was used.

- Job page and cards: readable employment type and skills, "Remote" once, and valid country/locality values in the JobPosting JSON-LD (follow-up to the locations entry below, from two audits of live job pages). **Why / how much (live-job snapshot, 2,813 jobs):** (1) the type chip printed the stored enum on 1,772 jobs (63%: `full_time`; plus `part_time` 84, `internship` 18, `fellowship` 3) and "Full-time" on the other 936; `lib/jobs/employmentTypeLabel.ts` now shows Full-time, Part-time, Contract, Internship, Temporary, Fellowship and so on, and capitalised words for anything unknown, never raw `snake_case`. (2) Skill chips showed slugs ("corrective-action", "design-for-manufacturing"): 4,620 of the 15,423 skill tags (30%) on the 1,954 jobs that have skills are hyphenated; `lib/jobs/skillLabel.ts` reuses the site's `SKILL_TARGETS` labels (Python, Node.js, C++, SQL), keeps acronyms upper case (AI, AWS, CAD, CI/CD, ISO 27001), a few brand spellings (LabVIEW, iOS) and small words lower case ("Design for Manufacturing", "Infrastructure as Code"); text that already has capitals or spaces is left as written. Used on the job page and on job cards. (3) "Remote" appeared twice: on the job page as the location chip plus a second "Remote" chip, and on cards as a location pill plus the work-type pill, for the 196 remote jobs with no country; the location chip/pill is now the only one that says it (the page's chip reads "Remote" with a globe, the card's location pill is left out when there is no country to show). **JobPosting JSON-LD (flagged: SEO / structured data):** running the old and new `buildJobJsonLd` over the snapshot, the old code emitted **41 `addressCountry` values that are not ISO 3166-1 codes** ("BC" for "Burnaby, BC", "AN" from "Mülheim an der Ruhr", "EN" from "Aix-en-Provence", "LE" from "Le Mans", "ON" from "London, near London Bridge...", "AM" from "Frankfurt am Main", "HQ", "SF") and 200 junk `addressLocality` values (codes such as "US-REMOTE", several places joined by "•", prose); the new code emits 0 and 0 on the same data, by the same check. **Cause:** `inferCountryFromLocationRaw` lifted any two-letter token out of free text, any US state code made the job American ("WA" in "East Perth WA" gave the US for an Australian job), euro pay was taken to mean Germany (Amsterdam, Zürich and Madrid became "DE"), and the raw text was used as the locality. **Now:** `addressCountry` is written only when it is one of the 249 ISO codes (`lib/location/country.ts`, `UK` becomes `GB`); the stored `countryCode` wins; then a country the text clearly names ("Winnipeg, Canada", "France, Auvergne-Rhône-Alpes", "Kleve, Nordrhein-Westfalen"; a text naming two countries gives none); then the company's country; then pay in USD, CAD, GBP or AUD; then a city we have a page for (Amsterdam gives NL, Zürich CH); and only then, as before, euro pay gives DE (a last resort, often right, not always; it is what the old code did for every euro job). A state or province code is written as `addressRegion` only when it fits the country (a US state after a US place, a province after a Canadian one), and `;`, `•` and `|` lists are split into one place each, each with its own country, leaving out a place whose country is unknown. `addressLocality` goes through `cleanLocality`: codes, "Remote", "HQ", lists, text of 9 words or more, anything over 60 characters, and a bare country name are dropped (the country stays). Unchanged: the shape of the JSON-LD, `jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE`, `applicantLocationRequirements`, the rule that a remote job has no `jobLocation`, and what a job with no determinable country gets (the same 203 jobs, all remote, have no `jobLocation`, as before). 115 of the 2,813 jobs now have a different list of countries from before (the invalid ones fixed, "DE" guesses replaced by the city's country, a few added from the text). **Tests:** `__tests__/seo/jobJsonLdLocation.test.ts` (the exact bad strings: "HQ", "SF", "Frankfurt am Main", East Perth WA, Amsterdam, "US-REMOTE", the bullet-joined list; stored country preferred; every emitted country is in the ISO list across a grid of junk inputs), `__tests__/jobs/jobLabels.test.ts` (36 cases), the full suite passes (649). **Limits:** the euro last resort can still say DE for a euro-paid job in a country we have no city page for and no text for; the job-card remote pill logic is not unit-tested (checked by rendering); "Changé, Pays-de-la-Loire" and "France-GRENOBLE(ISERE)-EYBENS" still fall to the euro guess.

- Locations: flags and country names everywhere a country shows, and more of the place on the job page. **Why:** the VIDA Roads job in Melbourne showed "Melbourne, AU" with no flag, "Country  AU" in the company card and no state, while job cards on company pages had a small flag. Checked on the live-job snapshot (2,813 jobs, fetched 2026-11-06): `countryCode` is set on 1,756 (62%) and empty on 1,057 (38%); `city` on 2,042; `stateCode` on **0**; `remoteRegion` on 576, and its only value is "Global", which is also on 273 remote jobs with a US country code, so it cannot be believed. Only 14 distinct codes occur (US 1,291, CA 253, GB 112, DE 33, FR 22, AU 16, NL 7, IN 6, ES 5, IE 5, IT 3, PT 2, BR 1); the code is ISO `GB`, never `UK`. 69 jobs have a `locationRaw` of 3 characters or fewer ("US", "UK", "NYC", "HQ", "..."), 94 name several places with `;`, and 54 are employer prose over 60 characters ("If successful for this role you will be based in Manchester.Please note, due to the nature..."). **Flags were emoji, copied into several places** (`JobCard`, `JobsFilters`, `CountryLocationTemplate`, the job page for remote jobs, the homepage tiles). Windows does not ship flag emoji, so Chrome and Edge there show two letters in a box; reproduced in Chromium here by hiding the emoji font ("AU Melbourne, AU"). So the flags are now images: `public/flags/<code>.svg`, 32 countries (every code in the snapshot plus the countries in the site's country and city lists), about 16 KB in total, from the MIT-licensed `country-flag-icons` 3x2 set (licence text in `public/flags/LICENSE.txt`); no dependency added, no JavaScript, loaded only where used, cached 30 days (`next.config.js`). **How:** `lib/location/country.ts` (code to name, `UK` to `GB`, fixed names for the 32 so server and browser print the same text, `Intl.DisplayNames` for the rest; a two-letter value that is not one of the 249 ISO codes, such as "HQ", is not a country and is ignored); `components/ui/CountryFlag.tsx` (decorative `<img alt="" aria-hidden>`, renders nothing for a country without a flag; the country name is always in text beside it); `lib/location/jobLocation.ts` (`describeJobLocation`, one reading of a job's place for the job page and the cards). **Where:** job page hero chip ("Melbourne, Victoria, Australia" with flag; city and country link to `/jobs/city/<slug>` and `/jobs/location/<slug>` only when those pages exist: country pages for the countries in `lib/seo/countrySlug`, city pages only for cities in `CITY_TARGETS` and only in their own country); remote jobs get a globe and "Remote · United States" (the separate "Remote" chip is dropped when the location chip already says it); company card "Country" is the flag and name, and the row is left out when the country is unknown (it said "Global"); job cards (country name instead of code: "Melbourne, Australia", USA and UK kept short); homepage "Top locations" tiles; the country landing page's filter panel; the filter panel's country list (it printed bare codes for IT, PT, BR, BE and others missing from its 18-name table); the salary guide's country table. **Reading `locationRaw`, carefully:** the country is taken from the text only when its last part is a country name or alias, or a state, province or Land that belongs to one country ("Kleve, Nordrhein-Westfalen", "Seattle, Washington"); two-letter codes are never read as countries, "Georgia" and "Victoria" are skipped, and a text naming two countries gives nothing. Checked on the snapshot: of the 1,148 jobs that have both a stored code and a text naming a country, **0 disagree**; 422 of the 1,057 jobs with no stored code now get one (DE 196, US 198, GB 16, CA 8, others 4), so jobs with a flag go from 1,756 to 2,182 (62% to 78%). 807 single-place jobs now show a state or region (US states by name, Canadian provinces, Australian states, UK nations, German Länder) taken from the posting's own text, 442 of 579 remote jobs say where, and jobs whose text is only a short code fall from 69 to 6. A stored country that the text contradicts ("Paris, France" stored as US) follows the text, as the old `isLocationValid` did. Several-place jobs keep "First place +N more" (70 in the snapshot); the job card used to count commas as places and print "Kleve +1" for "Kleve, Nordrhein-Westfalen" (494 jobs), now only `;` counts. Employer prose longer than 90 characters is cut at a word with "…". **Verified:** unit tests for the helpers (51 tests across `lib/location`, including case, `UK`/`GB`, `None`, unknown codes, the ambiguous cases above, and that `public/flags` holds exactly the countries in the table); the real job page (`app/job/[slug]/page.tsx`, real `JobDetailParts`, `JobCard`, CSS Modules and compiled Tailwind) rendered in Chromium with only the database replaced, for AU (the VIDA Roads job), US, GB, DE, CA, PT, a German place with no stored country, a remote US job, a remote job with no country, a `;` list, a country-less place, employer prose, a non-country code ("ZZ") and no data at all, at 1280 px and 390 px: all flags load, no horizontal scroll, the Country row reads "Australia" with the flag. `tsc`, ESLint on the changed files and the whole jest suite pass. **SEO (flagged):** no canonical, hreflang, robots, title or JSON-LD change. The hero text changes ("Melbourne, AU" becomes "Melbourne, Victoria, Australia", "Remote (US)" becomes "Remote · United States") and gains up to two links that the "Explore more" block already carried; the breadcrumb is untouched because it mirrors the BreadcrumbList JSON-LD (a country or city level would need the JSON-LD changed too). All of it is in the server HTML. **Not done / found:** `stateCode` is empty on every live job, so `/jobs/state/<state>` pages filter on a column nobody fills and JobPosting `addressRegion` is never emitted (the state shown here comes from the posting's text, not from data; the real fix is in ingest, which was out of scope); `countryCode` is still empty on 635 jobs after the text reading; the homepage and `/search` location dropdowns are native selects whose options cannot hold an image, and an emoji there would show letters on Windows, so they stay text; `app/company/**` (the "Top locations" list printing "Melbourne, AU", the HQ country in the header) was left alone for the pagination work and still prints codes; `/remote/<role>/country/<code>` prints the bare code in its H1 and `/jobs` and slice pages print "role · US"; `lib/constants/homepage.ts` still carries unused flag emoji; Australian state suffixes without a comma ("Sydney NSW", 10+ jobs) are not read; city links are skipped for cities outside `CITY_TARGETS` (most of them).

- Titles and meta descriptions: topic and live count first, brand last, nothing over 65 characters, descriptions 130-155 characters with a freshness phrase. **Why:** a comparable board (spainjobs.io, measured live) leads titles with the topic or count and ends descriptions with "Updated daily"; ours had role-page descriptions of 91-128 characters (105 for /jobs/software-engineer), company titles that overflowed for any name over about 16 characters (`<name> $100k+ Jobs - N Open Positions | Six Figure Jobs` is 49 characters plus the name), and role x city, role x skill and remote city/country titles of 76-104 characters that Google cuts. I do not know how any of this ranks; titles are a ranking signal and these can move rankings either way. **How:** new pure module `lib/seo/metaText.ts` (`truncateAtWord`, `fitTitle`, `buildListingTitle`, `buildDescription` and per-page builders), used by each `generateMetadata`; every number in a description is a value the page already computed (job count, company count, average USD salary, highest listed pay, minimum-pay range), nothing is invented. Titles changed: `/jobs/[role]` "Software Engineer Jobs - $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs" -> "72 Software Engineer Jobs Paying $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs" (highest risk: biggest page type, and the count now changes the title); `/company/[slug]` keeps the current wording when it fits and otherwise drops the brand, then shortens, then cuts at a word boundary; `/jobs/[role]/city/[city]`, `/jobs/[role]/skills/[skill]`, `/jobs/skills/[skill]/remote`, `/remote/[role]/country/[country]`, `/remote/[role]/[location]` and `/jobs/[role]/[filter]` are rewritten to "N <topic> Paying $100k+ | Six Figure Jobs"; `/remote/[role]` keeps its wording and only drops the brand for the long role names that overflowed (40 of 201 role options overflowed at a 4-digit count). Job pages: wording unchanged, but the last-resort cut in `fitTitle` now lands on a word boundary instead of mid-word. Descriptions only (title already fine): home, `/companies`, `/jobs/city`, `/jobs/state`, `/jobs/skills`, `/jobs/industry`, `/jobs/category`. The home title is deliberately unchanged: "Six Figure Jobs" is both the brand and the head keyword, so moving it last buys nothing and risks the one query we own. Left alone because already within limits: `/blog` index, `/jobs`, `/remote`, `/jobs/level`, `/jobs/location`, and the `(N openings)` titles of city/state/skill/industry/category (longest real label gives 64-65 characters with a 4-digit count, checked against every label in `pseoTargets`). Not touched: `/salary/**`, and slice pages (`lib/seo/meta.ts` titles run up to about 80 characters; changing `buildSliceTitle` would also change the CollectionPage JSON-LD `name`, so it is left for a decision). **Verified:** `__tests__/seo/metaText.test.ts` (35 tests) covers every distinct role label, all city/state/skill/industry/category labels, and very long role and company names: titles never exceed 65 characters, truncation ends on a whole word, descriptions are 130-155 characters, end with the freshness phrase and are unique per page; `tsc --noEmit`, eslint on the changed files and the full jest suite pass. No existing test asserted an exact changed title (`jobMeta.test.ts` passes unchanged). **Limits:** no database in the sandbox, so `generateMetadata` itself was not run against real data and nothing was checked in a browser; "Updated daily" rests on the existing daily ingest and on copy already on these pages; no canonical, robots, hreflang or JSON-LD changes (the `/companies` meta description no longer equals the CollectionPage JSON-LD description, which is unchanged).

- Company pages list all of a company's live $100k+ jobs, ten at a time, with a "Show more jobs" button. **Asked:** "make sure company pages don't only show 0 or 1 job post but all, with a show-more button after every 10 jobs" (examples: `/company/vida-roads` "1 open $100k+ job", `/company/pelago`). **Root cause: the page had no cap in code, the thin pages are data.** `app/company/[slug]/page.tsx` fetched every matching job (no `take`, no `pageSize`) through `buildWhere({ companySlug })`, the join is by `companyId` (`companyRef.slug`), and the live pages match the live-job snapshot exactly: vida-roads 1 and pelago 1 (page and snapshot agree), anduril-industries 102 jobs / 1,085,865 bytes of HTML, roblox 36, google 24 (all three equal the snapshot count). So the page already showed everything the database returns; what is missing is rows. Across the 2,813 live jobs (1,264 companies) 888 companies (70%) have exactly one live job and 1,237 have ten or fewer. Evidence that it is the data: (1) every one of the 2,813 live jobs was updated within two days of 2026-11-06 (593 today, 2,220 yesterday, none older), consistent with `runExpiryCycle` (`lib/jobs/expiry.ts`) expiring any job whose `updatedAt` is older than 7 days, so only what the last sync re-read survives; (2) freehire lists 11 open Pelago jobs (last seen 2026-11-04, none closed), 8 of them with a published salary of $100k or more, against 1 on our page; (3) the Amplitude page shows 0 because its jobs are stored under a UUID-named company (the relink in `refreshJob` only applies when the sync re-reads a job). The daily sync after the freehire "keep confirmed-open jobs alive" fix has not run yet, so the pending sync is what should bring these pages back to their real size; I could not read the production database, so which of Pelago's other seven jobs were expired or never ingested is not confirmed. The 7-day expiry is the only code-side cause I found, and it is intended. **What it does now:** the first 10 jobs are in the server-rendered HTML (crawlable links; before, a 102-job company sent 102 cards, about 1 MB), then "Showing 10 of 37 jobs" and a "Show more jobs" button under them. Without JavaScript the button is a real link, `/company/<slug>/page/2` (jobs 11-20, with "Previous jobs" back to the company page and "Show more jobs" to page 3). With JavaScript (`components/companies/CompanyJobsMore.tsx`) it becomes a button that fetches `GET /api/company/<slug>/jobs?page=N` (`app/api/company/[slug]/jobs/route.ts`), appends exactly the next 10 with the same `JobCard` the page already used, moves keyboard focus to the first new job, announces "10 more jobs loaded. Showing 20 of 37 jobs." in a polite live region, and removes the button after the last chunk; a failed request keeps the button and shows a link to the next page. The page, the route and the page math share one filter and order (`lib/companies/companyJobs.ts`: the same `buildWhere`, order max pay, min pay, newest, then `id` so equal rows cannot repeat or vanish between chunks; `lib/companies/jobsPaging.ts`: page size 10, page parsing, clamping). The route validates the slug and `page` (digits, 1-1000, else 400), cannot be asked for more than 10, caches 5 minutes in process and `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=600`, and sends the salary columns as numbers because `buildSalaryText` ignores strings (found while testing: with the string form the appended cards lost their salary; `/jobs` infinite scroll sends the same strings and may have the same fault, left as it is). `jobListingSelect` in `lib/jobs/queryJobs.ts` is now exported so the route sends the listing columns, not whole job rows with descriptions. The "Hiring at X" cards, the FAQ, the page title and the meta description still use the real total of all jobs. **SEO (flagged):** canonical is unchanged (`/company/<slug>`); the new `/company/<slug>/page/N` pages (N from 2) are `noindex, follow` with canonical = the company page, the same rule as `/jobs?page=2`, `/company/<slug>/page/1` redirects to the company page, an out-of-range or malformed N is a 404, and no page emits hreflang. Like the existing `/jobs` pager the links carry `rel=next` and `rel=prev`; `/jobs` emits no prev/next link tags in the head, so none were added. The company page stays ISR (`revalidate = 3600`); the next-page address is a path, not `?page=N`, because reading `searchParams` would make every company page dynamic. **JSON-LD (flagged):** the `ItemList` (it listed up to 50 jobs) and the `CollectionPage` `mainEntity` (up to 24) now list only the jobs on the page (10), with the right `position`, so the markup matches what is visible; `numberOfItems` is the real total; JobPosting blocks, Organization, Breadcrumb and FAQPage are unchanged (the FAQ count was already the real total). **Verified:** 23 unit tests for the page math and parsing and 17 for the route (same filter and order, page size fixed at 10, bad slug or page, caching, salary as numbers, failure); full jest suite (573 pass), tsc, eslint. In headless Chromium, with the real components bundled with esbuild, server-rendered and hydrated, over the real rows of the live snapshot on a mock server (49 checks): first 10 in the HTML as links, the link becomes a button, each click adds exactly 10 and the last adds the remaining 6 of Roblox's 36 and removes the button, Anduril's 102 take 10 clicks with no repeats and in database order, focus lands on the first new job, the live-region text, double click sends one request, failure and retry, Enter and Space, page 2 followed by a click, JavaScript disabled (the link goes to `/company/roblox/page/2`, then 3, then the last 6), and no horizontal scroll, a 44 px full-width button and no console warnings at 390 px. **Not checked:** the real page against a database (none here: the page's own wiring, `generateMetadata` for page N and the `[n]` route re-export were checked by the type checker and read by eye, not rendered; `next build` compiles and type-checks with the new `/company/[slug]/page/[n]` and `/api/company/[slug]/jobs` routes listed, run without a database), and the real JobCard images (they load from the network). **Limits:** `/company/<slug>/page/N` is a second address for the same jobs, noindex, so jobs 11 and beyond are reachable for crawlers through links but not indexed as listing pages (their own `/job/` pages are in the sitemap); the first-10 JobPosting JSON-LD still comes from the whole job list, as before.

- Homepage "Explore Premium Roles" cards: a small soft 3D (clay-style) illustration per role. **Asked:** "use similar icons for roles and try to create better images", after liking how spainjobs.io shows rounded illustrated icons on its discipline cards. **Not copied:** their artwork is theirs; this is an original set drawn by hand as inline SVG (gradients, gloss patches, blurred contact shadows, light from the top-left; palette of lime greens, warm neutrals, one blue, one orange; no text in the art). **What:** new `components/roles/RoleIllustration.tsx` maps a role slug to one of 14 illustrations (software = terminal, frontend = browser, backend = server rack, full-stack = layered windows, DevOps/SRE = gear with a loop, data engineer = pipes into a database, data scientist/analyst = chart, ML/AI = node graph, mobile = phone, security = shield, product = roadmap with pin, design = palette, engineering manager = org chart, staff/architect = layered slabs) and to a briefcase for any other slug (the role `other` and every unmapped slug land there; senior/staff/principal/lead/junior prefixes are dropped before lookup). The art itself lives in `components/roles/roleArt.tsx`. `ExplorePremiumRoles` renders it bottom-right of each card and the arrow moved to the top-right corner; role names, counts, averages, the Trending badge and the `/jobs/<slug>` links are untouched. Each SVG has fixed width/height, is `aria-hidden` (the card text names the role) and carries only the gradients it uses, with per-instance ids. **Verified:** real component rendered with esbuild and Chromium at 1440, 1024 and 390 px (no horizontal scroll, all eight cards 176px tall, no text covered), the server HTML still has the same eight links and the same text, and `__tests__/components/roleIllustration.test.ts` checks that every homepage card slug resolves to its own illustration, unknown slugs get the fallback, and every SVG is sized, decorative, text-free and self-contained. **Cost:** the eight illustrations add 27,237 bytes to the homepage HTML (about 3.2 KB gzip, 2.6 KB brotli). **Limits:** hand-drawn vector art, not photo-real 3D; only the homepage cards use it so far (the `/remote` and `/jobs` role lists are plain link lists and are unchanged); most live jobs are stored with the role `other` (see earlier entries), which would get the briefcase wherever the component is used for a job's own role.

- Header: a search field on every page instead of the magnifier button. **Asked:** search should work like spainjobs.io (a wide field in the header, a full-width row under the logo on phones). **Before:** a 44px icon button that opened a "Find your next six-figure job" dialog (with hint links); the mobile menu had a "Search Jobs" item that opened the same dialog. **Now:** a real `<form role="search" method="get" action="/search">` with a labelled `q` input (visually hidden label, 48px tall, 16px text so iOS does not zoom, magnifier inside the left edge). Submit does exactly what the dialog did: trimmed text, `router.push('/search?q=' + encodeURIComponent(q))`, blank does nothing; without JavaScript the same form does a plain GET to `/search?q=`. Enter submits, Escape blurs. Layout: 1360px and wider is one row (logo, the "$100k+ salaries shown" pill, five nav links, then the field, 560px max); 1024-1359px is logo | field on row 1 and the nav on row 2 (one row would squeeze the field to about 436px at 1280); under 1024px it is logo | menu button on row 1 and the full-width field on row 2. Pure CSS grid, so the server HTML and first paint are the same (no layout shift). The header stays sticky on wide screens; on the two-row layouts only the bottom row (field on phones, nav on small desktops) stays pinned and row 1 scrolls away, so a 125px header does not cover a phone screen. Phone header is 56px of logo row plus the 68px field row (was 64-80px). **Removed:** the dialog, its hint links, and the mobile menu's "Search Jobs" item (the field is always on screen); `components/ui/dialog.tsx` stays because the report-job form uses it. **Not done on purpose:** no instant results in the header. `HeroSearchInput` fits the hero form (it sends that form's salary and work-arrangement choices, and its "See all N jobs" button submits to `/jobs`), but the header submits to `/search`, which reads the query differently (roles, locations and leftover words via `parseSearchQuery`), so the "See all N" count could disagree with the page it opens. The homepage hero search is untouched and the header field is shown on the homepage too (simplest correct behaviour). **Verified** by bundling the real `SiteHeader` with esbuild plus the compiled site CSS into a static page and driving it in Chromium (Playwright) at 1440, 1360, 1359, 1024, 768, 390 and 320px: field visible and labelled, 48px high with 16px text, 516-560px wide on desktop, no horizontal overflow at 320px, Enter goes to `/search?q=staff%20engineer%20remote` and `/search?q=pm`, Escape blurs, blank does nothing, the mobile menu opens and closes, focus order is skip link, logo, five nav links, field, and the pinned row stays at the top after scrolling. `renderToString` of the header contains the five nav links as plain `<a href>` plus the search form. **Not verified:** a real `next dev` run (no database in the sandbox), iOS Safari, the field with a phone keyboard open. No change to canonicals, hreflang, noindex or JSON-LD; the primary nav links stay plain anchors in the server HTML, and the header gains one form and no crawlable links (the removed menu item was a button inside a closed sheet). The pinned header's drop shadow (a heavy black `0 18px 60px rgba(0,0,0,.35)` left over from the dark theme, visible as grey smudging under the nav on the light page) is now a soft light shadow.

- Favicon and app icons now say "$100k" instead of "fig". **Asked:** "for favicon lets have $100k instead of fig". **What:** same brand look (lime `#84cc16` rounded square, near-black `#0a0a0a` mark), new mark is "$100k" in Inter Display Black, tight tracking, converted to plain SVG paths (no `<text>`, no font dependency) and scaled to the square's width. Regenerated `app/icon.svg`, `app/favicon.ico` (PNG-in-ICO, 16/32/48) and `app/apple-icon.png` (180x180, RGB, no alpha, square full-bleed lime so iOS rounds the corners itself). The glyphs are stretched vertically (1.6x, 2.3x on the 16px frame) so the text fills the square; the 16px frame also has looser tracking so the two zeros do not fuse. **Verified:** rendered in Chromium at 16, 32, 48 and 180 px on white and dark backgrounds and looked at magnified screenshots; text colour on lime is 10.0 : 1 (my WCAG calculation). **Limits:** at 16px the mark reads as "$100k" but the digits are only a few pixels wide, so it is the least legible size; 32px and up are clear. There is no web manifest or 192/512 PNG in the repo, so nothing else was updated. The wordmark (`public/logo.svg`, `public/logo.png`, `public/og-image.png`, `components/layout/Wordmark.tsx`) still reads "six fig jobs" and was not touched. No change to canonicals, hreflang, noindex or JSON-LD. Browsers cache favicons hard, so a hard refresh may be needed to see it.

- Installed the Nectiv "AEO Content Structure Optimizer" Claude Code skill into the repo (`.claude/skills/aeo-content-structure-optimizer/`), as requested. Source: https://github.com/Nectiv-Digital/nectiv-claude-skills at commit `cd1c809` (2026-11-02), MIT licensed (its LICENSE file is kept next to it). It is tooling for the person working on the repo, not site code: nothing under `app/`, `components/` or `lib/` changes, so no page, canonical, hreflang, noindex or JSON-LD is affected. Before installing I read `SKILL.md` and `references/evidence.md` and checked the four Python scripts: they use only the standard library (an optional TextBlob import) and make no network or subprocess calls. Its deliverable is a Google Doc and its evidence is correlational, partly vendor-published (the skill itself says so), so treat its effect sizes as directional. Already used to audit `/salary/[role]` (previous entry).

- Salary guide `/salary/[role]`: correct numbers, and structured so answer engines can quote it. **Why:** `/salary/software-engineer` showed "Median base salary $160,000", "Typical range $90,000-$359,000", "Sample size 20", and a country table in `$` for GB and CA. Checked against the live-job snapshot: those figures are reproduced exactly by the old method, which (1) pooled every posting's minimum and maximum into one list and took the middle of it, (2) called the lowest and highest pay a "typical range", (3) mixed GBP, CAD and USD amounts and printed them all with `$`, and (4) counted only jobs whose stored role slug equals `software-engineer` (22 of the 371 jobs with "software engineer" in the title; the list on the same page used the wider `contains` rule, so the numbers and the list disagreed). **Now:** `lib/salary/roleGuide.ts` computes from the same filter as the list (cached 10 min); each listing counts once at the midpoint of its range; headline figures are USD listings only and need at least 20 of them, otherwise the page says so and states no figure; percentiles are real (25th, median, 75th); countries are shown in their own currency (at least 5 listings); companies with at least 2 listings (an ATS id is never shown as a company name); experience levels as before (at least 5 per level). On the same snapshot the page now reads median $195,000, middle half $163,000-$230,000, 53 USD listings (72 in total), instead of $160,000 / $90,000-$359,000 / 20. **Page structure** (per the audit factors in the Nectiv AEO skill, whose evidence is correlational and partly from vendors, so treat the effect sizes as directional): a "Key Takeaways" section first under the H1 (4 bullets, each quotable alone, the last one stating that the data covers only listings at or above the band, so it runs above pay for the role overall); a visible "Updated <date> · N live listings" line (the newest listing refresh in the sample, not the render time); sections headed by the question they answer, each with a real `<table>` (percentiles, level, companies, countries); a short methodology list; a visible FAQ whose text is the same string as the FAQPage JSON-LD; the three generic "Salary-first / Remote eligibility / Seniority focus" boxes are removed. **Structured data (flagged: JSON-LD changes):** `Occupation.estimatedSalary` now carries real `percentile25`, `median`, `percentile75`, `duration: P1Y` (it had the minimum and maximum labelled as `percentile10` and `percentile90`); the bare top-level `SpeakableSpecification` is now the `speakable` property of a `WebPage` with `dateModified`; FAQPage answers are computed from the data and match the visible FAQ; BreadcrumbList and AggregateOffer are kept (offer low/high are the lowest and highest listing). Meta description now leads with the median, middle half, sample size and month. Canonical, robots rule (index when at least one listing), title and the band toggles are unchanged. **Measured** by running the real page, loader and summary against the snapshot (a stand-in for the database, 72 matching jobs), not against production: 3 real HTML tables, 9 of 13 headings are questions (7 echo the subject), first section is Key Takeaways, no horizontal scroll at 390 px. **Not done / limits:** the role filter still only sees the jobs stored under a matching role slug (about 10% of live jobs are stored as `other`), so the sample is smaller than the real market; `/salary/[role]/[...loc]` and `/salary` have the same old numbers and are not changed; experience level is empty for many jobs, so the level table often does not appear.

- Search page and company logos: readable text. **Why:** two screenshots from the live site. (1) `/search?q=...` showed the filter panel and the "No jobs found" box as a mid-grey slab with faint labels. Cause: the `.glass`, `.surface` and `.soft-shadow` utilities in `app/globals.css` were still the old dark-theme styles (translucent near-black `slate-950` with a heavy black shadow), but the site is light-only (`<html class="light">`), so they blended into a muddy grey while the text kept its light-theme dark grey. Measured in Chromium with the real compiled CSS: panel background `rgba(2,6,23,0.55)` over the page colour, label contrast about 1.4 : 1 (my calculation from the blended colour); now a white card with the standard border and a soft shadow, 6.6 : 1. Only `/search` (noindex) and the mobile menu sheet use these classes. (2) On the homepage "Top Companies Hiring Now" row, hovering a logo showed two tooltips: the browser's own (`title`, readable, includes the job count) and a custom one that rendered as faint ghost text under the logo, because the hovered tile's transform made a stacking context that the next row's tiles painted over, and the carousel's `overflow: hidden` clips it on the last row anyway. **Now:** the custom tooltip is gone, the company name stays in the DOM as screen-reader text (the link keeps its `aria-label`), and the native tooltip with "Name • N active $100k+ jobs" is the only one. No change to canonicals, hreflang, noindex or JSON-LD; the company links and their text stay in the HTML.
 The 'Clear filters' link keeps its 44px phone tap target from the earlier tap-target change (it had been 16px high).
- Keyword search matches where a word starts, not any run of letters. **Why:** the instant-search results for "seo" included "Staff Software Engineer, WarehouseOS", because the filter was a plain substring match and "seo" is inside "Warehou-seO-S". The same flaw hit every short term: "pm" matched "development", "ux" matched "luxury", "ml" matched "HTML". **Now:** a term matches when the text starts with it or it follows a space, `-`, `/`, `(`, `,`, `&`, `:`, `.`, `|` or `"` (`wordStartPatterns` in `lib/search/keywordPlan.ts`, used by the filter in `lib/jobs/queryJobs.ts`, by `scoreJob` and by the typo check, so the three agree). Typing the start of a word still works ("engin" finds Engineer). In the ranking a whole word in the title ("PM") now beats a longer word that starts with it ("PMH"), and an abbreviation's full phrase ("pm" = "product manager") ranks level with the abbreviation. The same filter serves `/jobs`, `/api/jobs`, `/search`, similar jobs, the visa page and the instant results. **Measured** by running the real filter against the 2,813 live jobs fetched earlier (not against the production database): pm 318 → 175, ux 121 → 10, ml 113 → 55, ai 1,481 → 1,271, js 119 → 78, seo 12 → 11, qa 7 → 5, sre 16 → 15; react (90), software engineer (399), marketing (71), account executive (52), data scientist (23) and node (36) are unchanged; the top five for "pm" are now all Product Manager roles. **Limits:** a short term still matches longer words that start with it (PMO, PMP, PMH, "Seoul" for "seo"); "js" no longer finds jobs whose only match is a skill tag like NodeJS or NextJS (all 41 of the jobs it dropped were of that kind), although "node" and "next" do; the generated SQL has about 11 times more LIKE conditions per term and field (about 55 per variant), which I have not load-tested against the production database.

- Homepage search: instant results are now the matching jobs, not roles (replaces the role suggestions merged an hour earlier). **Why:** checked on the live site after the first version deployed. `GET /api/jobs/suggest?q=seo` returned nothing, and "marketing" returned one role ("Svp Marketing", 1 job), because **90% of our live jobs (2,531 of 2,813) are stored with the role `other`**: the role normaliser only recognises a small set of exact titles, and role suggestions can only ever cover the other 10%. The normal keyword search finds 12 jobs for "seo" (10 with the form's default $100k+ filter), 71 for "marketing" and 399 for "software engineer" (332 with the filter), against 72 jobs stored under the role Software Engineer; the role counts the first version showed were therefore misleading as well as thin. **Now:** after two characters the box shows the five best matches from the same relevance-ranked keyword search as `/jobs?q=` (typo correction, aliases): title, then company and pay, each a link to the job, and a last row "See all N jobs for “seo”" that submits the form. `GET /api/jobs/suggest?q=&minSalary=&remoteMode=` (`app/api/jobs/suggest/route.ts`) answers `{ total, jobs: [{ title, company, pay, href }] }` through `queryJobs`; the salary and work arrangement the visitor has chosen in the form are passed along (and validated; anything else is ignored) because the form's default $100k+ filter changes the count, so the total in the footer is what the Search button shows. Cached 5 minutes in process per query and filter, `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=300` for the CDN, 150 ms debounce, an aborted request on the next key; a failing search answers 200 with no results and logs the error. When a role, location or remote region is chosen in the form the box shows no instant results (the endpoint does not filter by them, so the list would not match what Search returns) and Search applies everything as before; changing a pill refreshes the results in the background and never opens the list over the pills. Removed the role-matching code from the first version (`lib/roles/roleSuggestions.ts`, `roleCounts.ts`) since nothing uses it any more. **Tested in a real browser** (Chromium, the real component, a mock endpoint with realistic rows and a working salary filter): 31 checks, including the debounce, the salary pill, a chosen role, keyboard navigation with wrap-around, Enter on a job, Escape, links, the footer submit, a failing endpoint and a 390 px phone with long titles cut by an ellipsis; building that test showed that an open list covers the filter pills and that refreshing on a pill change would have opened it on top of them (fixed). Unit tests: 7 for the endpoint (matches and link/pay, the exact search arguments, form filters, junk filters ignored, short and over-long queries, caching, database failure). **Not checked:** the results against the production database until it deploys, and the load: each distinct typed prefix runs a keyword search (one count and one page query plus ranking), softened by the debounce, the 5-minute cache and the CDN cache, but not load-tested. **Found, not fixed (bigger than this feature):** with 90% of jobs stored as `other`, every role page (`/jobs/<role>`), role count, "similar jobs" and salary comparison that relies on the stored role sees about a tenth of the jobs (the job page now reads the role from the title at display time, `resolveJobRoleSlug`, but the stored value is unchanged). Assigning the role from the title at ingest, and backfilling, is the fix.

- Homepage search: live role suggestions as you type. **Asked:** "don't we have live instant search so we can search by roles?" We did not: the box was a plain text field that searched only on submit, and the only typeahead in the code (`app/components/RoleTypeahead.tsx`) is imported nowhere (left as it is). The homepage's Role dropdown also cannot be reused for this: of its 201 options only 76 are role slugs the database stores and 30 have a landing page (for example "SEO Marketing" would find nothing), and the matcher behind it matches inside words ("pm" matches "development"). **What it does:** typing two or more characters shows up to six roles that match and have jobs right now, each with its live job count ("seo" shows SEO Manager 11 jobs, SEO Specialist 7 jobs), plus a last row "Search all jobs for “seo”".
