A role opens `/jobs?role=<slug>`; Enter on nothing highlighted searches the typed text exactly as before. **How:** `GET /api/jobs/suggest?q=` (`app/api/jobs/suggest/route.ts`) matches against live-job counts per role (`lib/roles/roleCounts.ts`: one grouped query with the same public-job filter as every listing page, cached 10 minutes and shared by all visitors; seniority variants are counted onto the base role because the role filter matches `roleSlug contains slug`) and answers with `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=300`; if the lookup fails it answers 200 with no suggestions and logs the error. `lib/roles/roleSuggestions.ts` does the matching on whole word starts, then exact synonyms ("swe", "pm" find Software Engineer, Product Manager), never suggests a role with no jobs, and ranks a label that starts with the text above a later word, then by job count. `components/home/HeroSearchInput.tsx` is the input: a plain `q` text field in the same GET form, so without JavaScript, or if the request fails, the box searches as before; 150 ms debounce and an aborted request on the next key; an ARIA combobox (arrow keys with wrap-around, Enter, Escape, click outside, screen-reader count announcement). **Tested in a real browser** (Chromium, the real component bundled with esbuild, a mock endpoint running the real matching code over realistic counts): 24 checks, including debounce (three fast keys, one request), keyboard navigation, Enter, Escape, links, the list covering the filters row, a failing endpoint (no list, no error, search still works) and a 390 px phone with no sideways scroll. That run **found a bug in my first version**: clicking "Search all jobs for …" did nothing, because closing the list on click removed the submit button before the browser could submit the form; fixed and covered by the checks. Unit tests: 8 for the matcher, 4 for the endpoint (matching, caching, short queries, database failure). **Limits:** it suggests roles only, not companies or job titles. A suggestion's count is the number of jobs stored under that role; jobs whose title was not recognised as a known role are stored as `other` (7 of 9 titles in one sample), so "Search all jobs for “seo”" can find more than the role rows show. Not seen yet: the suggestions against the production database; after deploying, `/api/jobs/suggest?q=seo` and the homepage box are the check.

- Company names that were ATS identifiers (a job at Amplitude said "9c26369c-d400-4eb7-a9b3-ab8f6469f9e8" in the listing card, the page header, the company card and "Opens 9c26369c-…'s own application page"). **Cause:** freehire itself names that company with an Ashby organisation id (its record has `name` = `slug` = the UUID, 38 jobs, no website or description), and ingest took names as given. The real name was in the record twice: the apply URL is `jobs.ashbyhq.com/amplitude/…` and the description opens "Amplitude is the leading AI analytics platform". **How widespread (our live site, 2026-11-06, read through `/api/jobs`):** 28 of 2,813 jobs (1.0%) had an id or address as the company, from 13 different names across Ashby, Gusto, Loxo, Oracle HCM, ADP, Paycor, Workday and plain domains: the Amplitude UUID (4 jobs), `neatdevs-llc-<uuid>` (10), `iwoca.co.uk` (3), `pod6.app.loxo.co/inara-talent` (2), `sage-veterinary-imaging-<uuid>`, `app.loxo.co/reliance-one-1`, `jobs.aecon.com`, `weber.wd115.myworkdayjobs.com/WB`, `activefence/d5.005`, two `icfcjb.fa.ocs.oraclecloud.com/…` entries, an ADP `<uuid>:19000101_000001` and a Paycor hex id. In freehire's 500 newest Ashby jobs at $100k+ only 2 (0.4%, both Amplitude) had this, so it is rare in new data but real. **Fix:** new `lib/companies/repairCompanyName.ts`. `looksLikeIdName` recognises a UUID or a UUID tail, a hex id, a host and path, a platform address, or `name/code`; it left all 1,394 distinct real company names I could check untouched apart from those 14 (tests cover "Impact.com", "monday.com", "C3.ai", "U.S.Bank", "Cantor Fitzgerald/BGC", and lowercase brands like "iwoca"). `repairCompanyName` then recovers the name, in this order: from the name itself ("neatdevs-llc-<uuid>" gives "Neatdevs LLC", "reliance-one-1" gives "Reliance One", "jobs.aecon.com" gives "Aecon"), from the ATS board in the apply URL (`jobs.ashbyhq.com/amplitude/…` gives "Amplitude"; Greenhouse, Lever, Workable, SmartRecruiters, Workday, Recruitee, Teamtailor, BambooHR, Breezy, CATS), from the first sentence of the description ("X is …", read block by block), else null. Of the 28 live jobs, 24 are repaired from the name and URL alone; the other 4 (ICF ×2, ADP, Paycor) name no employer anywhere in the record, and for those the job is now skipped instead of published under an id, as ingest already did for other garbage names (so those 4 jobs will leave the site on the next sync that re-reads them; say so if you would rather show a placeholder). `mapFreehireJob` uses it, so new imports are right, and does not look an id-named company up at freehire for a website. A repaired name resolves to the existing company by name, so Amplitude's four jobs join the real Amplitude company (22 jobs at freehire) and its page. **Existing jobs:** a job re-read by the sync keeps its stored company on the refresh path, so `refreshJob` now moves a job whose stored company is an id to the resolved real company (`relinkToRepairedCompany`: company, company id and dedupe key; a job stored under a real name is never moved, and a job whose title and place already exist at the real company is matched to that one first, so the unique key cannot collide). The next sync with a lookback that covers a job repairs it (the 14-day run scheduled for after the quota reset covers the recent ones); older jobs, and the old id-named company rows, are left as they are: those rows will simply have no jobs. **Not fixed, found while looking:** 91 live jobs from 46 companies show a Greenhouse board token in lower case without spaces (`accenturefederalservices`, `heihotels`, `theproviderfinder`, `chop`, `bridgebio`, `stackadapt`…). Some are the real brand (iwoca), many are not, and a rule cannot tell them apart; correcting them risks splitting a company across two rows, so it was left alone. Tests added (36 for the repair module, 3 for the mapper, 2 for the relink rule). **Not checked:** the refresh/relink path against the real database (none here); it is covered by the unit tests of the rule only, so look at `/company/amplitude` after the sync.

- Job page: "Last checked" instead of "Updated", a shorter phone hero, a location chip that fits, a Feedback button that stays out of the way; and the three tests that failed in CI are fixed. **CI (the red "SEO Local Gates" check).** `sitemapIndexRoute.test.ts` and `hubsSitemapRoute.test.ts` failed on every PR and on `main` itself (3 tests). **Cause:** `app/sitemap.xml/route.ts` and `app/sitemap-hubs.xml/route.ts` read `getSiteUrl()` once into a module-level constant when they are imported, `getSiteUrl()` prefers `SITE_URL`, and `seo-gates.yml` sets `SITE_URL=http://localhost:3000` for its server step, so the tests got `localhost` where they expect `https://www.6figjobs.com` (in one case the route even prefixed an already-absolute child URL with `localhost`, because the stubbed child indexes return production URLs). Setting the variable in a `beforeEach` did not help for exactly that reason (the routes are already imported by then). **Fix (tests only, no production code):** both files pin `SITE_URL` in `beforeAll`, then `jest.resetModules()` and import the routes dynamically, and restore the original value afterwards. Reproduced first: with `SITE_URL=http://localhost:3000` set locally the same 3 tests failed; after the change all 147 SEO tests pass with and without CI's variables. **Last checked.** The sidebar row "Updated: Today" used `updatedAt`, which moves for unrelated edits. It now reads "Last checked" from `lastSeenAt` (shown as "Just now / 5 hours ago / 3 days ago", omitted when unknown): the ingest sets it every time a job is seen listed at its source and the freehire closed-job check sets it for every job it confirms open (added earlier), so for freehire jobs it is the last time freehire still listed the job. It is a statement about when we last saw the job open, not a promise that it still is. **Phones.** Measured in a hand-built replica of the hero (real CSS and tokens, headless Chromium at 390 px, the Google posting's text): the title went from 5 lines to 3 and the hero is 98 px shorter. Logo and company name now share a row (`display: contents` on the text wrapper, below 768 px only) so the title uses the full width; the logo is 48 px instead of 72 px; chips and the pay card are tighter. The location chip showed "Los Angeles, CA, USA; Mountain View, CA, USA" on two lines; below 768 px it now reads "Los Angeles, CA, USA +1 more" (wider screens keep the full text, which is also the tooltip). **Feedback button.** On job pages it was lifted above the sticky Apply bar permanently, but that bar slides away while the hero is on screen, so the button floated mid-screen over the salary card. `MobileBarShell` now sets `data-apply-bar` on `<html>`; below 768 px on job pages the button is an icon-only 40 px circle in the corner and lifts only while the bar is showing (measured: 40 px circle at the bottom edge with the bar away, 28 px above the bar when it shows). **Not checked:** the live page (no database here), the real header's height and the real display font (the replica uses a stand-in), and any phone other than a 390 px viewport. **Not done:** the company card still says "Country: Global" when the country is unknown, and there is no visa-sponsorship badge yet.

- Job page: the description is shown as Description / Requirements / Benefits, like Remote Rocketship. **What:** on top of the labelled sections (previous entry), `groupDescription` (`lib/jobs/descriptionStructure.ts`) sorts a posting's own headings into three buckets by their titles (`classifySectionTitle`: "Minimum qualifications", "What you bring", "You have", "Required", "Nice to have" are Requirements; "Benefits", "What we offer", "Compensation", "Pay range" are Benefits; everything else, and the opening text, is Description), in that order, each bucket only if the posting has something for it. The posting's own headings stay as sub-headings inside a bucket and nothing is added or reworded. It applies only when at least two buckets are filled; otherwise the flat sections are shown as before, and postings with no headings stay as flowing text. The jump list now lists the buckets. Equal-opportunity, privacy, accommodation and recruitment-scam sections stay folded at the end. **Measured on 300 live USD postings** (fetched from freehire's `/agent/` endpoint, 2026-11-06): 120 (40%) have any headings and the other 180 are plain text, so they are unchanged; of the 120, the first version of the patterns put 67 into two or more buckets, and after adding the headings it missed ("You have", "You are", "Required", "Strong Preferences", "Bonus Points", "What we can offer you", incentive/bonus plans, plural "Equal Opportunities", recruitment-scam warnings) 109 do, 30 of them with all three. Rendered the real Google posting (Description + Requirements with its Minimum / Preferred qualifications as sub-headings) and a real Verkada posting (all three buckets plus a folded equal-opportunity note) in headless Chromium: both read correctly. **Known limits:** matching is on heading text, so an unusual heading (for example "Mindset & Traits", "Domain Knowledge") stays under Description; a pay-range paragraph that is bold text inside another section, not its own heading, stays in that section (as on the Verkada posting); and a posting's responsibilities stay inside Description rather than getting their own bucket, because that would need guessing at unlabelled text. Not seen on the live page (no database here). Tests added for the classifier, the grouping and the new boilerplate patterns (full suite passes).

- Job page: the description is now split into labelled sections with a jump list, an Apply button follows it, and the sidebar no longer repeats the hero chips. **Why:** with the full descriptions now stored (previous entries), a long posting such as the Google one (4,175 characters) rendered as one unbroken block with the company's generic paragraph first. Remote Rocketship (checked by fetching a live job page, 2026-11-05) splits the text into labelled Description / Requirements / Benefits sections and puts an Apply button after it; I took that idea, not its content. **What changed:** new `structureDescription` (`lib/jobs/descriptionStructure.ts`) splits the sanitized description at the posting's own `<h3>` headings (text before the first heading becomes "Overview"; trailing colons are dropped; empty headings are skipped; ids are unique) and invents no headings, so a posting without headings renders exactly as before. `DescriptionSections` shows a jump list when there are three or more sections, each section under its own heading with a divider, and folds equal-opportunity / privacy / accommodation sections into a `<details>` so they do not sit between the reader and the apply button (the text stays in the page). `DescriptionApply` adds an "Apply on company site" button after the description; its clicks are recorded with the new surface `description-end` (`APPLY_SURFACES`; the `surface` column is a free string, so no migration). The "Ready to apply?" card no longer lists Type and Level, which are already chips in the hero. **Checked:** the real Google description (fetched from freehire) through the page's own sanitizer, `structureDescription` and the page's CSS and design tokens in headless Chromium at 900 px and 390 px: Overview / Minimum qualifications / Preferred qualifications, jump chips, dividers, bullets and the final button all render and read correctly. **Not checked:** the live page itself (no database here), so the sticky sidebar, the hero and the folded `<details>` were not seen in place. **Known limit:** this posting's responsibilities list has no heading in the source, so it stays at the end of "Overview"; guessing a "Responsibilities" heading would put words in the employer's mouth, so it was not done. Tests added for `structureDescription` (full suite passes: 443).

- Job page: similar roles that are actually similar, a pay rank on more jobs, one `jobLocation` per workplace, and a salary-by-experience-level table on role and category pages. **Similar roles.** Seen on the Google "Senior Staff Software Engineer, Ads Content Safety AI/ML" page, whose "similar six-figure roles" were a psychiatric nurse practitioner and an unrelated engineer. **Cause:** ingest stores a role slug only when the whole title slug is canonical, so that title (and, in a sample of nine real freehire titles, seven of nine) is stored as `other`; the job page resolved `other` to no role, and with no role the similar-jobs query had no filter at all (newest US jobs of any kind). **Fix:** new `resolveJobRoleSlug` (`lib/roles/indexableRole.ts`) uses the stored role, else reads the role from the title with the existing matcher, trusting a match only when the role's words appear together in the title head (so "UX Writer and Content Designer" is not turned into `ux-designer`, nor "Product Marketing Manager" into `product-manager`); checked on real titles: "Senior Staff Software Engineer, Ads Content Safety AI/ML" gives `software-engineer`, "Sr. Analytics Engineer" `analytics-engineer`, "Partner Solutions Engineer, gTech Ads Sellside" `solutions-engineer`, and the nurse practitioner and "Legal Deployment Strategist" give none. With a role the similar jobs are that role; without one they are a relevance-ranked search for the title words (`similarJobsKeyword`, two words or more); with neither the section is simply not shown instead of listing arbitrary jobs. The same resolver now feeds the "Explore more" links and the pay rank. **Pay rank.** The "pays more than N% of listings" line needed a resolved role and 15 peers, so it was missing on most freehire jobs; it now uses the title-based role, and when the role has too few listings (or none) it ranks against all listings in the currency and says "six-figure listings" (`loadPeerSalaries(null, currency)`; that sample is capped at 3,000 rows, unordered). **JSON-LD locations.** `locationRaw` such as "Los Angeles, CA, USA; Mountain View, CA, USA" was emitted as ONE place whose `addressLocality` was the whole string; Google reads `jobLocation` as a list of places, so it now emits one `Place` per `;`-separated workplace (up to 10; locality, two-letter region, and each place's own country, falling back to the job's), only when the job has no city. **Salary by experience level** (`components/listing/SalaryByLevel.tsx`, on `/jobs/<role>` and `/jobs/category/<category>`): median pay and listing count for entry / mid / senior / lead / executive, each linking to its `/jobs/level/<level>` page, with a caption saying what it measures (midpoint of each range, level read from the title, in the page's currency). Levels with under five listings are left out and the table is not shown unless at least two levels remain. Caveat on the data: `experienceLevel` defaults to `mid` and is inferred from the title only, so titles that name no level land in "mid"; the caption says the level is read from the title. Tests added for the role resolver, the keyword, the multi-location JSON-LD and the by-level maths (full suite passes: 439). Not checked: any of this against the production database or rendered in a browser (this environment has neither), so the table's look and the real medians are unseen.

- Job descriptions were cut off at ~1,000 characters, and company pages only showed about a week of jobs; both fixed at the source, and the "Typical salary" card now speaks for its own page. **Found from** `/job/senior-staff-software-engineer-ads-content-safety-j-1yiz7jo` (description ended mid-sentence: "The Ads Content Safety team are seeking an") and `/company/pelago` (1 job shown). **1. Truncated descriptions (root cause).** The freehire scraper used `/api/v1/jobs/search`, which freehire documents as returning a "truncated index preview"; every description in it is ~1,000 characters (checked live: 989, 998, 999, 999, 997 for five Google jobs). The same filters on `/api/v1/agent/jobs/search` return each description in full: that job is 4,175 characters, with the responsibilities and the minimum/preferred qualifications we never showed. Across 300 live USD jobs the published descriptions now run 1,654 to 20,068 characters (median 6,034) instead of ~1,000. Google's JobPosting guidance asks for the complete description (responsibilities, qualifications, ...), and our JSON-LD is built from the same stored text, so it was incomplete too. **Fixes:** `lib/scrapers/freehire.ts` now reads the `/agent/` endpoint (same parameters, same request count); `refreshJob` (`lib/ingest/index.ts`) replaces a stored description with a clearly fuller one (`isFullerDescription`: +200 characters and +20%, so re-wording never overwrites); the daily closed-job check already fetches every active freehire job, and now also upgrades the truncated description of each from that response, so existing jobs repair themselves over the next run with no extra requests. **Also fixed:** the job page turned every newline between list items into `<br />` after the list clean-up had run, giving `<ul><br /><li>...<br></li><br /><li>` (invalid HTML and a gap between every bullet); `sanitizeDescriptionHtml` now drops breaks next to block tags (checked on the real Google description: 0 stray breaks, was one per item). **2. Company pages showed only the last week of jobs.** Two causes. (a) The daily sync re-reads only the newest ~36 hours and the expiry removes any job whose `updatedAt` is older than 7 days, so every freehire job disappeared a week after import whether or not it was still open (Pelago's "Senior Product Designer", posted 26 Oct, freehire "fresh", still open, was gone). The closed-job check now sets `updatedAt`/`lastSeenAt` for each job freehire confirms open and still listable, so a job stays while freehire lists it (at most about 30 days plus the 7-day grace); jobs freehire flags as re-stamped, or older than 30 days, are left to expire as before. (b) `mapFreehireJob` dropped everything whose `reality.class` was not `fresh`, but `fresh` only means up to 14 days old: on 300 live USD jobs, 76 were `stale` without `fake_freshness` (30 aged 15-30 days, 4 aged 0-1 day) while the re-stamped Anduril-style ones (91-146 days) all carry `fake_freshness: true`. New `isListableByReality`: never a flagged job; `stale` is accepted up to `MAX_LISTED_AGE_DAYS` (30). On the same 300 jobs this publishes 144 instead of 122 (+18%). Pelago has 8 open jobs at $100k+ on freehire; the page showed 1. **Effect on indexing (not measured):** company pages are indexable from 5 live jobs (`MIN_COMPANY_INDEXABLE_JOBS`), so companies like Pelago (`noindex` at 1 job) can now qualify. **3. "Typical salary" card.** It showed the same copy and the same $100k-$300k+ bands on every page. It now names its own segment ("Most Software Engineer roles pay ...", "roles in London", "remote roles in Europe", via a new `scope` prop passed by each listing template), says how the segment's median compares with all listings ("22% below all listings ($176k median)", shown from 3% apart, same currency, one cached market query shared by all pages; off on salary-tier pages where it would be true by construction), and its bands start at the page's own floor (`bandFloorFor`: a $200k+ page reads $200k-$300k, $300k-$400k, $400k-$600k, $600k+ instead of an empty $100k band). The all-jobs page is unchanged (it is the market). Tests added for the age rule, the keep-alive and description upgrade (including dry run and the "flagged/too old is not refreshed" cases), `isFullerDescription`, `bandFloorFor` and `describeVsMarket`; full suite passes (427). **Needs your attention:** (i) these changes only help data we import from now on and what the closed-job check touches; jobs already expired by the old 7-day rule stay expired until re-imported, so after deploying run "freehire daily sync" by hand with `lookback_days` = 30 (about 5 markets x up to 100 pages; the key's daily quota is 10,000, and I did not check whether the `/agent/` endpoint counts differently against it), then watch `kept alive (...)` and `full descriptions replacing truncated ones` in the closed-check log; (ii) I could not reach the production database, so the Pelago comparison (1 shown vs 8 on freehire) is freehire's data against the live page, and the card text was checked on synthetic distributions, not production numbers; (iii) the 30-day age limit is a judgement call (it matches `FREEHIRE_LOOKBACK_DAYS`): raise `MAX_LISTED_AGE_DAYS` for more jobs, lower it for fresher ones; (iv) the daily run now writes to up to ~2,800 job rows (one `updateMany` per 500) and the first run also rewrites each truncated description; no Google Indexing API notifications are sent for these updates.

- freehire rate limits: fail fast instead of sleeping for hours. **Found in the log of the 8-day backfill run (2026-11-05):** `[freehire] 429 rate limited; waiting 49611s (attempt 1)`. freehire answered with `Retry-After: 49611` (about 13.8 hours), the sync obeyed it in full, and the job sat idle for 45 minutes until the job timeout cancelled it. That is the real cause of both stuck runs today (it is why the backfill never reached the company lookup, and the likely cause of the earlier closed-check stall: its waits were capped at 60 s but repeated per job); the earlier "unknown cause" notes in this file are superseded by this. An unauthenticated request from outside GitHub still gets 120 requests a minute, and the next run (made with the fix) logged `retry-after=46729 limit=10000 remaining=0 reset=46729` on every market and failed in 2 seconds: the key has a daily quota of 10,000 requests and it was used up (partly by today's several 8-day backfills, three closed-check dry runs and the stalled closed check). It resets about 13 hours later (around 10:27 UTC on 2026-11-06), so the scheduled 05:17 UTC run on that day will fail fast with this message; a normal day is roughly 3,000-4,000 requests (sync plus about 2,800 closed-check lookups plus about 600 company lookups), so it should fit. **Change:** one shared rule, `decideRateLimitWait` (`lib/scrapers/freehire.ts`): a `Retry-After` of up to 120 s is waited out, a longer one is given up on at once. The sync logs `retry-after`, `x-ratelimit-limit`, `-remaining` and `-reset` on every 429 and throws a clear error ("asked to wait 49611s (about 14h)… the API key's quota looks used up: check its plan and usage with freehire"), which fails that market in seconds and ends the run with a failing status (the owner is emailed); the closed-job check reports an error for that lookup immediately (so the existing 5% error limit makes it skip itself); the company-website lookup stops for everyone after a long 429. **Needs your attention:** freehire's API docs say the public endpoints are free "for personal and non-commercial use" and that "showing postings in a product you offer to others requires an API plan under the data licence"; please confirm the key this site uses is on such a plan, and what its daily quota is, because the daily sync plus the per-job closed check (about 2,800 requests) may simply exceed it. Added tests for the rule, the closed-check lookup and the company lookup under a 49611 s limit.

- The freehire company-website lookup (added for logos) now has an overall time budget (4 minutes) and logs how many companies it found and how long it took (`[freehire] company websites: N found for M companies in Ks`). When freehire is slow or rate-limiting, it stops looking up further companies and the job ingest goes on without them (those keep their name-based logo), instead of waiting behind a nice-to-have. Found while watching an 8-day backfill run (2026-11-05): the sync step was still going after 34 minutes where the previous 1.5-day run took under 3, and the lookup was the only new code on that path; whether it was the cause is not established (the log of a running job cannot be read), so this is a safeguard, not a confirmed fix. Added a test for the budget.

- Fixed the daily freehire sync getting stuck in the closed-job check. **What happened:** the scheduled run of 2026-11-05 (13:09 UTC) finished the sync in under 3 minutes, then sat in "Expire jobs freehire has closed" for 42 minutes with no output until the 45-minute job timeout cancelled it, so the final "Expire jobs not refreshed in 7 days" step never ran. The cause was not pinned down (the log was empty): the check makes about 2,800 one-by-one requests, each allowed up to 4 attempts with waits of up to 60 s on rate limiting, so a persistent rate limit can add up to hours, and nothing printed progress or enforced an overall limit. **Fixes:** `checkClosedFreehireJobs` takes a `deadlineMs` budget (the script passes 15 minutes): when it runs over, it stops and expires NOTHING (status `skipped`, with a `::warning::`), since anything not looked at is unknown; it also prints progress (phase, every 250 checks: open, errors, reported closed) so a stall shows in the log next time. In `freehire-sync.yml` both closed-check steps have `timeout-minutes: 20`, and the 7-day expiry now runs whenever the sync step itself succeeded (`!cancelled() && steps.sync.conclusion == 'success'`), even if the closed check failed or timed out. The 40% safety limit and the 5% error limit are unchanged. Added tests for the budget (stops, expires nothing even when jobs were reported closed) and the progress lines. **Not verified:** that the hang will not recur; the root cause (most likely sustained 429s from freehire right after the sync's own requests) is a hypothesis, and the next scheduled run's log will show where time goes.

- Company logos: the real root cause, a proper source of company websites, and better name lookups. **Findings:** freehire's job records carry no logo and no company website; its company records (`GET /api/v1/companies/{slug}`) have a website for roughly a third of companies (checked live: Stripe, Datadog, Genomics England and Tenstorrent yes; recruiters such as Paragon Alpha, Mongoose Gray, Saragossa and Michael Page no) and a logo only for some Y Combinator companies (0 of 40 sampled). Free logo services were tested too: Clearbit's logo API no longer answers, UpLead returns 404, Google and DuckDuckGo return only small favicons, so the existing chain stays (logo.dev free plan, then icon.horse, the best of the free ones, then initials); the gap was the missing company address, not the service. **Root cause of the wrong logos** (previous entry): ingestion inferred a company's website from the apply link's host (`inferWebsiteFromUrl`), and for freehire jobs the apply link is a job board or ATS, never the employer; the board list also matched exact hostnames only and lacked WhatJobs and the other aggregators freehire collects from. **Changes:** (1) `lib/scrapers/utils/boardHosts.ts` now lists the major boards and aggregators (WhatJobs, Indeed, Glassdoor, ZipRecruiter, Jooble, Adzuna, Talent.com, Careerjet, Jobrapido, Monster, SimplyHired, Reed, Totaljobs, CV-Library, Workopolis, Job Bank, EURES, MyCareersFuture, Catho, freehire) and matches subdomains (`uk.whatjobs.com`) but never look-alikes; `normalizePublicCompanyWebsite` uses this same list (the separate copy from the previous entry is removed). (2) New `resolveCompanyWebsite` in `lib/companies/upsertFromBoard.ts`: a stated real website wins, freehire jobs never infer a website from the apply link, other sources still do (but not from a board or ATS host). (3) `loadFreehireCompanyWebsites` (`lib/scrapers/freehire.ts`): `ingestFreehireJobs` looks up each distinct company once (3 workers, 150 ms pause, Retry-After on 429, a failed lookup is skipped and never blocks ingestion) and passes its real website into ingest, so those logos come from the company's domain instead of a name guess (`companyRef` added to `BoardJobInput`; `company_slug` mapped). (4) A stored website that is a board or ATS address is replaced by the real one when known, or cleared, the next time that company's jobs are ingested (it was only ever filled when empty). (5) Name lookups use the brand part of the name only (`logoLookupName`): "Paragon Alpha - Hedge Fund Talent Business" matched an unrelated company's blue "H" logo, "Paragon Alpha" returns the right logo (checked for six recruiters: Mongoose Gray, Paragon Alpha, Quant Capital, Oxford Knight and Stanford Black came back right; Saragossa returned a palm-tree logo that I believe is theirs but did not verify). **Limits:** companies freehire has no website for (mostly recruiters) still rely on logo.dev's name search, which is fuzzy and can be wrong for obscure names, and falls back to initials when it has nothing; existing companies are corrected as their jobs are re-synced (the daily sync re-reads the last ~36 hours), the older ones stay until `scripts/repair-board-data.ts` is run or they get a fresh job; the extra lookups add about 600 requests to a daily sync (about a minute). Added `__tests__/scrapers/freehireCompanies.test.ts` and `__tests__/companies/upsertWebsite.test.ts`, extended `website.test.ts`.

- Fixed wrong company logos on jobs that came from job boards. **What was happening** (seen on `/jobs/city/london`): recruiter jobs from WhatJobs (Paragon Alpha, Mongoose Gray, Saragossa…) all showed the same red "?" tile. It was not a generic placeholder: it is WhatJobs' own logo. Those companies are stored with `uk.whatjobs.com`, the job board's address, as their "website"; the logo URL is built from the website domain (`/logo/domain/uk.whatjobs.com`), so the board's logo was displayed as the employer's, and the "Company website" link on those pages pointed at the board. **Fix:** `lib/companies/website.ts` now treats known job boards and aggregators (WhatJobs, Indeed, Glassdoor, ZipRecruiter, Jooble, Adzuna, Talent.com, Careerjet, Jobrapido, Monster, SimplyHired, Reed, Totaljobs, CV-Library, Dice, Workopolis, Job Bank, freehire) as "not a company website" (matching the host and its subdomains only, so look-alike names such as `indeedsoftware.dev` still count). The logo then falls back to the name lookup (`/logo/name/<company>`), and if no logo is found the existing initials tile is shown, so a recruiter with no known site shows its initials instead of someone else's logo; the board link disappears from those pages. This is a display-time fix covering job cards, the job page and company pages. **Not changed:** the stored `Company.website` values in the database are untouched (cleaning them would be a separate, reviewed data step), and I did not find where ingestion first writes the board address, so new jobs from the same source will carry the same value until that is fixed upstream. The blocklist is a fixed list of well-known boards, so a board that is not on it would still be used. Added `__tests__/companies/website.test.ts`.

- Typical-salary card on listing pages, the skill, city and level hubs moved onto the shared design, and a restyled company header. **Card:** `<ListingHeader aside>` takes a white card at the right of the tinted header; `ListingSalaryAside` (async server component, `lib/jobs/listingSalaryStats.ts`) shows the median, the range most roles fall in and listings per salary band, computed from the SAME query input as the list beside it, so the card and the list agree. One currency per page (the country's currency on a country page, USD otherwise, stated in the card), band edges scale with the market's pay floor (1x, 1.5x, 2x, 3x: £75k–£115k–£150k–£225k for the UK, and so on), cached 10 minutes per filter set, and nothing is rendered when there are fewer than 20 listings or the lookup fails (when the card is absent the header takes the full width). On `/jobs`, `/remote`, salary tiers, role pages, country pages and categories. **Hubs:** `/jobs/skills/[skill]`, `/jobs/city/[city]` and `/jobs/level/[level]` (the pages released in batch 1) still used the old Tailwind layout; they now use `ListingPage`/`ListingHeader`/`JobRows` like the rest, with chips linking to related skills, cities in the same country and other levels. These pages also emitted `FAQPage` JSON-LD with no visible FAQ, which Google's structured-data guidelines do not allow; the same questions are now rendered on the page. Metadata, canonicals, robots rules and batch gating are unchanged. **Company page:** the header is the same tinted rounded block (display-font title, white tag pills, larger logo); content and structured data unchanged. **Checked:** screenshots at 1440 and 390 px, axe-core on 9 page types with no violations, `tsc` and 387 tests (new: currency bands, currency choice). **Limits:** the local test database has too few listings to show the card on the hubs, so the first real look at it there is on production; one local dev-server run briefly returned 404 for a country page after a file change (200 on retry, production unaffected), so it was a dev-server artefact, not a routing change.

- Wordmark logo, full-width layout, one design language across the pages, a rebuilt job detail page, and a mobile pass. **Logo:** the "6+" mark is gone; the logo is now the words "six [fig] jobs" in Outfit ExtraBold with "fig" on a lime tile (the tile-carries-part-of-the-name idea comes from 4dayweek.io's logo, which puts the "4" on a red tile). Live text in the header and footer (`components/layout/Wordmark.tsx`, Outfit self-hosted as `app/fonts/Outfit.woff2` via `next/font/local`, preloaded because the logo is above the fold), so no image request. Icon set regenerated from the font's own outlines: `app/icon.svg`, `favicon.ico` (16/32/48), `apple-icon.png`, `public/logo.png` (square tile, used as the Organization `logo` in JSON-LD) and `public/logo.svg` (the outlined wordmark, for sharing); the share image `public/og-image.png` is rebuilt in the light card style. The accessible name of the logo link is now "six fig jobs home" so it contains the visible text. **Naming caveat:** the logo says "six fig jobs" as requested, while page titles, JSON-LD and copy still say "Six Figure Jobs"; changing the site name everywhere is a separate decision. **Full width:** every page container was `max-w-6xl` (1152 px) and the header and footer 1200 px; all are now 1600 px with side padding that grows with the screen (16/24/40 px), and the ticker, listing pages and footer line up with it. **Design language:** the shared listing header (`/jobs`, `/remote`, salary tiers, roles, locations, companies, categories) is now the same rounded, brand-tinted block as the homepage hero, with white pills and an 800-weight display title; the hero side panel scales with the screen. **Job detail page** (`app/job/[slug]`): a tinted hero with the logo, company, title and white fact chips (location, work arrangement, posted, type, level), next to a white pay card with the salary, the Apply button and a comparison with similar roles ("Pays more than N% of M senior software engineer listings in USD", a position bar and the typical figure); the new `lib/jobs/salaryRank.ts` ranks the job's range midpoint against live listings of the same role and currency, needs at least 15 comparable listings, is cached 10 minutes per role and currency, and the card simply omits the comparison when there are too few or the lookup fails. The description sits in a white card, the sidebar keeps a sticky "Ready to apply?" card (facts, save, share, report) and the company card, all with rounded cards to match. The hero Apply button reports as a new `hero` surface in the apply-click counts (`APPLY_SURFACES`). **Mobile:** the homepage filters sit behind a "Filters" toggle on phones (a real checkbox and label: no JavaScript, keyboard operable, hidden fields still submit their defaults), so the first screen shows search, popular links and the first jobs; pill and select touch targets are 44-48 px; the sticky Apply bar on job pages now slides in only after the hero's own Apply button has scrolled out of view, instead of duplicating it on first paint. **Checked:** screenshots at 1920, 1440 and 390 px; axe-core reports no WCAG A/AA violations on 11 page types (it caught, and this change fixes, a hidden-but-focusable checkbox on desktop); a browser test of the mobile toggle (collapsed submit gives the defaults, expanded submit carries the chosen values); `tsc` and the full suite (383 tests). **Not done:** the comparison could not be seen locally (the test database has too few listings of one role), so its first real appearance is on production; listing pages have the new header but not yet a side card with figures; lighthouse scores on the new pages were not re-measured. Added `__tests__/jobs/salaryRank.test.ts`.

- Removed dark mode (the site is now light only) and borrowed the useful parts of 4dayweek.io's UI. **Light only:** `<html>` always carries `class="light"` (`app/layout.tsx`), `ThemeProvider`, the header toggle and the `next-themes` dependency are removed, and the viewport declares `colorScheme: light` and a light `themeColor` so native form controls and scrollbars are light too. The old dark token values still sit in `design-tokens.css`/`globals.css` but are unreachable; deleting them is a separate cleanup. **Dark-only leftovers found and fixed:** an axe-core contrast sweep over 14 page types found `/blog` failing (16 elements) because four public pages (`/blog`, `/blog/[slug]`, `/jobs/no-degree`, `/jobs/visa-sponsorship`) used hard-coded dark-theme colours in inline styles (`#a3a3a3` text, `#111` cards, `#fff` headings, `#1f1f1f` borders); they now use the theme tokens, and the small lime and category pills use darker text so they pass WCAG AA on their tinted backgrounds. After the fixes the same sweep reports no violations on any of them. The owner dashboard under `/ubaid93` was not touched (it has its own inline dark styling and is not public). **Learned from 4dayweek.io** (fetched and screenshotted on 2026-11-05): (1) a "Typical salary" card, the median, the range most roles fall in and the number of listings behind it, with bars for listings per salary band: new `lib/jobs/salarySnapshot.ts` (USD listings only so the figures share one currency, each job counted once at the midpoint of its range, needs at least 20 listings, cached 10 minutes, a failure hides the card and nothing else), shown in the homepage hero panel above the existing per-market pay floors; (2) the hero is now a large rounded block in a pale tint of the brand lime with the white card inside it, so the light page has one colour moment; (3) the header marks the current section with a pill and `aria-current="page"`. **Deliberately not copied:** locked or "Pro early access" listings, sign-up pushes, press logos (we have none), the countdown widget; their own page shows inconsistent counts, ours all come from one filter. Added `__tests__/jobs/salarySnapshot.test.ts` (median, quartiles, band edges, junk values, minimum size). Not done: a typical-salary card on listing pages (`/jobs`, salary tiers, roles); the local screenshots use a tiny test database, so the real numbers will differ.

- Homepage search filters redesigned (`components/home/HeroFilters.tsx`, replacing five native dropdowns in `app/page.tsx`). Work arrangement (Any / Remote / Hybrid / On-site) and minimum salary ($100k+ to $400k+) are now pill toggles built from native radio inputs, so they submit with the form, work without JavaScript and keep keyboard and screen-reader behaviour (arrow keys / space, visible focus ring, grouped in `fieldset`/`legend`). Role, location and remote region have long lists, so they stay native `<select>`s (phone pickers, accessibility) with one consistent style: 44 px height, theme-coloured chevron, brand-lime focus ring. Same field names and values as before, so `/jobs` receives the same query: checked with a browser test (Remote via keyboard, $200k+, United States, keyword "python" gives `/jobs?q=python&country=united-states&remoteRegion=&remoteMode=remote&minSalary=200000`; untouched defaults give `minSalary=100000` with the rest empty, as before). Screenshots checked in dark and light on desktop (1440) and mobile (390). The remote-region filter is still shown when work arrangement is not Remote (unchanged behaviour).

- New logo, favicon set and a more modern header and hero. **Logo:** the old icon was the text "$6F" in Arial and emerald green (`#10B981`, not the site's lime); several hand-drawn concepts (bars, staircase, a stepped 6, coin) were tried and dropped as generic or unreadable. The new mark is "6+" (six figures and up): a real outline of the digit 6 from Bricolage Grotesque ExtraBold, the site's headline typeface, with a "+" in lime, on a lime rounded tile for icons. Readable at 16 px; checked on light and dark browser tab strips. **Files:** `app/icon.svg` (SVG favicon), `app/favicon.ico` (16/32/48), `app/apple-icon.png` (180, full-bleed, iOS rounds it), `public/logo.svg` and `public/logo.png` (512; used as the Organization `logo` in JSON-LD on the homepage, about page and blog), `public/og-image.png` re-rendered with the new mark; the explicit `icons` block in `app/layout.tsx` is removed so Next's file conventions emit the tags (`/favicon.ico`, `/icon.svg`, `/apple-icon.png`). **Header and footer:** new `components/layout/BrandMark.tsx`, the bare "6+" with the 6 in the current text colour and the plus in the brand accent, so it works on both themes with no tile; the wordmark now sits in one span (the old inline-flex `gap: 8px` spread "Six  Figure  Jobs" apart). **Hero:** two columns on desktop (≥1024 px) with a panel of real numbers (role count, company count, markets) and the per-market pay floors, taken from `HIGH_SALARY_THRESHOLDS` so they cannot drift from what the site filters on; hidden on mobile, where the copy says the same. **Colour consistency:** the primary button is flat lime with dark text in both themes (it was a green gradient with a glow), and the emerald accents on the LIVE/LATEST pill and the Feedback button and form submit are now brand lime. **Checked:** screenshots in dark and light on desktop (1440) and mobile (390), the icon tags emitted by the page, `tsc` and the full test suite (373 passed). **Limits:** the Search/Jobs/Remote pages other than the homepage were not redesigned in this change; the five native dropdowns under the search box are unchanged; I cannot judge how original the mark is against existing logos, so run an image search and a trademark check before relying on it commercially. Social platforms and browsers cache favicons and og images, so the old ones can show for a while.

- Added a Jooble-format XML job feed at `/feeds/jooble.xml` for job-aggregator submissions. **Format** (`lib/feeds/joobleFeed.ts`) follows Jooble's "XML feed specifications" PDF: root `<jobs>`, `<job id>` per job, all values in CDATA (a literal `]]>` is split, XML-forbidden control characters removed), required `link`, `name`, `region`, `description`, `pubdate`, plus `salary`, `company`, `updated`, `expire`, `jobtype` when known, dates as DD.MM.YYYY, plain-text descriptions with paragraph and list breaks. **Caveat:** that spec is a 2015 document (Jooble's own copy is gone; the copy used is hosted by Matador Jobs), so confirm it against the current one with xml_support@jooble.com when applying. **Content:** the same jobs the job sitemap advertises (same quality gate and de-duplication), at most 5,000, each linking to its page on this site (not the employer), jobs older than 45 days by publication and update date left out (Jooble ignores them), jobs without a description or region skipped. `updated` is the row's `updatedAt`, which also moves when the daily sync re-reads a job, so it can be newer than the last real edit. The route is request-time, cached 30 minutes in process and an hour at the CDN, and sends `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` so the feed never competes with the pages. It is not in any sitemap or `robots.txt`. Nothing has been submitted anywhere: applying to Jooble (a support ticket or email to xml_support@jooble.com) is an account/terms step for the site owner. Verified locally: the feed parses as XML, 40 of 40 jobs have every required tag, ids unique. Added `__tests__/feeds/joobleFeed.test.ts`.

- Added the IndexNow key file (`public/17f4a894a890464ca452a2b1a610c269.txt`, containing only the key) so changed or new URLs can be submitted to Bing, Yandex, Seznam, Naver and Yep through OpenSEO. Google does not use IndexNow. The key is public by design (it only proves ownership of the host). Only released URLs are submitted: the 17 pages in `sitemap-batch/1.xml` and the main hubs, none from unreleased batches.

- Fixed `sitemap.xml` listing other sitemap indexes. **Found from Search Console** (sitemap shown as "Couldn't fetch", last read 4 Sept 2026, i.e. before the 5 Nov submission, so that status may be stale): `sitemap.xml` is an index and listed `sitemap-jobs.xml` (itself an index pointing at `sitemap-jobs/1`) and `sitemap-company.xml` (an index with no entries at all). The sitemap protocol does not allow an index inside an index, and Google handles that poorly; I could not reproduce a fetch failure (every sitemap URL returned 200 with valid XML for a Googlebot user agent, also via the non-www and http redirects), so this is the one structural defect found, not a confirmed cause of the old error. **Change:** `sitemap.xml` now expands the jobs, company and slices indexes into the urlset files they point to (`/sitemap-jobs/1` directly; a child index with no entries is dropped instead of advertised), so every entry is a urlset. `sitemap-jobs.xml` is unchanged and stays in `robots.txt` for Google Jobs discovery. Verified locally: all four children of the new index return 200 and `<urlset>`. Tests in `sitemapIndexRoute.test.ts` stub the three child routes and assert that no index is listed, children are expanded and an empty one is dropped.

- Batched release of the pSEO pages, with one sitemap file per batch. **Why:** nothing on the site is indexed yet, and the previous change made about 60 new templated URLs indexable and listed at once, which is the pattern that tends to end in "Crawled – currently not indexed" on a new domain. **What:** new `lib/seo/pseoBatches.ts` assigns each pSEO page a batch from its path alone (static, no data lookups): batch 1 = ten top skills (Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, React, AWS, Kubernetes, Docker, Java, SQL, Machine Learning), six cities (New York, San Francisco, London, Toronto, Seattle, Austin), `/jobs/level/senior` and four senior engineering role pages; batch 2 = the remaining skill, city, level and Tier-1 role pages; batch 3 = role × skill / role × city combos for the five top roles over batch 1 skills and cities; batch 4 = all other combos. A page is released only when its batch is: unreleased pages serve `noindex, follow` and are not in any sitemap (`isPseoHubUnlocked(count, path)`, `isPseoRoleUnlocked(count, path)` and the new `isPseoComboIndexable(count, path)` in `lib/seo/pseoUnlock.ts` include the batch check, so the page tag and the sitemap cannot disagree; the 25/10/5 job-count floors still apply on top). **Sitemaps:** each released batch is its own file, `/sitemap-batch/<n>.xml` (new route `app/sitemap-batch/[batch]/route.ts`; an unreleased or unknown batch returns an empty 200 `<urlset>`, a database error also returns an empty file briefly instead of a 500), and `sitemap.xml` lists only released batches, so Search Console reports indexing coverage per batch; the pSEO entries moved out of `sitemap-hubs.xml`, which keeps the pillars, Phase 1 roles, salary guides, remote pages and blog. **Release control (Railway variables, no deploy):** `PSEO_RELEASED_BATCH` (default 1, range 1–4) and/or `PSEO_BATCH_SCHEDULE`, a comma-separated list of dates (batch 2 releases on the first date, batch 3 on the second, …); the further-along of the two wins. Phase 2+ (`INDEXING_PHASE>=2`) is unaffected: combos are ungated and the dedicated sitemap families take over. **Effect on production when this deploys:** only batch 1 (about 25 URLs) stays indexable and listed; the rest of the pages added by the previous entry go back to `noindex, follow` until their batch is released. Tested: unit tests for batch assignment, release control (manual, schedule, clamping, bad dates, phase 2), the per-batch collector (floors, batch split, unreleased batch empty, phase 2, `PSEO_UNLOCK=0`) and the page-level helpers; verified in a local dev server that with batch 1 released only batch-1 pages are `index` and listed in `/sitemap-batch/1.xml`, and releasing batch 3 flips a batch-3 combo page to `index`.

- Removed the last "verified" / "no entry-level clutter" wording from the role-page and salary-tier meta descriptions (`RoleTemplate.tsx`, `SalaryTierTemplate.tsx`): `/jobs/senior-software-engineer` still said "Browse 29 verified … jobs" after the earlier copy cleanup. Description text only; titles, robots and canonicals unchanged.

- SEO: unlocked the substantial programmatic pages, fixed skill counts, and cleaned up page metadata. **What was wrong (live audit of 72 pages on 2026-11-05, plus competitor research in OpenSEO: remote100k-style job boards get their traffic from role, skill/tech, city and salary pages):** skill, city and seniority pages with hundreds of live jobs were `noindex` only because of the blanket Phase 1 policy (`/jobs/skills/python` 279 jobs, `/jobs/skills/aws` 338, `/jobs/city/new-york` 135, `/jobs/level/senior` 708, `/jobs/senior-software-engineer` 29), while one-job role × skill pages were indexable; skill pages counted wrongly (`java` also matched "javascript", `sql` matched "postgresql"; the Node.js, Golang and C++ hubs showed 0 jobs because freehire stores `nodejs`, `go` and `cpp`); `public/og-image.png`, used by every page as its social preview, was a blank black rectangle, and 28 pages that set their own `openGraph` had no `og:image` at all (Next replaces the layout's `openGraph` object instead of merging it); the blog titles were 79–90 characters, about 25 descriptions were over 160, and a few descriptions still said "Verified salaries, direct apply"; `/salary` had no JSON-LD. **Unlock rule** (new `lib/seo/pseoUnlock.ts`, used by both page metadata and the sitemap, so a URL is listed only if the page serves `index, follow`): skill, city and level hubs index from 25 live jobs (`PSEO_HUB_MIN_JOBS`), Tier-1 role pages outside the Phase 1 list (senior/staff variants, SRE, solutions architect…) from 10 (`PSEO_ROLE_MIN_JOBS`), `PSEO_UNLOCK=0` switches the whole unlock off; role × skill pages now need 5 jobs (they were indexable from 1) and role × city keeps 5. `lib/seo/evergreenSitemap.ts` lists the unlocked hubs and role pages and, for the ten Phase 1 roles, role × skill / role × city pages with at least 10 jobs (counted with the same `buildWhere` input as the page; one grouped query counts all roles); in phase 2+ the dedicated sitemap families own these URLs so nothing is listed twice. **Skills:** the filter now matches the exact quoted token in `skillsJson` (case-insensitive) and the hubs map to the spellings freehire stores (`node-js` → `nodejs`, `golang` → `go`, `c-plus-plus` → `cpp`, …; new `skillStoredTokens` in `pseoTargets.ts`); two new hubs, C# and Google Cloud (`csharp`, `gcp`), added because freehire data has them in volume; the level list moved to `LEVEL_TARGETS` so the page and the sitemap share it. Counts on the skill hubs therefore change (Java and SQL go down, Node.js/Go/C++ go up from 0). **Metadata:** a real 1200×630 social image replaces the blank one, `defaultOgImages()` (`lib/seo/site.ts`) is passed to the 26 `openGraph` blocks that lacked images, layout alt text no longer says "verified"; blog posts get a short `metaTitle` (≤ 60 characters), long descriptions are capped at 160 (`capDescription`), "verified"/"direct apply" claims removed from `/remote`, category, visa-sponsorship and no-degree descriptions, `/salary` title shortened and given BreadcrumbList + CollectionPage JSON-LD. **Not changed:** `/pricing` and `/post-a-job` are plain redirects to `/jobs` (the earlier "canonical contradiction" was the audit crawler following the redirect); homepage `<title>` still says "Verified"; company pages stay `noindex`. **Risk to watch:** this widens the indexable set beyond the deliberate Phase 1 policy. Google may leave some of these "Crawled – currently not indexed" on a young site; the job-count floors keep them substantial, and `PSEO_UNLOCK=0` (a Railway variable) reverts it without a deploy. Verified locally against a Postgres copy: with the thresholds lowered to match the small local data, the unlocked skill pages serve `index`, thin ones (Rust) `noindex`, every URL in `sitemap-hubs.xml` serves `index` with a matching canonical, `og:image` and `twitter:image` appear on 8 sampled page types. Production counts and sitemap response time are not yet measured. Added `__tests__/seo/pseoUnlock.test.ts`, `__tests__/jobs/skillFilter.test.ts` and pSEO cases in `__tests__/seo/evergreenSitemap.test.ts`.

- Closed-job check is now live on the daily schedule (`freehire-sync.yml`): the 05:17 UTC run expires freehire jobs that freehire reports closed and that its open search no longer lists, through the normal expiry path (Google indexing removal queue, sitemap revalidation). Manual runs stay report-only unless `closed_apply` is ticked. Safety limits are unchanged (skip if >5% of lookups fail, abort and fail the run if it would expire >40%). A wrongly expired job reappears on the next sync that re-reads it. To turn it back to report-only, make the schedule use the "report only" step.

- Closed-job check, second production dry run with the two-confirmation guard (2026-11-05): 2,752 jobs checked, 0 lookup errors, 2,749 open, 3 reported closed by freehire: 1 kept because freehire's open search still lists it, 2 confirmed (both from one recruiter, "Civil Litigation" roles); nothing expired (dry run). The log now prints each flagged job's freehire API URL so a flagged job can be verified by hand. The workflow stays report-only; enabling expiry (`closed_apply`, or the schedule) is a one-line change once reviewed.

- The closed-job check now needs two confirmations before expiring a job. First production dry run with the per-job check (2026-11-05): 2,833 active jobs, 796 had their freehire slug found by title search and 81 could not be identified (not judged), 2,752 were checked with 0 lookup errors and 0 not-found, 2,751 open and 1 reported closed (versus 442, mostly wrong, from the earlier open-set comparison). That one job was open again, with `closed_at: null` and listed in freehire's open search, minutes later (reopened, or two records sharing an id). So a job is now expired only if freehire reports `closed_at` AND the open search no longer lists the posting; a conflict keeps the job and is reported ("reported closed but still listed (kept)"). Takeaway: closures among the last week's jobs are rare (about 1 in 2,700), so this mostly keeps the list tidy; the 7-day expiry remains the main mechanism. Still report-only in the workflow until a dry run with this guard is checked.

- Replaced the closed-job check with a reliable one. The open-set comparison from the previous two entries is removed (`freehireReconcile.ts`, its script and tests). New `lib/scrapers/freehireClosedCheck.ts` asks freehire about each active freehire job individually (`GET /api/v1/jobs/{public_slug}`) and expires a job only when freehire returns an explicit `closed_at`; a 404 is counted and ignored (freehire documents that closed roles are kept and marked closed), and a failed lookup is never a verdict. To make that possible each job now stores its freehire slug: new nullable column `Job.sourceRef` (migration `20261105150000_add_job_source_ref`, additive, no index needed), carried through `BoardJobInput` / `ScrapedJobInput` and written on create, upgrade and refresh (a later ingest without a slug never clears it). Jobs imported before this have no slug; the check finds it by searching freehire for the title and matching the external id (stores it, which is identifier metadata, so it also happens in dry runs) and simply does not judge any job it cannot identify. Safety: the run is skipped with a warning if more than 5% of lookups fail, aborted if it would expire more than 40% of what it checked, and requests are paced (3 workers, 350 ms) with 429 Retry-After handling. Checked against live freehire: the four jobs the previous approach wrongly flagged are all reported open, and a made-up slug is reported not found. `freehire-sync.yml` runs it after the sync, report-only by default (`npm run check-closed:freehire -- --dry-run`); ticking `closed_apply` makes a manual run expire. Added `__tests__/scrapers/freehireClosedCheck.test.ts` (explicit `closed_at` only, 404 and errors never expire, both safety limits, slug resolution) and a slug case in the freehire mapper tests. Deploy order: the migration must be applied before the code that reads the column.

- **Closed-job check is now report-only (correction to the entry below).** A production dry run on 2026-11-05 flagged 442 of 2,738 judged jobs as closed (16%, under the 40% safety limit), but spot-checking six of them against freehire showed four were still open there (`closed_at` empty, freshness "fresh", salary above threshold, and `mapFreehireJob` accepts them): they were posted 2026-11-02 around 20:30, in the bulk-refresh cluster, and freehire's offset paging over many near-identical `posted_at` values skips rows between pages. The `complete` flag only detects the paging cap and request failures, not skipped rows, so "missing from the fetched set" does not mean closed, and applying it would have wrongly expired roughly two thirds of what it flagged. The daily workflow would have applied it from the next scheduled run, so `freehire-sync.yml` now runs it as `--dry-run` always (step "Closed-job check (report only)"); the input `reconcile_dry_run` is replaced by `reconcile_apply` (default off, documented as unsafe). Nothing was expired by the dry run (`expired: 0`). The code and tests stay; a reliable version needs an authoritative per-job check (freehire's `GET /api/v1/jobs/{public_slug}` returns `closed_at`), which requires storing the freehire slug on each job (small additive migration). The 7-day expiry still removes closed jobs, just later.

- Core Web Vitals baseline and a server-response-time fix. **Baseline** (Lighthouse 12, Lighthouse's standard mobile profile with simulated slow-4G/4x CPU, run from a cloud sandbox against the live site on 2026-11-05; lab data, not field data, so treat absolute numbers as indicative): performance score 91–97, CLS 0.000 on every page, LCP 2.2–2.8 s (Google's "good" is ≤ 2.5 s: `/jobs` 2.8, `/` 2.7, job page 2.7, `/remote` 2.5, `/jobs/400k-plus` 2.2), total blocking time 96–140 ms on listing pages and 256 ms on the job page, 411–562 KB transferred. Not measured: INP (needs real interaction) and field data (the PageSpeed Insights API's shared quota was exhausted and a site this size usually has too little traffic for CrUX). Findings: the LCP element is a text paragraph on all three pages checked, hub pages took 450–515 ms to first byte (`/` 150 ms, job page 254 ms), two render-blocking stylesheets cost ≈ 300 ms on `/`, and main-thread time is dominated by React's own hydration chunk rather than any one component. **Fix:** since hub pages became request-time (so a deploy-time build cannot freeze the "unavailable" fallback), every visit ran the same 5–10 aggregate queries. New `lib/cache/memo.ts` (`memoAsync`: 5-minute in-process cache, concurrent callers share one load, failures are never cached) now wraps the facet/average/top-list queries on `/jobs`, salary tiers, role pages, country pages and `/remote`, the freshness the old `revalidate` of 5–10 minutes gave; the job list itself (count + page) is still queried per request. Measured on a production build against a local database: first load of these pages ran 20–36 queries, the repeat ran 2. The effect on production time-to-first-byte is not yet measured; re-run Lighthouse after deploying. **Tried and dropped:** Next's `experimental.inlineCss` (to remove the render-blocking stylesheets) did not inline anything in this setup (stylesheets stayed linked) and produced no reliable difference, so it was not shipped. Added `__tests__/cache/memo.test.ts`.

- Added apply-click tracking and updated the wording that claimed the opposite. The Apply button (apply card and mobile bar) now reports a click with `navigator.sendBeacon` to the new `POST /api/apply-click`; the link itself is unchanged (still opens the employer's page in a new tab with `rel="nofollow sponsored noopener noreferrer"`, no redirect, no effect on SEO or JobPosting markup). **Privacy:** a row is only `{jobId, surface, device (phone/computer), createdAt}` in the new `ApplyClick` table: no IP address, user agent, cookie or visitor id is stored. Nothing is sent when the browser sends Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control; the server ignores crawlers/headless/script user agents, counts a visitor's repeat clicks on one job within 10 minutes once (hash of IP+user agent held in memory only), and always answers 204, so a failure (including the table not existing yet) never affects the visitor. **Wording:** the apply card said "Six Figure Jobs doesn't track it"; it now says "We only count that the button was clicked (no cookies, nothing about you)", and the privacy policy lists apply clicks under information we collect. **Admin:** new Apply clicks page (`/ubaid93/clicks`: 7/30-day totals, phone vs computer, most-clicked jobs). **Database:** new model `ApplyClick` with migration `20261105120000_add_apply_click` (one table, two indexes, additive). Migrations are not run by the build, so it must be applied once: new manual workflow `db-migrate.yml` reports `prisma migrate status` by default and runs `prisma migrate deploy` only when "apply" is ticked, behind the production database fingerprint guard. Tested locally in a browser: one click stored, a repeat click not, Do Not Track sends nothing, a Googlebot user agent is ignored. Added `__tests__/analytics/applyClick.test.ts`.

- Improved search. **Data:** freehire jobs arrived with no skills at all (production: `?tech=python` returned 0 jobs and `?q=python` only 4), because skills and visa sponsorship were only ever filled by the AI-enrichment step, which is paused. The freehire adapter now passes freehire's own `skills` (cleaned, lowercase, max 12) and `enrichment.visa_sponsorship` through ingestion (`skills` / `visaSponsorship` on `BoardJobInput` and `ScrapedJobInput`) into the existing `skillsJson` and `visaSponsorship` columns, on create, upgrade and refresh; a re-ingest updates skills but never switches an existing `visaSponsorship=true` off or wipes skills when the source sends none. No schema change. Existing jobs get them on the next sync that re-reads them: run the `freehire daily sync` workflow once with `lookback_days = 8` (new input) to backfill. **Keyword search** (`/jobs?q=`, `/search`, `/api/jobs`, all through `queryJobs`): typo correction against a vocabulary of words in active jobs (new `lib/search/`; one-edit for 4–5 letter words, two for longer, first letter must match, words with digits left alone; cached 30 minutes; falls back to plain search if the vocabulary query fails), aliases (frontend/front-end, swe/sde, ml, pm, k8s…), and real relevance ranking for `sort=relevant` (title word > title > company > skills > location, exact words above aliases above corrections; ranks the 1,500 newest matches). A keyword search now defaults to relevance (the "Most Relevant" option previously sorted by salary), the page says "Showing results for X" with a "Search for \"Y\" instead" link (`exact=1` turns correction off). **Filters:** the `/jobs` sidebar gains Technology (most common skills with counts) and "Offers visa sponsorship" (`visa=1`); both are shown only on `/jobs`, where the page applies them. **Bugs fixed:** `/api/jobs`, which powers infinite scroll, ignored the search keyword, so scrolling a search appended unfiltered jobs from page 2 on; the natural-language parser (`/search`) matched country codes and seniority words as substrings, so any query containing "developer" or "designer" was silently filtered to Germany ("de") and "sre" counted as senior ("sr"); it now matches whole words; and `/search` dropped every word of a query once a role was recognised ("pyton developer" ignored "pyton"), it now keeps the leftover words that are real words in our jobs. New query params `visa` and `exact` are allowed through `proxy.ts`. Tests: `__tests__/search/*`, `__tests__/jobs/nlToFilters.test.ts`, skills/visa cases in `__tests__/scrapers/freehire.test.ts`. Limits: relevance ranks only the 1,500 newest matches; typo correction only fixes words that exist in current jobs; no search by description text.

- Added "Report this job" to the job page (small link under the Apply card, opens a dialog). Reasons: closed/dead link, recruiter or agency posting, wrong or missing salary, fake or scam, other (detail required). Reports are posted to the new `POST /api/report-job` and stored in the existing `Feedback` table with types `broken-link`, `recruiter-posting`, `wrong-salary`, `fake-job` or `job-other`, so they appear in the admin Feedback list (new labels added) and are forwarded to `FEEDBACK_WEBHOOK_URL` if set; no schema change or migration. The endpoint, unlike the generic `/api/feedback`, is hardened because it is public: only the fixed reasons are accepted, the job id must exist, a hidden honeypot field makes bots a silent no-op, details are capped at 500 characters, the stored link can only be an on-site `/job/…` path, and an in-memory rate limit allows 10 reports per visitor per hour and one per visitor per job per day (keys are hashed; no IP address is stored). Validation and rate limiting live in `lib/jobs/jobReport.ts` (client-safe) with `__tests__/jobs/jobReport.test.ts`. Limitation: the rate limit is per server process and resets on restart. Nothing acts on reports automatically; they are for review in the admin.

- Added closed-job sync for freehire jobs. Previously a job that closed after we imported it stayed live until it went 7 days without an update (the daily sync only re-reads the newest ~36 hours), leaving dead apply links on the site and in the sitemap. freehire's search returns only open postings and its per-job endpoint needs a slug that we do not store (changing `externalId` would change job ids and duplicate rows), so the new `lib/scrapers/freehireReconcile.ts` re-collects everything freehire still lists for the last 8 days and expires our active freehire jobs posted in the last 7 days that are missing from that set, through the same path as the existing expiry (`expireJobsByIds`: `isExpired`, `validThrough`, Google indexing removal queue, sitemap revalidation). Safeguards: it skips itself with a workflow warning if any market failed or did not reach the end of the window (new `complete` flag on each `FreehireMarketReport`, false on request failure, the 10,000-result paging cap or a page limit), if freehire returns no open jobs, and it aborts (workflow fails, owner is emailed) if it would expire more than 40% of the jobs it judged; jobs older than the judged window or without a posted date are never touched. Runs in `freehire-sync.yml` after the sync and before the 7-day expiry (`npm run reconcile:freehire`; manual runs have a `reconcile_dry_run` checkbox that reports without expiring). Measured against live freehire on 2026-11-05: all five markets complete in ~4.5 minutes, USD fetched 6,181 of the 10,000 cap (≈770 new qualifying jobs/day), 3,113 open mappable jobs. Added `__tests__/scrapers/freehireReconcile.test.ts` and `freehireCollectComplete.test.ts`.

- Fixed the "temporarily unavailable" page that every deploy left on the homepage (and `/jobs`, `/remote`, `/companies`, the salary-tier pages) for several minutes. **Cause (from the Railway build log of 2026-11-05):** the build container cannot reach the database (`Can't reach database server`), so each page without URL params caught the error during `next build`, prerendered its fallback, and served that static HTML until its first revalidation; DB-backed sitemap routes could do the same for 12–24 hours. **Fix:** new `skipPrerenderWhileBuilding()` in `lib/runtime/fallback.ts` (called from `withRuntimeFallback` and the five direct fallback sites) opts a page out of prerendering when its data load fails while `NEXT_PHASE=phase-production-build`; at runtime it does nothing, so a real outage still shows the fallback. The 13 database-backed sitemap routes are now request-time (`dynamic = 'force-dynamic'`) with a shared `Cache-Control: s-maxage=3600, stale-while-revalidate=86400` set in `buildSitemapMetaHeaders`, and `/ubaid93/setup` (admin first-time-setup form that depends on live database state) is request-time too, which also stops a database-less build failing on it. **Verified locally:** a production build with an unreachable database lists `/`, `/jobs`, `/companies`, `/remote`, the salary tiers and all DB-backed sitemaps as request-time; started against a reachable database, their first request returns live content with no fallback text; started against an unreachable database, the pages still render the fallback with HTTP 200. **Trade-off:** these pages are now rendered per request instead of served from the ISR cache (they previously had `revalidate` of 5–10 minutes); each hub render runs a handful of database queries. Static output for pages that do not depend on the database is unchanged.

- Added the "Logos provided by Logo.dev" link (to https://logo.dev) to the footer, as required by logo.dev's free plan.

- Rebuilt `sitemap.xml` for Search Console submission. **What was wrong (checked against the live site on 2026-11-05):** pages that serve `index, follow` were missing from every sitemap (`/jobs/software-engineer`, `/jobs/data-engineer`, `/jobs/product-manager`, the `/salary/<role>` guides, `/remote/<role>` pages and `/blog` with its six posts), because the Phase 1 policy silences the families that carry them; `sitemap.xml` stamped every child with the request time as `lastmod` (Google stops trusting `lastmod` when everything always says "now"); and `robots.txt` listed `sitemap-company.xml` and `sitemap-salary.xml` against the CLAUDE.md rule. **What changed:** `sitemap-hubs.xml` now lists the five pillar hubs plus the evergreen pages that are indexable in the current phase, produced by the new `lib/seo/evergreenSitemap.ts`, which applies each page's own `generateMetadata` indexability rule (`/jobs/<role>`: Tier-1 role, ≥3 live jobs, Phase 1 allowlist; `/salary/<role>`: ≥1 live $100k+ posting; `/remote/<role>`: Tier-1, ≥5 remote jobs, Phase 1 allowlist), limited to the ten Phase 1 roles, so a URL is advertised only if the page itself is indexable. Each entry's `lastmod` is the newest job update behind that page (blog: the post's `updatedAt`), and `sitemap.xml` children get the newest job update instead of "now"; when the database is unreachable `lastmod` is omitted rather than invented, and an incomplete result is never cached. Both routes now render at request time (`force-dynamic`, 1-hour in-process cache, `Cache-Control: s-maxage=3600`) because a deploy-time prerender cannot reach the database and would freeze a pillars-only file. `robots.txt` now declares only `sitemap.xml` and `sitemap-jobs.xml`. **Unchanged:** the Phase 1 policy itself (country, city, category, level, company and visa pages stay `noindex` and out of the sitemap), the job sitemap (`/sitemap-jobs/1`, freshness + quality gate + dedupe), the salary-tier sitemap, canonical URLs and robots meta. Validated: 60 randomly sampled live job URLs all return 200, `index`, a canonical identical to the sitemap URL, and JobPosting JSON-LD; all 24 hub/evergreen URLs checked locally the same way. Added `__tests__/seo/evergreenSitemap.test.ts` and `hubsSitemapRoute.test.ts`; updated `robotsRoute.test.ts`.

- Redesigned every listing page, added a light theme, and switched to self-hosted fonts. **Listing pages** (salary tiers `/jobs/100k-plus…400k-plus`, role pages, country pages, `/jobs`, `/remote`, `/companies`, company pages, category pages, and all pages that render `JobList`): the gradient hero panels with emoji badges, four stat tiles, charts, pie and trend lines, and "Active filters: 0"-style tiles are replaced by one flat layout (new `components/listing/ListingParts.tsx` + `Listing.module.css`): visible breadcrumb, H1, a single summary line (count · average salary · updated), sort control, sticky filters on the left and compact job rows (the homepage row design) on the right, then plain internal-link sections (cities, companies with logos, skills, levels), short copy and a visible FAQ. The Grid/List toggle is gone (rows only). `/jobs` previously emitted `FAQPage` JSON-LD with no visible FAQ; the FAQ is now on the page. The internal "Priority company pages… worth crawling" block on `/companies` and the "Why this category is built for Google" block (with a "100kk+" label bug) on category pages are removed. Salary-page building blocks (`PageHero/PageSection/PageStatGrid`) are restyled flat. Removed ~12 stat/chart database queries per page render (salary tier: 8 → 1; role page: 7 → 2) and the unused `RemoteHero`, `RoleTemplate`, `SalaryTierTemplate`, `CountryLocationTemplate`, `JobsPage` and `RemotePage` CSS. **SEO:** canonical URLs, hreflang, robots/noindex rules, breadcrumb/ItemList/CollectionPage JSON-LD, H1 text, internal links and pagination links are unchanged; page `<title>` strings are unchanged except where noted below. **Copy accuracy:** visible text, meta descriptions, FAQ answers and JSON-LD descriptions no longer claim "verified" salaries, "ATS feeds", "apply direct, no recruiters" or "no recruiter middleman" (the data includes recruiter postings and comes from company career pages and job boards via freehire); the social preview images ("Direct apply · Verified salary · No recruiter spam") and the header pill ("$100k+ verified only" → "$100k+ salaries shown") are reworded, and internal notes that were shown to visitors ("thin pages stay noindex until…") are removed. The homepage `<title>` still says "Verified $100k+…" and was deliberately not changed here (SERP impact). **Light theme:** new header toggle (`ThemeToggle`, next-themes, default stays dark, choice is remembered). Tokens live in `src/styles/design-tokens.css` (`html.light`) and `app/globals.css`; ~250 hard-coded colours in CSS modules were mapped to tokens and ~35 TSX files from Tailwind palette classes (`text-neutral-300`, `bg-neutral-900`…) to theme classes (`text-muted-foreground`, `bg-card`…). Brand lime is not readable as text on white, so accent text uses a new `--color-primary-text` (`#4d7c0f` in light, ~5.4:1); `--primary-foreground` is now dark in the dark theme (it was white on lime). **Fonts:** Bricolage Grotesque (headings), Instrument Sans (body) and JetBrains Mono (salaries) as self-hosted variable woff2 via `next/font/local` (`app/fonts/`, Latin subset, size-adjusted fallbacks, no third-party request); previously the CSS named Inter/Cal Sans/JetBrains Mono but never loaded them. **Accessibility:** axe-core reports 0 violations on 14 pages × light/dark × desktop/mobile (local data); fixed `role="list"` containing buttons in the filter panel, the filter-sheet trigger's ARIA attributes, and the stat strip's `<dl>`. Known gaps: Tailwind `amber/red/rose` status colours and a few gradients were left as they are; other minor pages not in the audit may still have dark-only styling; some logos are white-on-transparent and show as blank tiles on the white logo container (e.g. Anduril).

- Added a free fallback for company logos. `/logo/[kind]/[value]` now asks logo.dev first (strict mode, `fallback=404`, so a missing logo is a real 404 instead of a generated placeholder) and, for domain lookups only, falls back to icon.horse (`https://icon.horse/icon/<domain>`, which needs no key). If neither has an image the route returns 404 and `CompanyLogo` shows the coloured initials tile. Name-only lookups have no free equivalent and still use logo.dev only. Each probe has a 3-second timeout, the resolved source is cached in memory for 24 hours (max 5,000 entries), and the redirect is cacheable by the CDN for 7 days. No new dependency. This reduces reliance on logo.dev but does not remove it; logo.dev's free plan asks for a visible "Logos provided by Logo.dev" attribution link, which is not in the site yet. Extended `__tests__/companies/logo.test.ts` to 18 tests (fallback chain with mocked fetch, validation, caching).

- New homepage headline: "Six-figure jobs. No salary guessing." (two lines, second in the accent colour), with the markets (US · UK · EU · Canada · Australia) as the eyebrow and a subheading that no longer repeats the promise. Company names are now passed to the logo builder at every call site (job cards, job page, company page, company directory, role pages, homepage carousel), so companies with neither a stored logo nor a website can get a logo.dev by-name logo.
